# Findings 0501 to 0750

246 findings: 186 confirmed, 32 unconfirmed, 28 refuted. [How the register works](README.md).

<a id="f501"></a>
### #501 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003bf650 — beModelCtrlInfoWork_Construct

Game-class constructors are recoverable systematically from the engine's constructor shape: a function that calls igObject_InitHeader (0x2fef20) and stamps a class vtable at +0, then zeroes the class's reflected fields. The stamped vtable (the last one, i.e. the most-derived class) is in data/vtables.json, so the class half of the name is recovered, not guessed; the role is the body. scripts/seed_names_constructors.py scans every unmatched FUN_ for this shape and names it \<Class>_Construct. Applied: beNDMWStatusCtrlBaseEquip_Construct (0x3ba610; vtable 0x53f150), beModelCtrlInfoWork_Construct (0x3bf650; vtable 0x537ee0), beNDMWMdlPEBaseInfoWork_Construct (0x3c0120; vtable 0x540520). config/SLUS-20836/names/Constructors.tsv, applied by tools/apply_names.py; make check 8971/19277 0 mismatching and make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. Only 4 constructors in the whole binary call 0x2fef20 and stamp an RTTI-known vtable, so the family is small; the rest of the game's constructors (e.g. FUN_003ba610's siblings) stamp vtables that are not RTTI records.

**Notes:** The generator is scripts/seed_names_constructors.py (--out config/SLUS-20836/names/Constructors.tsv). It tracks lui/addiu/ori/move/addu per function to resolve the vtable address, then takes the last vtable stamped at +0 as the most-derived class. Middleware (ig* Alchemy) is filtered out per finding #488. make typed-check stays green (baseline 1908).

**Evidence:** config/SLUS-20836/names/Constructors.tsv

<a id="f502"></a>
### #502 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002595e0 — FUN_002595e0

The engine boot chain is mapped. The CRT entry 0x100008 clears BSS, sets gp=0x55c870, SetupHeap(0x58d280,-1) (syscall 61), calls the EE runtime init 0x1141c8, FlushCache 0x10b160, __init_user/static ctors 0x2d1640 (thunk -> FUN_001029d0), then calls the game's main FUN_002595e0 (0x2595e0, 976 B) with argc/argv, then exit 0x2d1650 (thunk -> FUN_00100bc0, the atexit runner) and the C++ dtor runner FUN_00124d60 (walks the list at 0x4854a0). FUN_002595e0 builds the app object (0x234900/0x234d70), creates the three Insight managers (0x1a1ae0, 0x1a7cc0, 0x1a67c0), builds the frame object *(0x57dc50) via 0x1007e0/0x238110, increments its +0xe8 frame counter, and enters the frame function FUN_0025a580 (0x25a580, 832 B, non-windowed) or FUN_0025a8c0 (0x25a8c0, 928 B, drawing path calling 0x463b30/0x463c70/0x4578c0 in the renderer). This is the target list for a native main: the ported host (DW4_Port include/dw4/engine.h) runs the same init->object-graph->frame->present shape, and igObject_InitHeader 0x2fef20 is the first engine function ported (all 4-byte offsets).

**Notes:** Static decode only; the functions are not decompiled. Doc: docs/engine/engine-boot.md. The port host (port/src/engine.c, src/main.c, tools/verify_host.py in make check) boots, constructs 38135 objects over 192 classes from stag0102, runs the ported igObject_InitHeader, and renders frames. Next port order: igObject_InitHeader (done), the manager/app constructors 0x234900/0x234d70/0x1a1ae0/0x1a7cc0/0x1a67c0, then 0x25a580/0x25a8c0, then FUN_002595e0.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/engine-boot.md

<a id="f503"></a>
### #503 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001a1ae0 — igEnbayaContextPool_Create / igAnimationCombinerBoneInfoPool_Create / igAnimationStatePool_Create

The game's main (FUN_002595e0) constructs three Insight managers, one per class, through per-class _Create factories, and those factories are now named. Each _Create is: igMetaObject_RunStaticInit(\<Class>_Register) then igMetaObject_CreateInstance(\<Class> MetaObject, owner). The class is recovered from BOTH ends and they agree: the 12-byte thunk tail-calls the class's _Register (0x1a1ca0 -> igEnbayaContextPool_Register 0x1a1cb0, 0x1a7d90 -> igAnimationCombinerBoneInfoPool_Register 0x1a7da0, 0x1a6890 -> igAnimationStatePool_Register 0x1a68a0), and the MetaObject global passed to CreateInstance is the one that class's own GetMetaObject accessor lazily fills (0x1a1b20 reads/writes 0x561b60 = igEnbayaContextPool; 0x1a7d00 reads/writes 0x562250 = igAnimationCombinerBoneInfoPool; 0x1a6800 reads/writes 0x5620d8 = igAnimationStatePool). So 0x1a1ae0/0x1a7cc0/0x1a67c0 = igEnbayaContextPool_Create / igAnimationCombinerBoneInfoPool_Create / igAnimationStatePool_Create, and 0x1a1ca0/0x1a7d90/0x1a6890 = the matching _RunStaticInit thunks. All six were already matched in src/unmatched; naming them moves 6 units to src/engine/ with 0 changes to the bytes (make check 8972/19277 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL). main stores the results at 0x562718, 0x562528 and 0x562540 respectively (sw v0,0x2718/0x2528/0x2540(at) after each jal).

**Notes:** The 0x2f8a20 call between them is the engine core acquire (returns FUN_002f88e0(2), else FUN_002f88e0(0)); main releases the previous manager first (refcount at +4, release 0x2ff820 on zero). The port (port/src/engine.c) now constructs these three as reflection-typed instances, which is igMetaObject_CreateInstance at the semantic level: allocate the class's reflected size, run igObject_InitHeader, stamp the vtable.

**Evidence:** config/SLUS-20836/names/Managers.tsv

<a id="f504"></a>
### #504 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002d16a0 — FUN_002d16a0

FUN_002d16a0 is the engine core init/acquire called from the game's main, and it is now decompiled and byte-matched. It is refcounted on a 64-bit global (0x586dd8): the MSVC-style 'if (counter++ == 0)' idiom (ld v1,counter; daddiu v0,v1,1; bnez v1,END; sd v0,counter in the delay slot), and on the zero path it sets 0x4fb410=0 and 0x4fb418=1, calls 0x32d1f0, allocates a 928-byte (0x3a0) object via 0x2d3540 (thunk_FUN_00126308), runs 0x2d1930 on it, stores it at 0x586dc8, then calls the init chain 0x2b9620/0x2b9470/0x2c4d80/0x2bb200/0x2bad40 and 0x2d1690/0x2d1e40. Every path then calls 0x2d18d0(*(0x586dc8), arg0).

**Notes:** MATCHING NOTE: m2c first emitted 'DAT += 1; if (DAT == 0)' (5 diffs, 3 register diffs); the post-increment spelling 'if (DAT_00586dd8[0]++ == 0)' matches exactly, including the load/compute/branch register choice (v1/v0) and the delay-slot store. So the original idiom was the post-increment, not a pre-incremented test.

**Evidence:** src/unmatched/FUN_002d16a0.c

<a id="f505"></a>
### #505 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x00259a20 — FUN_00259a20 / FUN_00259cd0 / FUN_00259d10

Three small boot helpers called directly by FUN_002595e0 -- 0x259a20 (52 B, 'if (v = 0x259a10) 0x2d18c0(v)'), 0x259cd0 (60 B, '0x32d4b0(&local, argv0); GPR_00554bec = local == DAT_004fb410[0]') and 0x259d10 (44 B, 'if (GPR_00554bec) 0x259b60') -- are each exactly 4 bytes SHORT of retail under every C spelling tried. Retail has an extra nop *after* the jal and *before* the join label (e.g. 0x259d10: beqz/nop, jal/nop, nop, ld ra...). The 60-byte one also showed a register-only residual after the best spelling (retail keeps the local's address in v1 and the result in v0; m2c uses a0). NEGATIVES so the shape is not guessed: source restructurings that do NOT produce the nop -- empty else, trailing return inside/after the if, nested if, goto to a trailing label, 'if (0) {}', do/break, an empty block/statement, and compiling the same body as C++ (extern "C"). permuter is the remaining route for the register residual.

**Notes:** These are candidate blockers for a runnable main: they are the first three calls in FUN_002595e0. The extra-nop pattern is a codegen residual, not a missing statement: the function's own size covers it (0x259d10 symbol size 0x2c = 44). permuter_one.py is NOT usable on these: for FUN_002ff820 (2 diffs, a parameter-spill delay-slot choice) its target assembly failed -- decomp-permuter's assembler rejects the EE MMI opcode ('opcode not supported on this processor: mips3'), so a function containing lq/sq cannot be turned into a permuter project as the harness stands.

**Evidence:** asm/FUN_00259d10.s

<a id="f506"></a>
### #506 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0025a580 — FUN_0025a580

FUN_0025a580's per-frame field setup is recovered from the disassembly (0x25a5e0..0x25a62c). The frame object is *(0x57dc50), 0xf0 bytes, lazily created by FUN_00238110(FUN_001007e0(0xF0)); the function first requires arg0+24 != 0, stores arg0 at *(0x57df80), then writes: +0xe8 (232) += 1 (the frame counter), byte +0xb4 = 0, byte +0xd5 = 0, byte +5 = 0, float +0x94 = +0x98 = +0x9c = 0.1f (0x3DCCCCCD), float +0xa0 = 1.0f (0x3F800000), byte +0x5c = 0, byte +0x5e = 1, byte +0xa8 = 1. It then reloads *(0x57dc50), decrements +0xe8 and, when it reaches 0, runs the object's destruct sequence (release the +0xec and +0 refs via FUN_0030c940 and free through FUN_00100610), i.e. the frame context is one-shot: acquire, run, release.

**Notes:** The port now writes the same fields each tick (port/src/engine.c frame_tick) and verify_host.py pins +0xe8 == frames, +0x94 == 0.10, +0xa0 == 1.00, +0x5e == 1, +0xa8 == 1. Note the doc's earlier shorthand said '+0x5e = 1' and '+0xa8 = 1' -- confirmed; and the frame counter at +0xe8 is written with a 4-byte lw/addiu/sw. Folded into #512, the current answer for FUN_0025a580 frame context field setup; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** asm/FUN_0025a580.s

<a id="f507"></a>
### #507 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x002101a0 — FUN_002101a0 / FUN_00259a20 / FUN_00259cd0 / FUN_00259d10

A 475-function codegen family is blocked by one extra nop, and it is the same class of artifact as the guard 76-byte variant (#313): retail contains 'jal X; nop; nop' at a join, i.e. a nop immediately before the label a preceding branch jumps to, and no natural C reproduces it. Measured over the whole image: the motif is present in exactly 475 functions, and NONE of them has a src/ unit (checked against tools/units.py, not filenames). Distribution: 456 at 0x2e0000+ (Intrinsic Alchemy, out of port scope per #488), 14 in 0x210000-0x260000 (includes the boot helpers FUN_00259a20/FUN_00259cd0/FUN_00259d10), 4 in the SDK, 1 in 0x20d000-0x210000. Minimal example FUN_002101a0 (0x2101a0, 80 B): 'lw v0,44(a0); andi v0,0x40; bnez v0,END; move s0,a1; jal 0x21ed40; nop; nop; END:...' -- i.e. 'if (!(a0->f44 & 0x40)) FUN_0021ed40(a0); FUN_00212280(a0,a1);' and it compiles to 76 B, one nop short. ~40 hand-written forms were tried and ALL give 76 B: plain if, empty else, ternary, ||/&&, goto forward/backward, nested/two labels, switch, do/while(0), while/for break, comma, block scope, early/trailing return, volatile dummy, local-flag, double negation, explicit empty statement/block, and the dead-block nop mechanism the guard permuter found ('if ((!g)&&(!g)) { g++ }' -- folded away by -O3). C++ mode (with proper prototypes) gives the same 76 B. No -O level or -O3,s/-O3,p scheduling flag changes it. The ONLY reproduction found is an inline asm: 'asm("nop")' / '__asm__("nop")' / 'asm volatile("nop")' all verify byte-identical; __nop/_nop/__builtin_nop do not (they emit a call).

**Notes:** Not banked: an artificial asm would be a reward hack (the guard doc's term), not recovered source, so the family stays open like guard variant B. permuter_one.py cannot search it either -- decomp-permuter's assembler rejects the EE MMI lq/sq ('opcode not supported on this processor: mips3'), so any function containing them cannot become a permuter project. The likely explanation matches guard B's: a front-end emission the -O3 scheduler normally removes, inhibited per-file; a file/codegen state we cannot yet express in one function's C.

**Evidence:** asm/FUN_00259d10.s

<a id="f508"></a>
### #508 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x00234d70 — FUN_00234d70

FUN_00234d70, the app object's argv constructor called from the game's main (0x259684: 0x234900(&app), 0x234d70(&app, argc, argv)), is reconstructed and down to 19 differing words (from m2c's 87). Logic: pick a global from GPR_00554c14 (else-branch uses 0x587028, if-branch 0x587060), FUN_002f88e0 it, FUN_002b90e0 the result into g; then for i in [0,argc): arg = argv[i]; if arg, lazily build *(0x586f20) = FUN_002eadf0(FUN_002eb010(16)) and v = FUN_002eaf60(*(0x586f20), arg), else v = 0; store v in a stack slot, FUN_001ca620(g, &slot), and release the slot's object (refcount at -4, handle at -8, FUN_0030c940 at zero); then FUN_00235370(app, g) and release g (refcount at +4, FUN_002ff820 at zero).

**Notes:** Reconstruction (logic certain): pick a global from GPR_00554c14 (else 0x587028, if 0x587060), FUN_002f88e0 it, FUN_002b90e0 the result into g; for i in [0,argc): arg = argv[i]; if arg, lazily build *(0x586f20) = FUN_002eadf0(FUN_002eb010(16)) and v = FUN_002eaf60(*(0x586f20), arg) else v = 0; store v in a stack slot, FUN_001ca620(g, &slot), release the slot (refcount at -4, handle at -8, FUN_0030c940 at zero); then FUN_00235370(app, g) and release g (refcount at +4, FUN_002ff820 at zero). m2c got 87 diffs. MATCHING INSIGHT (reusable): retail spills g to 108(sp) and reloads it before every use; declaring it 'void *g[1]' (loaded per use) reproduces exactly retail's register allocation for the LOOP -- the first 34 words become byte-identical and the function drops from six saved registers to five. But the insight does NOT close the function: with the correct tail argument 'FUN_002ff820(g[0])' the allocator rotates again (six saved registers, ~81 diffs). Retail keeps g[0] in a0 from the post-FUN_00235370 null test through the two refcount loads to the final call; every spelling that passes the object (a repeated g[0], a 'void *p = g[0]' local at function or block scope, an int* local, or reusing an existing temp) makes MWCC use a callee-saved register or reload instead. A 'FUN_002ff820(g)' variant scores 19 diffs but is WRONG -- with g an array that passes &g[0] (sp+108), not the object -- so it is not a near-match and was discarded. The 64-bit refcount idiom must be '((unsigned long long)((long long)v << 0x29) >> 0x29)' (dsll32/dsrl32), not 32-bit sll/srl. Boundary: the tail's g[0]-in-a0 CSE/live-range, same class as the guard family's allocator residuals. Best candidate: FUN_00234d70.best.c (uncommitted).

**Evidence:** asm/FUN_00234d70.s

<a id="f509"></a>
### #509 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002d1770 — FUN_002d1770 / FUN_00234b90

The reference-release idiom in the object graph compiles ONLY from 'if (&obj->field) { release(obj->field); }' with a REAL STRUCT at the recovered offsets -- not from a value test ('if (obj->field)'), not from a byte-offset expression with a char* base ('(char*)obj + N'), and not from pointer arithmetic on a void** (which scales by 4). Retail emits a redundant 'addiu vX,base,OFF; beqz vX' on the field's ADDRESS before the value test, plus a 64-bit refcount mask 'dsll32 vX,vX,9; dsrl32 vX,vX,9'. Twenty-two functions are byte-matched with it: FUN_002d1770 (core shutdown), FUN_00234b90 (app destructor), FUN_00234ca0 (frame release), ten 136-152 B per-class destructors (FUN_00131bc0, FUN_001524c0, FUN_00194490, FUN_00230f70, FUN_002cf040, FUN_0044c110, FUN_00131b20, FUN_0014f4e0, FUN_00231030, FUN_002bf5e0), and nine multi-release/multi-stamp 192-256 B teardowns (FUN_0019c0e0, FUN_0019d120, FUN_00225790, FUN_0044be30, FUN_001a8620, FUN_00150490, FUN_001962d0, FUN_00152b10, FUN_002c0790).

**Notes:** The struct is a shape, not a recovered layout: 'void *fNNN' at the exact offset is enough (the release path reads only the pointer and its -4/+4 refcount); a byte field the code really accesses (FUN_002d1770's +21) must be a member too. Reusable templates: two vtable stamps = base ctor then derived; the field-address test is unconditional (the compiler cannot fold '&x'); a refcount release reads +4 and a handle release reads -4 with the pointer at -8; the tail is 'if (arg1 > 0) \<thunk>(arg0); return arg0;' with arg1 a 16-bit signed value. NEAR-MISSES with a measured boundary: FUN_00335700 / FUN_00344030 (5 diffs), FUN_00347150 / FUN_00361a20 / FUN_003bf2f0 (8), FUN_0033e920 (17), FUN_002f2670 (15, a C++ vtable-call spelling) and the 232-264 B four-vtable teardowns (FUN_0033f5e0 48, FUN_0014c580 46, FUN_003bf9d0 50). For the 5-8-diff ones the residual is the scheduling of arg1's 16-bit sign extension: retail computes 'dsll32/dsra32 v0,s0,0x10' at the join just before the 'blez' and sets the return register after the call; ours hoists the dsll32 into the preceding branch's delay slot and sets v0 before the call. No clean source form fixes it -- a plain 'int a; a = arg1; if (a > 0)' still leaves 5 diffs, and mid-block declarations are rejected by MWCC's C mode (an earlier 'implicit int' reading of 0 diffs was a COMPILE FAILURE misread by a buggy helper, now corrected). Left open rather than baked with a hack.

**Evidence:** src/unmatched/FUN_00150490.c

<a id="f510"></a>
### #510 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00464d80 — dw4asset scene --cam / dw4asset render --camera / dw4 engine --camera

The stage framing is now a real control, and the earlier 'identity camera' reading was the tool listing every igCamera, not a broken composition. The composition (dw4_scene_camera/dw4_scene_node_camera) was already correct: for stag0102 node 4 cameraShape00 composes child x parent to (0, 102.490, 74.565) -- finding #495's measured position -- and node 8 gives the same. What misled the handoff was that 'dw4asset scene --cam' printed all 29 igCamera nodes in node order and the last (node 98 cameraanimShape02, a graph-path root) has no ancestors, so its world/view is the identity. The stage camera is selected as the first igCamera in node order whose composed world is not the identity (node 4). 'dw4asset render --camera [NAME]' and 'dw4 --camera [NAME]' now derive the view from the pack: view = the camera world's affine inverse, fov = 0.6912 rad (39.60 deg, finding #496), aspect = 0.7 * width / height (the measured m[0]/m[5] = 448/640). With --camera on the live placements the whole stage renders legibly (frame filled, 50% coverage of non-background interior). NEGATIVE confirmed: feeding the raw identity world as --view is what produced the smear; the renderer was never wrong. '--span N' is a clip of half-size N around --cam (default: the drawn set's own centre), not a framing control.

**Notes:** port/src/scene.c dw4_scene_camera/dw4_scene_node_camera, include/dw4/scene.h; dw4asset.c cmd_scene --cam/--cam-node, cmd_render --camera/--fit; engine.c dw4_engine_run --camera; tools/verify_render.py pins the stage camera position and (with --scene) a deterministic framed render.

**Evidence:** port/src/scene.c; port/tools/verify_render.py

<a id="f511"></a>
### #511 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x20e33000 — dw4_engine_run merge_scene / dw4_render_mesh_n

The host dw4 frame placed only a fifth of the stage because merge_scene collapsed one instance per mesh name (first capture row wins) instead of one instance per capture row. A stage places the same model many times, and each capture row is a placement (#484/#487), so the merge must iterate the rows and append every matching mesh. Fixed: dw4 now merges 195,868 vertices / 130,194 triangles, exactly matching dw4asset render --scene (was 34,307 / 22,599, ~5.7x short). Separately, dw4_render_mesh_n's smooth path shaded meshes that carry no normal buffer (539 of 549 stage meshes ship none, #458) with zero interpolated normals, i.e. ambient-only/black, which read as a background-coloured frame; when the interpolated normal is degenerate it now falls back to the face normal.

**Notes:** port/src/engine.c append_mesh/merge_scene; port/src/render.c dw4_render_mesh_n. Verified: both paths report 195868 vertices / 130194 triangles; port make check green (verify_host coverage changed, frames still differ).

**Evidence:** port/src/engine.c; port/src/render.c

<a id="f512"></a>
### #512 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x0025a580 — FUN_0025a580 / FUN_0025a8c0 / FUN_00234ca0

FUN_0025a580 frames a one-shot frame context and its release block IS the finding #509 idiom (FUN_00234ca0 is the same release, already byte-matched). Sequence: gate on arg0+24 != 0; store arg0 at *(0x56df80); lazily create *(0x56dc50) = FUN_00238110(FUN_001007e0(0xF0)) when NULL; write +0xe8 = 1 and the per-frame fields (+5/+0x5c/+0xb4/+0xd5 = 0 byte stores, +0x94/+0x98/+0x9c = 0.1f, +0xa0 = 1.0f, +0x5e/+0xa8 = 1); then decrement +0xe8 and, at <= 0, release the +0xec and +0 refs (refcount at -4, handle at -8, FUN_0030c940) and FUN_00100610 the object, clearing *(0x56dc50) -- so the context is acquire/run/release every tick, NOT a persistent counter (the port used to keep it alive and report +0xe8 = frames; it is one-shot). Then the subsystem update sequence 0x239390, 0x238d40, 0x3343a0, 0x33bf50, 0x3719c0, 0x3b04e0, 0x463b30(app), 0x259f80(app), 0x463c70(app), a list build (0x236f80 + 0x2eaf60 on *(0x586f20)), and the present loop calling vtable +0x98 and +0x2c4 then polling app+0x2c / s0+0x10 / s0+0x40 / s0+0x3c with FUN_00259d40/0x463bc0/0x463bb0/0x25afe0/0x463b70. **Draft state:** an m2c draft compiles, reuses #509's real-struct release spelling, and fixes the 7 flag bytes to byte stores (m2c wrote sw/swr/swl); residual 207 differing words, size 844 vs retail 832, dominated by callee-saved allocation (retail spills s0/s1/s2, the draft s0-s5) -- the next iteration is reducing live temps, not structure.

**Notes:** build/m2c/FUN_0025a580.c (not banked). Port side: engine.c frame_tick now models the one-shot acquire/release and the app +0x18 gate; tools/verify_host.py pins +0xe8 peak 1 and the field values. FINDING #506's byte/float offsets confirmed; the earlier reading of +0xe8 as a persistent frame counter is corrected to a one-shot context refcount. Topic head for FUN_0025a580 frame context field setup: also read #506 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** port/src/engine.c

<a id="f513"></a>
### #513 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00463b30 — port/src/window.c / dw4 --window

The host's present seam is now a real backend: SDL2 2.30 was installed (pkg-config + libsdl2-dev; the container had neither, nor CMake) and DW4_Port's 'dw4 --window [--headless]' presents each frame through src/window.c, with --headless selecting the SDL dummy driver so the full texture-upload/present path runs with no display. The Makefile picks SDL2 up via pkg-config and compiles clean no-ops without it, and verify_host.py exercises the headless path (it skips when SDL2 is absent), so the default build needs nothing extra. NEGATIVE for the GPU half: no OpenGL/EGL/GL headers or drivers are installed and there is no GPU, so the VU1 families (LIGHT/TRANS/CLIP/COMPRESS/MULTIPASS, findings #489/#494) are NOT implemented on a GPU; the shading still runs in the software rasteriser (render.c). The window seam is where that GPU pipeline would present, but the next renderer work is the family effects, not the window.

**Notes:** port/Makefile (SDL2 via pkg-config), include/dw4/window.h, src/window.c, engine.c --window/--headless, tools/verify_host.py. Verified: make check green with host window 'SDL2 headless present ok'.

**Evidence:** port/src/window.c

<a id="f514"></a>
### #514 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00463b30 — port/src/gl_render.c / dw4 --gl

The host renderer now has a real GPU path. dw4 --gl draws the merged stage with EGL + GLES 3 into an offscreen FBO (src/gl_render.c), the game's shading model in a fragment shader (ambient + two-sided N.L diffuse, positional d/d^2 or linear attenuation, spotlight cone, Blinn-Phong specular -- the LIGHT program names of #489/#494, matching render.c). EGL surfaceless means no display or window is needed, and it runs on whatever EGL provides: llvmpipe (CPU) in this container, the host GPU when a device is present. Measured: 89% of pixels (480x336, --scene capture + --camera) match the software renderer within +/-60 per channel, the output is byte-deterministic across runs, and verify_host.py pins coverage/determinism, skipping when EGL/GLES is absent. It composes with --window (GPU draw, SDL present). ENVIRONMENT FACT measured: the host is an AMD desktop GPU (its PCI ID, a DRM render node) but the container has NO /dev/dri and no /dev/nvidia*, so nothing inside can open it -- PCSX2 runs its software GS renderer (PCSX2.ini Renderer = 13). Restarting the container with --device=/dev/dri (and --group-add video/render) makes the GL path hardware-accelerated with no code change. NEGATIVE: the per-family VU1 work (TRANS/CLIP/COMPRESS/MULTIPASS, multi-texture, fog) is not implemented on the GPU yet; the shader covers the LIGHT model only.

**Notes:** port/Makefile (egl/glesv2 via pkg-config), include/dw4/gl_render.h, src/gl_render.c, engine.c --gl, tools/verify_host.py. GL_RENDERER here reports 'llvmpipe (LLVM, 256 bits)'.

**Evidence:** port/src/gl_render.c

<a id="f515"></a>
### #515 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0025a580 — port/src/gl_render.c

The GPU backend is now a real renderer surface: (1) textures -- dw4_gl_render/frame_mesh gained a UV attribute + sampler2D, the shader samples the same texpack decode the software path does (v flipped), and the light rig / fog / tint are uniforms; (2) the offline stage renderer has both backends -- 'dw4asset render --scene --gl' and '--name --gl' route through EGL/GLES, with --texture/--texentry; (3) --gl no longer requires a camera: dw4_render_fit_view exposes the software fit camera as a matrix, used by dw4 and dw4asset when no --camera/--view is given; (4) one shared light/fog rig -- dw4_render_parse_light/load_lights/parse_fog live in render.c and are consumed by dw4, dw4asset and gl_render, so software and GPU shade from the same dw4_light/dw4_fog. ENVIRONMENT CORRECTION to #513/#514: the container NOW has a DRI render node and GL reports 'AMD desktop GPU', not llvmpipe -- the host GPU was passed through (--device=/dev/dri). Measured: the dw4asset GPU scene render has the same geometry as the software one (silhouette IoU 1.000; only the no-normal face-normal vs dFdx shading differs), the single-mesh GL vs software agreement is 76-77%, verify_render.py pins gl-name/gl-scene coverage+determinism and that texture/fog change the GPU frame, verify_host.py pins the host GL path.

**Notes:** Still not implemented on the GPU: TRANS skinning/instancing, CLIP's projected-MVP path (GL already clips), TEXGEN, MULTIPASS, COMPRESS, BLEND. The FOG family is implemented (see the next finding).

**Evidence:** port/src/gl_render.c

<a id="f516"></a>
### #516 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0047a188 — VU1 FOG family / port/src/render.c

The VU1 FOG family is 3 programs -- FOG_LINEAR, FOG_EXP, FOG_EXP2 (plus CLIP_FOG_LINEAR/EXP/EXP2, the same effect at the clip stage) -- and is implemented on both backends. The effect is a fragment blend toward a fog colour by view-space depth: linear falls from start to end, exp/exp2 by density (exp(-d*d) for exp2). As with the LIGHT model (#498/#499) the values are runtime state, so the rig is a caller input: dw4_fog {mode,color,start,end,density}, parsed by dw4_render_parse_fog('\<linear|exp|exp2>,start,end,density,r,g,b') and installed by dw4_render_set_fog; the software rasteriser blends after tint+texture using the interpolated view-space z, and the GL fragment shader does the same via the vFogZ varying. CLI: dw4asset render/dw4 host take --fog SPEC. Pinned in verify_render.py (linear/exp/exp2 each differ from off and from each other, and the GL path's fog changes the frame).

**Notes:** FOG_LINEAR is VU1 image offset 77840 (0x13010); image base 0x00467178 -> 0x0047a188. FOG_EXP 0x0047a1c0 (+336), FOG_EXP2 0x0047a430 (+1056); the CLIP_FOG_* are at image offset 79680/79904/80208 -> 0x0047ad00/0x0047adc0/0x0047afb0.

**Evidence:** port/src/render.c

<a id="f518"></a>
### #518 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00378ed0 — beCameraCtrl::vfunc_31 / FUN_00378ed0 / beCameraMode

The camera system is decompiled (Ghidra C for beCameraCtrl and beCameraMode). beCameraCtrl (vtable 0x53a210) fields: _viewerMang +0x20, _viewerPara +0x24, _cameraMode +0x28, _avgPos +0x2c, _savePos +0x38, _isPosChange +0x44, _cameraNowFrame +0x48, _cameraNow +0x4c, _cameraOld +0x50, _eyeMatrix +0x54, _intMatrix +0x94. beCameraCtrlInfo._data (+0x1c) is a beCameraBoxData or beCameraDemoData: beCameraBoxData has _switch +8, _geometryList +0xc, _transformList +0x10, _eyePosList +0x14, _intPosList +0x18. beCameraMode has _cameraPath +0x4c, _camera +0x50, _insight +0x54, _svWorldTimeL +0x58, _nowTimeL +0x60, _timeLength +0x68, _isEnd +0x70, _isFreeze +0x71. UPDATE CHAIN: beCameraCtrl::vfunc_31 (0x379c40) is the per-frame update; it calls FUN_00378ed0 (0x378ed0, 2968 B, box-data path) when the camera mode is valid, else FUN_00379a90 (0x379a90, 424 B, demo/cutscene path). FUN_00378ed0 computes _avgPos = average of FUN_0038f5a0 eye points from the visual context (FUN_0038f630auStack_d0 = transform, FUN_00190a30 = get translation; 2/3/4-point averages), marks _isPosChange, picks the box whose bounds contain _avgPos (FUN_0018ab70), and on a box change copies _eyeMatrix <- _eyePosList[0] and _intMatrix <- _intPosList[0] via FUN_001908f0 (Insight matrix copy), else interpolates between the current and target box keys (FUN_00190a30 get, FUN_00192f00 lerp translation, FUN_001925f0/FUN_00192cc0 lerp matrices, FUN_00190a10 set). So _eyeMatrix is the camera EYE world matrix and _intMatrix the interpolation/look matrix, both authored per box in the stage's camera data. beCameraMode::vfunc_29 (0x37a7f0) copies _camera's position (+0x2c/+0x30/+0x34) into the Insight object (beCameraMode+8) at +0x50/+0x58/+0x54 and sets fov/near/far; beCameraMode::vfunc_28 (0x37a900) advances a camera path by _nowTimeL/_timeLength and pushes transforms (FUN_001c5b30/FUN_00191e30).

**Notes:** BLOCKER (measured, not a guess): a live 4x4 in the visual context with eye (365.1, 117.9, -77.3) puts _avgPos (the player/camera target, 421.83,72,-110.07) at screen centre at depth 80 -- it recurs 7x, and read as a view it gives the right dark/saturated lighting (mean RGB 42,46,50 vs the frame's 55,45,40; saturation 0.32 vs 0.03 for the pack camera) -- but the rendered geometry does not match the frame. Root cause candidate: the port's capture is INCOMPLETE for this state -- of 715 placement rows only 408 match a stag0102 mesh, and 121 distinct live names have no mesh in pack 621 (M_TR32_barrelShape, M_Ring_setShape, M_clineShape, the TR32 crate/barrel set, EFRoot, GG_*Shape, CF_*Shape,...), i.e. the frame's crates/props are not drawable from the stage pack. So the camera may well be right and the port's scene reconstruction is the blocker; a camera-matched capture needs the missing meshes resolved (which pack holds them) first.

**Evidence:** Ghidra decompilation of beCameraCtrl and beCameraMode; real Goblin Pass frame compared with port renders

(1) STAGE: the goblin_pass_crate_barrell savestate's EE RAM holds 'stag0101' 16x (0x5a7a44, 0x5b5f78, 0x5b5fa0) and 'stag0102' only 3x (stage list); stag0101.igb resolves to [0.613] (387,440 B, 0 geometry attrs) + [0.614] (4,793,568 B, 566 geometry attrs), so the stage model pack is 614, not 621. (2) MESHES: of the 715 live placement rows/354 distinct names, 269 names are meshes of pack 614 (vs 234 for 621); the only NAMES absent from 614 are 75 UI fonts (font*) and ~10 loading-screen props (M_clineShape/M_coverblackShape/M_hecksShape/M_loadShape/M_triside*/M_tritop*/EFRoot) that live in AREA ldag*.igb packs, not the scene. The runtime objects are placed by beModelCtrlNode2 (vtable 0x537f40, inherits igTransform so _m is at +0x20) and the pack mesh is named, not linked: the player's skin gobr_skinShape2 (pack 614 id 22308) is NOT in the live igGeometryAttr content match, and the crates boxShape (38675) / M_box_bodyShape (36249) / M_TR32_barrelShape (36873) only appear at their authored igTransform positions ((132.5,50,281.9) and (0,0,0)), not at the frame -- yet placing gobr_skinShape2 at the beModelCtrlNode2 node nearest beCameraCtrl._avgPos (0x016de170, _m (421.83,72,-110.07), rotation ~180deg about Y) and the crate meshes at the nearby runtime nodes (0x014df9d0 _m (415,71.2,-127.5), 0x0130a858 _m (425,71.2,-126.2)) reproduces the frame's character and crates. scripts/camera_match.py does exactly this: world-space environment = every pack mesh whose local bbox diagonal exceeds --big (default 150) at identity; --character MESH at the node nearest --target; --prop MESH@NODE_ADDR at that node's transform. Reproduced end to end: python3 camera_match.py --from <dump>.bin --pack NDMW.AFS:614 --target 421.83 72 -110.07 --character gobr_skinShape2 --prop M_TR32_barrelShape@0x14df9d0 --prop M_box_bodyShape@0x130a858 --tsv gp_cm.tsv -> 124 rows (121 environment), then dw4asset render --stage stag0101.igb --ref 1 --scene gp_cm.tsv --view \<view> --fov 0.6912 --aspect 1.0 --w 640 --h 448 --gl. The result is a legible camera-matched frame: same walls/floor/band, the character on screen centre, the crates to the left. RESIDUAL, and NOT a bug in the placement: the port frame is BRIGHTER than the real one (mean RGB 116,108,95 vs 54,44,40) because the meshes are untextured and the light rig is the placeholder (findings #485/#499) -- that is item 3 (texture/material capture). The environment subset is the bbox-size heuristic, not a live-set membership test; the band and some environment meshes are not in the igGeometryAttr chain (they are in the staged packet buffer), so the pack's world-space meshes stand in for the stage, which is exact for a static stage. **Where:** 0x005395c0 — beTextureCtrlAttr._attrSet / igTextureBindAttr / igGeometryAttr

The world-mesh texture binding is NOT on igGeometry/igGeometryAttr; only the UI/effect/font bindings are readable from the live object graph, and the world meshes' textures live in the staged render packet stream

**Notes:** Item 3 investigation (continues #485). WHAT IS READABLE: (1) a direct per-geometry-attr link exists only for fonts/effects -- a scan of all +0x04..+0x100 pointer fields of the 1023 live igGeometryAttr1_5 (vtable 0x5238c0) finds a path to an igTextureAttr (vtable 0x522c60) or igTextureBindAttr (vtable 0x522bd0, _texture +0xc) for only 39 of them, 32 of which are font00.png; (2) the general container is the material attr set: beTextureCtrlAttr (vtable 0x5395c0, _name +0x8) has _attrSet (+0xc), an igAttrSet-like object (vtable 0x5294b0) whose +0x1c child list holds igGeometry shapes (37), beGroupKeep (0x5360b0, 66) and model objects (0x5284f0, 33), and whose +0x20 holds the igTextureBindAttr (90). Following those shapes to their geometry attrs maps only 8-15 pack meshes to a texture. WHAT IS NOT READABLE: no live igGeometry shape's own +0x20 attr list contains an igTextureBindAttr (832 shapes scanned: 803 geometry-only); no attr list anywhere contains both a geometry attr and a texture bind (0 of 2766); no shape references a beTextureCtrlData (0 of 832); an upward reference BFS from the character shape gobr_skinShape2 (0x01386a5c, whose attr list holds exactly one igGeometryAttr 0x0138a9d0 with no texture field) reaches no material attr set within depth 5. The live session's beTextureCtrlData set is 40 textures, almost all UI (stmojipnl01, num01-03, base01-04, moji, stcngwaku*, stpnlwn*, bit) plus a few model textures (P10G00s/P10G00n/P10G02n/P11G00s/P11G00n, TA01BJ01/02/04/18/20, TA01GM02, TA02BJ01, FD01BJ03/05/06/07, Z1BJ01LM/Z1BJ0600, sfZ0SY10) -- there is no wood/crate texture and no obvious rock texture, and #467 already established the stage's texture buffers are in separate texture packs (NDMW 618/619/625/626), not entry 614. So the world/character/crate textures are applied by the staged packet stream (the GS-packet buffer region 0x00cc3000-0x00d3xxxx holds the environment vertex data verbatim plus 16-byte command records), and reading them needs the packet/TEX0 path the port deliberately does not emulate -- not a field on the object graph. [igGeometryAttr1_5, igTextureBindAttr, igTextureAttr, beTextureCtrlAttr._attrSet, beTextureCtrlData; offline EE dump of goblin_pass_crate_barrell.p2s] CORRECTION (finding #567): the '16-byte command records' in 0x00cc3000-0x00d3xxxx could not be reproduced -- a whole-32-MB scan for GIF tags finds none there (the region holds per-vertex data), and the VU1 mirrors are unreadable through PINE, so the packet stream has to be captured as a PCSX2 GS dump. The *material* side of this finding is superseded by #563 (the materials are live-built attr sets) and #565/#566 (per-shape textures); the alpha there is a constant 0x80 = 1.0, so the enabled blend is opaque. See #567.

<a id="f523"></a>
### #523 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00169840 — FUN_00169840 / FUN_0017aa20 / p2LoadTexture / igTextureAttr / beTextureCtrlData

The p2 texture path is FUN_00169840 (per-model-part texture setup) -> FUN_0017aa20 (allocates a 0x88-byte draw descriptor from the model's +0x144 table) -> p2LoadTexture 0x20c270 (builds the GS TEX0 fields); the descriptors carry width/height/format, not the image or a VRAM address, so the image->descriptor and mesh->model links are still open

**Notes:** Packet-path progress (continues #522). DECODED: FUN_00169840(param_1=model, param_2=partIndex, param_3=&descIndex) reads a 0x30-byte texture-source record table at *(model+0x364)+0x10 + partIndex*0x30 (record fields: +0 = px, +4 = py, +8 = format (8/7/0x18/0xf/0x10), +0xc = secondary format (0x20/0x18), +0x24 = a descriptor index, +0x28 = the primary descriptor, +0x2c = the secondary). It creates/updates a 0x88-byte draw record at *(model+0x144)+0x10 + idx*0x88 via FUN_0017aa20(model, px, py, format, 3, 0, 1) (FUN_0017aa20 pops a free index from the +0x144 pool, calls FUN_0017a070, then fills the record: [8]=px, [9]=py, [0]=format, [5]=unit?, [6]=0, and the low byte of [4]=0x10000 selects the size table 0x48ac70 vs 0x44aca0), stores the descriptor at draw+0x48 and the secondary at +0x4c, sets draw+0x84 = 2, and calls p2LoadTexture(0, px, py, formatCode, 0). p2LoadTexture (0x20c270) allocates from the FUN_00208210 pool (global 0x56c0c8), stores the image arg at descriptor+0x30 (here 0, so the descriptor holds NO image pointer), writes px/+0x3c, py/+0x3e, format/+0x42, TBW in puVar8[2] bits 14-19 = ((px+0x3f)>>6), a PSM-ish nibble at +0x12/+0x13, a size at +0x40 = (table[format]*iVar7)>>9, and +0x44 = size-1 clamped to 0x1f. Callers: FUN_00140060 (0x140060) calls it per model part, and p2LoadTexture is called directly from 0x169a10/0x169a2c/0x169ce4 (the texture manager) and 0x17a81c+ and 0x1ea584. REMAINING GAP: the 0x88 draw descriptor has no image pointer and no TBP0 VRAM address (p2LoadTexture's image arg is 0), so image->descriptor is not stated here; and the model object's class was not pinned (the 0x5284f0 objects have a bbox at +0x364 and a child model at +0x144, not the texture table, so param_1 is a different class). NEXT: pin the model class by its vtable (its +0x208/+0x20c slots are called), then find the 0x30-byte texture-source record table live (search for a table whose records' +0/+4 match live texture px/py and whose +0x28 is a 0x20c270 descriptor), and the image->VRAM table the descriptor indexes. [game.elf FUN_00169840/FUN_0017aa20/FUN_0020c270/FUN_00140060; offline dump] Folded into #524, the current answer for FUN_00169840 is an igPsx2VisualContext method; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** tex2.c

<a id="f524"></a>
### #524 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00169840 — igPsx2VisualContext / igSetRenderDestinationAttr / FUN_00169840 / FUN_00140060

CORRECTION to #523: FUN_00169840 is a method of igPsx2VisualContext (vtable 0x525870, its slot at 0x525934), and FUN_00140060 is igSetRenderDestinationAttr (vtable 0x522f50, its slot at 0x522fb0). The tables it reads are the visual context's own VRAM buffer/texture-page lists, so the p2 path is render-to-texture/destination setup, not world-mesh source texturing

**Notes:** Pins the class the handoff left open. The engine's ig*/be* classes carry no __RTTI__ record (data/vtables.json does not list them), so the class of a no-RTTI vtable is recovered from each class's \<Class>_GetVTable function (the first rodata-range value stored to a stack slot; scripts/seed_names_metafield_slots.py's rule): vtable 0x522f50 -> Gap::igSetRenderDestinationAttr, 0x525870 -> Gap::igPsx2VisualContext. Cross-checked live: the object at 0x6e5160 with vtable 0x525870 reads _pTexList (+0x144)=0x6e5918, _pClutList (+0x148)=0x6e7cc0, _pTexLoadedList (+0x14c)=0x6e5a08, _currentTex (+0x150)=56, _pBufList (+0x364)=0x6e5900, _currentBuf (+0x368)=3, _pList... -- exactly igPsx2VisualContext's reflected field offsets (data/reflection.json). So FUN_00169840's arg0 is a visual context and its 'partIndex' (arg1) is a VRAM buffer index into _pBufList (+0x364 -> +0x10 -> +arg1*0x30), while the 0x88-byte records are _pTexList (+0x144) texture descriptors. FUN_00140060(_pRDAttr +0xc, visualContext) is the render-destination attribute that drives it. CONSEQUENCE: the '0x30-byte texture-source table' (b) is igPsx2VisualContext._pBufList and the 'image->VRAM' side is the _pTexList descriptor pool; this path makes a GS texture *handle* for an already-in-VRAM buffer (p2LoadTexture's image arg is 0 because the pixels are in VRAM), so it is not the source-texture binding for world meshes. Closes (a) and (b) of the #523 next-step; (c)'s image->descriptor link is no longer the right target. Topic head for FUN_00169840 is an igPsx2VisualContext method: also read #523 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

### #525 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x005395c0 — beTextureCtrlAttr._attrSet / igAttrSet / igTextureBindAttr / igTextureAttr / igPsx2Image

The readable world-mesh texture binding is the material attr set: beTextureCtrlAttr._attrSet (+0xc) -> igAttrSet; its +0x1c child list holds the igGeometry shapes the material applies to, and its +0x20 _attributes list holds the igTextureBindAttr -> igTextureAttr._image (+0x30) -> igPsx2Image._pNameString (+0x58). 90 live geometry attrs (56 pack meshes) of stag0101 resolve this way, including the largest environment mesh and the BG ground/object sets; the textures decode from texture packs NDMW.AFS 618/619

**Notes:** Extends #522 (which found only the 39 direct geometry-attr links and 8-15 via _attrSet). The missing step was the child list: beTextureCtrlAttr._attrSet's *igAttrSet* has an (unreflected) child list at +0x1c (an igNodeList whose elements are igGeometry / beGroupKeep / beCopyModelCtrl2 / igSwitch / igAttrSet), reachable one level deeper via igGroup._childList (+0x1c). Following it and mapping each shape's geometry attr (+0x20 attr list, igGeometrySetAttr/G._geometryAttrList +0x14 indirection) gives 90 live igGeometryAttr1_5. The beTextureCtrlInfo sets are named: BGGround00n (P10G00n/P10G02n/P11G00n), BGGround00s (P10G00s/P11G00s), BGObj00n (TA01BJ01/02/04/18/20, TA02BJ01, FD01BJ03/05/06/07, FD02BJ07, Z1BJ01LM, Z1BJ0600, sfZ0SY10, P11G00n), BGObj00s (FD01BJ03, TA02BJ01), plus the UI sets (i_*), and the two 'pack' sets (the 40 live beTextureCtrlData). Content-matching the 90 attrs to stag0101's model pack NDMW.AFS:614 resolves 56 pack ids; the largest live environment mesh by vertex count (pack 614 id 30486, 3514 verts) gets TA01BJ02.png, and rows 7521/8391/9167/11669/11993 carry textures too. The texture names exist in NDMW.AFS entries 618 and 619 (e.g. entry 618: TA01BJ20 image id 80, TA01BJ01 id 80, P11G00n id 136, P10G00n id 140), and decode through the port's dw4_texpack. IMPLEMENTED: scripts/texture_map.py builds the map offline from an EE dump; capture_scene.py --textures and camera_match.py --textures write it as a sixth TSV column; port/src/dw4asset.c texcache_get decodes the named texture from a --texentry list and dw4_render_mesh_tex/dw4_gl_frame_mesh sample it per draw (tools/verify_render.py 'scene texture'). LIMIT: in the camera-matched Goblin Pass frame only 6 of 124 rows carry a recovered texture and 3 resolve, and those six are background/UI meshes not visible through the recovered camera, so the frame's brightness gap is NOT closed -- the visible ground mesh and character are drawn from the beModelCtrlNode2/beModelCtrlInfoRamData graph, whose material link is still open. ADDENDUM: the traversal must also descend through the `beModelCtrlNode2` model nodes a material set names (a `wall01z01` attr set holds an `igSwitch` pointing at one); adding it lifts the map from 90 to 106 live geometry attrs and resolves the `wall01z01` set (TA01GM02.png). The camera-matched frame then carries 8 textured rows (was 6), still mostly background/UI, so the gap stands.

**Evidence:** scripts/texture_map.py; port/src/dw4asset.c

<a id="f526"></a>
### #526 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0025a580 — FUN_0025a580 / FUN_0025a8c0 / app object 0x234900

FUN_0025a580 (the per-frame body, 832 B) reconstructs to a clean control flow, but the residual is callee-saved register assignment: retail is s0=frame object, s1=arg0, s2=FUN_002eaf60 result (sp-80); a hand C rewrite and the m2c draft both allocate arg0 to s0 and the frame to s1 and cache &arg0+24 in an extra s3 (sp-96)

**Notes:** Control flow (from the retail disassembly 0x25a580-0x25a8bc, confirmed by a rewrite that reproduces it structurally): guard on arg0->field24; DAT_0056df80[0]=arg0; lazily create the 240-byte frame object (FUN_001007e0 -> FUN_00238110) into DAT_0056dc50[0]; bump +0xe8, write +0xb4/+0xd5/+5=0, +0x94/+0x98/+0x9c=0.1, +0xa0=1.0, +0x5c=0, +0x5e/+0xa8=1; then if the counter decrements to <=0, release +0xec and +0 (the #509 idiom), FUN_00100610(frame), clear the global. Then the six void updates 0x239390/0x238d40/0x3343a0/0x33bf50/0x3719c0/0x3b04e0, the app calls 0x463b30/0x259f80/0x463c70(arg0), the render object 0x236f80(*(int*)arg0, DAT_00587d08[0]), the lazily-created 16-byte object 0x2eb010->0x2eadf0 into DAT_00586f20[0], and 0x2eaf60(DAT_00586f20[0], DAT_004ecf60) stored at render+0x3c after releasing the old value. Then two vtable calls: (*(arg0+0x18))'s [0x98]\(arg0) then its [0x2c4]\(result, &sp+0x40) with the 16-byte local {0,1.0f,0,0}. The tail is a two-level loop: an outer guard if arg0+0x2c==0 and render+0x10==0; an inner do-while that computes a1 = (*(int*)arg0->field64 < 0xFE502B) ^ 1 and zeroes it when field52 % 15 != 0, calls 0x259d40(arg0,a1,a2), and loops while 0x463bc0(arg0) (calling 0x463bb0 each time and re-checking arg0+0x2c/render+0x40); then if render+0x3c != 0, 0x25afe0(arg0), render+0x40=1, repeat the outer; else fall out to 0x25afe0(arg0), 0x463b70(arg0), DAT_0056df80[0]=0. The rewrite lives at build/m2c/FUN_0025a580.c (scratch, not banked) and reaches 194 differing words (was 207). DIAGNOSIS: the difference is not shape but allocation -- ours saves s0-s3 (sp-96) because (a) MWCC gives the parameter arg0 s0 and the frame pointer s1 where retail has frame s0 / arg0 s1, and (b) it materialises arg0+24 into s3 for the later virtual call where retail reloads 24(s1). First-definition order does not explain retail (arg0 is defined before the frame). Next: find a spelling/typing that makes the compiler assign the frame to s0 and keep arg0 in s1, and that does not CSE the arg0+24 address. ADDENDUM: the allocation is source-driven, not a compiler limit -- a minimal MWCC test shows a 2-parameter function with a `short` second parameter CAN be allocated s1=arg0/s0=arg1, but only when the first call in the body takes no argument; as soon as the call passes a pointer (the real `FUN_002ff820(temp_a0)`) MWCC flips to s0=arg0/s1=arg1. So the retail spelling differs from the obvious one in a way that changes the call's register setup, and the next step is to find that spelling rather than to change flags.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/build/m2c/FUN_0025a580.c

<a id="f527"></a>
### #527 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x00537F40 — vu1 BLEND / beModelCtrlNode2 / beModelCtrlInfoRamData / igVertexArray

VU1 TRANS is the model-view/projection family and the port already implements its effect; the missing posing is BLEND (skinning), and the per-vertex bone assignment is not in beModelCtrlInfoRamData (12-byte positions+normals, no index/weight stream) nor on the beModelCtrlNode2 tree

**Notes:** The port's renderer already performs the TRANS family's effect (object -> world -> view -> project): a mesh's placement matrix is the model-view input, so `dw4_render_mesh`/the GL shader cover the TRANS_MV*/MVPS* programs' semantics for unskinned geometry. What TRANS does NOT cover is skinning: the live player mesh is drawn in bind pose and covers the frame (finding #519), and the family that blends vertices by bone weights is BLEND (8 programs, W1-W4 weights per docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md), not TRANS. BLOCKER (measured): the per-vertex bone assignment is not in the live model data the port can read. IsotopeModelctrl's runtime data is `beModelCtrlInfoRamData` (vtable 0x537bb0), whose buffer is 12-byte stride positions+normals plus an `igPrimLengthArray1_1` (finding #454) -- there is no index array and no weight/index stream, so the vertex->bone map is not there. The skeleton is the `beModelCtrlNode2` tree (an `igTransform`, `_m` at +0x20, `_time` +0x70, `_timeScale` +0x78, `_overrideAttrList` +0x88; 1119 live), and the player node is 0x016de170 (finding #521), but its `_overrideAttrList` (0x016d5338) is EMPTY and its child list (0x016649a0) is the skeleton, not geometry. NEXT: read the BLEND program's constant record to see where the per-vertex weights come from (a second `igVertexArray` stream? a `beModelCtrl*` field?), then implement the blend in the port; until the weights are located, drawing the bind-pose mesh at the node matrix is all that is possible.
normalised no evidence recorded

Namespace comes from the nearest RTTI ancestor via data/class-hierarchy.tsv (498/499 resolve). The name is recovered (class from GetVTable, role from the slot index), never guessed from the body: a body used by more than one class's vtable (6115 slot instances) is a shared inherited default and is skipped, as is one found in a resolved ancestor's vtable. ### #531 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00528e70 — beModelCtrlNode2 0x016de170 / igGeometry 0x014cd81c / igGeometryAttr1_5 0x012763a8

The live skinned mesh identifies the player: on the Goblin Pass crate/barrel capture (stag0101, pack NDMW.AFS:614) exactly ONE live igGeometryAttr1_5 has a skinned vertex array, and it is ogre_skinShape (pack 614 id 5150, 1473 verts), not gobr_skinShape2. The earlier camera_match --character gobr_skinShape2 was the wrong mesh: the live gobr_skinShape2 (igGeometry 0x01386a5c) has an EMPTY vertex array (vdata 0, numVerts 0), so only the pack's bind-pose mesh was drawn. Placed at the player beModelCtrlNode2 0x016de170 (the node nearest the camera look-at 421.83,72,-110.07) ogre_skinShape gives the correct scale/silhouette (an Ogremon), and the save is the LV-99 Ogremon. scripts/camera_match.py --character auto now detects it: scan every live igGeometryAttr1_5, keep the ones whose igVertexArray1_1 has non-NULL _blendWeights (+0x20) AND _blendIndices (+0x24), content-match the vertex buffer to the pack id.

**Notes:** REFUTED by #535: ogre_skinShape is a separate NPC actor (green skin, bone club) reached from its own igBlendMatrixSelect 0x014cd768, not the player. It was the only live mesh with vertex blend weights, and the --character auto shortcut wrongly attached it to the player node. The player is Susanoomon: node 0x016de170 -> igLod -> 3 igActors -> igAppearance -> igSkin P_susanoo_Skin0Shape0/1/2 (skinnedGraph 0x01667468), model common/p_susanoo.igb (NDMW.AFS entry 861), textures P_susanoo01.png / P_susanoo02.png. The material-on-the-node mechanism of #533 is still valid; only its 'the character's texture is E_ogre01.png' claim was about the wrong character. The live Susanoomon skin vertex arrays read empty (nv 0), so the player mesh is not resident on that path and ogremon must not be drawn as the player. Folded into #535, the current answer for Player model is Susanoomon, not ogre_skinShape; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

### #536 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x2a3bc800 — scripts/igb_inst.py:find_chains

The igb chain finder must scan every file offset: a character model pack's records start at an ODD offset (3-mod-4), not the stage packs' 2-mod-4

**Notes:** find_chains stepped p+=2 from an even lo=0x800, so it never tried the odd offsets a character model pack uses; it also jumped p=q after a chain, skipping a start inside a short false chain. NDMW.AFS entry 861 (p_susanoo.igb) found 0 chains >1 and sections failed. Correct finder: scan every offset, decode, then a chain start is a hit whose offset no other record ends on (ends set), walk from starts only (O(bytes)), and drop a chain contained in another. Entry 861 now yields section1 215 @+0x260f and section2 6184 @+0x7241, exactly hdr[1]/hdr[5]; entry 621 regression re-derives all 549 attributes byte-for-byte. Stage packs (614/621) start 2-mod-4 and are unchanged.

<a id="f539"></a>
### #539 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x004482c0 — scripts/retype_block_receiver.py

An unnamed FUN_* bracketed by two vfuncs of one reflected class has a proven receiver class: typing it banked 31 game functions / 55 accesses (+14)

**Notes:** retype_receiver_class.py reads the class from the function NAME, so it cannot touch a FUN_*. But MWCC emits a TU's functions contiguously, so a FUN_* between two '...\<Class>__vfunc_N' symbols belongs to that class (the same reasoning #488/#530 used for FUN_00388e60). The new pass requires more than the block: every arg0/this offset cast must land on a reflected field of that class with matching width, or the function is left alone. It retypes the receiver and converts the casts (e.g. FUN_004482c0 writes beNDMWItemWeapon._kindNo/_recNo/_isEquip/_pocket/_revision -- 5/5). Applied --game-only (the module map's 0x334000..0x460000): 31 functions / 55 accesses, typed coverage 1983 -> 1997 (+14 of 5662, 35.3%). Two bugs found and fixed in the process, both in the two older passes: (1) the padding filter was startswith('_pad'), which also skipped 21 real fields named _padManager/_padList (they are igObjectRefMetaFields); (2) retype_receiver_class skipped any function whose receiver was already declared '\<Class> *arg0' instead of only void*arg0. Those two fixes converted a further 5 game accesses (beNDMWItemDisk._isEquip/_kindNo/_recNo, beNDMWMdlItem._insight, beNDMWMdlPlayer2._padManager). make check 9195/19277 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL; baseline updated.

**Evidence:** scripts/retype_block_receiver.py

### #540 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x014cd768 — scripts/live_skin.py; port/src/skin.c

The live skinning transform is v' = M[slot[bone]] * v, with the palette matrix row-major and its translation in elements 12..14; the shipped character mesh is single-bone (weight 1.0)

**Notes:** Read from the Goblin Pass paused dump (base 0x00100000). Chain: igVertexArray1_1 0x014cca90 (_vdata +0x08 -> positions 0x11fa248, _numVerts +0x0c = 1473, _blendWeights +0x20 = 0x0128b2f0 all 1.0, _blendIndices +0x24 = 0x01269ef0 values 2..32); igGeometryAttr1_5 0x012763a8 (_primType +0x14 = 4 strip, _stripLengths +0x30); igBlendMatrixSelect 0x014cd768 (_blendMatrixIndices +0x28 = igIntList 0x014cd740, 33 entries, slot map [0,0,0,1,2,3,4,5,0,6,7,8,9,0,0,10,11,12,13,14,15,0,16,17,18,0,19,0,20,0,21,22,23]); the palette is an igBlendMatricesAttr _matrixCache (+0x0c) count (+0x10), 64-byte matrices. The transform convention matters: row-major with translation in elements 12..14 gives a coherent posed mesh; the transpose gives a different, wrong result. A C implementation (port/src/skin.c) and a Python one (scripts/live_skin.py) produce byte-identical OBJ for the bind pose and every 24-matrix palette, and tools/verify_skin.py gates it. STILL OPEN (#532): nothing links the mesh/igBlendMatrixSelect to its own palette in the object graph, so which of the pool's 24-matrix attrs is the ogre's is unconfirmed -- the pool holds 0x007b3620/0x007b3660 (40), 0x007b3640/0x007b3680/0x007b36a0/0x007c3d78/0x007c4638 (24), and the 24-matrix ones differ only slightly. A bind-vs-posed render of the ogre was made. CONFIRMED ON THE PLAYER (dev_world.p2s): the player P_susanoo_Skin0Shape1 (3580 verts, igVertexArray1_1 0x01450540, igGeometryAttr1_5 0x014aaf88) is resident and posed in that state; its three LOD actors (0x014cb738/0x014cd058/0x014cd1e8) have skinnedGraphs 0x014548d0/0x01488340/0x01488890 (all 24 bones -> slots 0..20) and their _blendMatrixCacheArray is mostly identity, so the pose is in the pool: palettes [4] 0x007b36a0 and [12] 0x007b37a0 give byte-identical posed OBJs, so the pairing ambiguity is moot for this state. The C path and scripts/live_skin.py agree on both dumps; a bind-vs-posed capture of the player was rendered.

**Evidence:** scripts/live_skin.py

<a id="f541"></a>
### #541 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001e9770 — FUN_001e9770

FUN_001e9770 is the VU1 skinning-matrix upload: it reads an igBlendMatricesAttr pool palette and stages its 64-byte matrices for the VU1

**Notes:** Confirmed with a PCSX2 debugger read breakpoint (the game loads dev_world.p2s; PINE cannot see VU1 or MMIO, so the debugger is required). Set a read breakpoint at 0x00864FA0 -- the _matrixCache of igBlendMatricesAttr object 0x007b36a0 (24 matrices, the palette live_skin.py labels [4]) -- and it fired inside FUN_001e9770 at +0x250 (0x001E98F0..0x001E9934), a lq/sq loop that copies 64-byte records (4x lq -> 4x sq) advancing t7 and a1 by 0x40 each iteration. The function head reads the upload descriptor at *(0x568F04) (cursor at +48) and computes the stride as *(0x562D48) << 6; *(0x562D48) = 24, exactly this palette's matrix count, so the loop stages 24 skinning matrices. This corrects the earlier conclusion that the pose was not the igVertexArray path in a way that invalidates the offline result: the pose SOURCE is the igBlendMatricesAttr pool, which is what scripts/live_skin.py already reads, so its offline pose (palette [4] -> arms-down Susanoomon) was right. What remains open: (a) the debugger register pane read t7=0 at the hit (stale pane), so the exact source register is unverified; (b) a read breakpoint at the palette fires, but mutating the igVertexArray1_1 bind vertices (0x0143e4c0) never changed the frame, so the vertex buffer the VU1 skins from is not that EE-RAM copy (cached or uploaded separately).

**Evidence:** PCSX2 debugger, dev_world.p2s; find #540

<a id="f543"></a>
### #543 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x01450540 — scripts/live_skin.py; FUN_001e9770

The visible character's skinning is EE-side and its pose source is the igBlendMatricesAttr pool (not a VU1 skin); the pool palette is duplicated and also compacted in a per-object buffer

**Notes:** Negatives measured on the dev_world capture (base 0x00100000). (1) VU1 is not skinning this mesh: extracted vu1Memory.bin (16384 B) from two savestates (dev_world, slot 2) and matched ALL 3580 player bind vertices (igVertexArray1_1 0x01450540, positions 0x0143e4c0) plus a 64-vertex subset - 0 hits; VU1 data holds matrices and 0..1 / 255-ish runs, no vertex positions. (2) EE RAM has exactly one full copy of the mesh (0x0143e4c0); the two other 3-float matches (0x00d2d05c, 0x01448b10) do not extend into a buffer. (3) The pool palette (obj 0x007b36a0 cache 0x00864fa0, 24 matrices) is byte-identical to obj 0x007b37a0 cache 0x00865ca0 - which is why live_skin.py palettes [4] and [12] produced identical posed meshes - and appears compacted (duplicate M1 dropped, reordered) at 0x014ccb10 near the player node 0x014cb5d8. (4) Mutating the pool palette, the actor's _boneMatrixCacheArray/_blendMatrixCacheArray, and the bind vertices (every 20th -> 120) never changed the rendered frame, because the pose is recomputed into the upload each frame before the render. Confirms #541 (pool -> FUN_001e9770 -> VU1 staging) and #454 (posed character drawn from a RamData-style path, not igGeometryAttr._vertexArray).

<a id="f545"></a>
### #545 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x006eb0d0 — port/src/render.c dw4_render_mesh_n

The software renderer kept the frame's 'gaps' because the recovered Goblin Pass camera is inside the stage's ground and wall geometry, and dw4_render_mesh_n dropped any triangle with a vertex behind the near plane (sz\<a... continue). Clipping each triangle to z>=0.05 and fan-rasterising the clipped polygon restores the near geometry

**Notes:** Measured: before the clip, single-mesh renders of the big terrain meshes (614 ids 21721/21732/21833/21835/8533/3190/3195...) covered 0.0% of the frame (the empty clear colour); after clipping every one covers 100.0% with the recovered view. The clip interpolates in view space (the view transform is affine, so every mesh-space attribute interpolates with the same t), reprojects each new vertex, and fan-triangulates; with all three vertices inside it is exactly the old single triangle. Pinned by port/tools/verify_render.py ('near-plane': pack 614 id 21721 from the recovered view must cover >=50%). The other half of the 'gaps' is the flat screen-space cards (finding #546).

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/findings/dw4.db; port/src/render.c; port/tools/verify_render.py

<a id="f546"></a>
### #546 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x01169000 — scripts/camera_match.py pack_bounds/stage_fallback_textures

The camera-matched env set (camera_match --big: every pack mesh with bbox diagonal>150) is not the world set: 40 of its 121 meshes are flat screen-space cards (extent in z exactly 0 in the pack: color0Shape1, maskShape, fadein/bgclear, win0Shape1-4, pSphereShape3/4, M_bgmainShape, polySurfaceShape*...), and those cards are the 'gaps' -- the big tan/lavender planes of the flat render and the dark planes after texturing

**Notes:** Measured on NDMW.AFS:614 with the goblin_pass dump: of the 121 --big meshes, 81 have z extent >1 and 40 have z extent <=0.05 (204 of the pack's 566 meshes are flat cards). The separation is a clean gap -- no mesh has 0.05\<z extent<2, and the world geometry's z extents run 30..714. The real membership test is therefore 'large AND 3D': camera_match --min-z DZ (default 1.0) replaces the bbox-only test, and the env set falls 121 -> 81. Excluding the cards removes the flat planes; what remains dark is the world geometry under the placeholder light rig (finding #499) and the unjoined material (finding #544), not missing meshes. Negatives: (a) the staged packet region also holds the cards (they are staged), so 'in the staging buffer' is not a world test; (b) the cards are placed (they have live transforms), the unplaced live shapes are the world -- but the RamData ground mesh has no live shape at all, so neither is a complete test either. FOLLOW-UP (same session): the z extent alone is not enough -- the placed backdrop sphere pSphereShape4 (614 id 29626, z extent 460) stayed and rendered as a grey atlas band. The second half of the test is the live placement: walk the live igGeometry shapes (capture_scene placement), and drop a pack id that the scene *positions with a real transform* (a prop, a backdrop sphere, the character) unless the same id is also drawn unplaced. Env set 121 -> 81 (bbox+3D) -> 55 with placed-only ids removed, and the grey band is gone. camera_match has a --keep-placed escape hatch.

<a id="f548"></a>
### #548 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x006eb0d0 — port/src/render.c raster_tri

The software rasteriser's depth was wrong twice over: the barycentric weights were A/B-swapped and it interpolated view depth linearly in screen space instead of perspective-correctly, so a distant wall won the z test against the near barrel/crate. Interpolate 1/w and use the perspective-correct weights

**Notes:** The barycentric convention from the normals is w2->A, w1->B, w0->C (w2=1-w0-w1; w0=cross(B-A,P-A)/det=lambda_C, w1=lambda_B, w2=lambda_A), so the depth line 'w1*saz + w2*sbz + w0*sdz' multiplied A by the B weight and B by the A weight -- provably wrong whenever sz[A]!=sz[B], which is every large triangle. The fix also makes it perspective-correct: z = 1/(w2/saz + w1/sbz + w0/sdz) and every attribute uses q_i=(w_i/z_i)/sum(w_j/z_j). Measured: the Goblin Pass frame changes from a far-wall-dominated frame (the wall over the barrel/crate, as observed) to the near floor mound winning the depth test with the crate fully visible. GL (if compiled) and the GS both interpolate perspective-correctly, so this also removes a software/GPU divergence. make check green (crosscheck all agreed, near-plane ok).

**Evidence:** port/src/render.c

### #551 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x01169000 — scripts/camera_match.py stage_fallback_textures (wall default = ground variant) + the floor/wall split in the env loop

The WALL texture is P11G00n (the BG ground material set's `n` variant, NDMW.AFS:618/619) -- a 256x128 atlas that carries both the mottled rock and the wall's blue light-dash band. The floor is the same set's `s` variant P11G00s (brown cracked mud). Assigning P11G00n to the wall/dome shell reproduces the real frame's rock AND the blue band's curve; assigning P11G00s (the old default) leaves the wall as flat brown mud

**Notes:** Split fix: the old rule `mean normal y > 0.5 -> ground` called BOTH the floor (31788, mean normal (0.04,1.00,0.02)) and the wall/dome shell (21835, (-0.47,0.86,0.19)) ground, so the dome got P11G00s. The floor is the near-planar horizontal mesh; the dome wraps, so its mean normal stays tilted even though it points up. New rule: floor iff n[1] > 0.5 AND |n[0]|+|n[2]| < 0.15. Evidence: per-mesh renders (only 21835 and 31788 draw in this view -- 21835 the walls/ceiling, 31788 the floor) and a candidate sweep (P11G00n / P10G00n / TA01GM02 / TA01BJ04 / TA01BJ02 / P10G00s / TA01BJ01 / Z1BJ0600 / TA01BJ20 / TA02BJ01 / FD01BJ07 -> only P11G00n and TA01BJ02 carry a band; P11G00n band shape and rock match). The real per-draw binding is still the staged packet material (#522). Topic head for Goblin Pass wall texture: also read #547 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

<a id="f557"></a>
### #557 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x01169000 — NDMW.AFS:614 M_BK19a0_boxShape (attr 12676) + igAttrSet g_woodencasez01 (inline 32x32 atlas)

The crate draws exactly what the game draws: its mid-height metal band and its metal corner posts are the GAME's own look, not a port defect (finding #556 refuted).

**Notes:** Measured three ways. (1) Real frame: the crate in the real Goblin Pass frame shows the same wood planks + a bright metal band across the MIDDLE of each face + bright corner posts, so the 'band mid-face' #556 called a defect is present on the PS2. (2) The count of wood repeats matches: the atlas's wood block holds 2 planks, and the crate's face is 2x2 quads each mapping the whole atlas (the v coordinate folds at y=4.819, the face's own middle: v=1/32 at the middle and 31/32 at the outer edges), so a face shows 4 planks, exactly what the real crate shows. (3) Live draw data is byte-identical to the pack decode: the crate's 415x12-byte position stream at 0x0143c3c0 and 415x8-byte uv stream at 0x0143eab0 match igb_models' decode of entry 614 byte for byte, and the inline image's 1024-byte 8bpp index block is byte-identical at 0x0130ac20. The material binding is confirmed live too: the crate node 0x0130a858's beModelCtrlInfo (vtable 0x537c10) at 0x0130a690 carries the name string 'g_woodencasez01' at +0x08 (the reflection-declared _kindString at +0x1c is KIND_OBJECT, which is what identifies it as a beModelCtrlInfo). So neither the texture decode nor the UV stream is at fault, and the remaining difference from the real frame is shading (finding #559: the vertex normals), not the texture. Topic head for Crate metal band is the game's own look: also read #556 (folded in, kept for their evidence).
normalised address corrected from 0x001169000 (nine digits) to 0x01169000 — the sibling rows cite EE RAM objects at 0x01xxxxxx, and the other reading would sit inside the Sony library code.

**Evidence:** scripts/pack_inline_tex.py; real Goblin Pass frame compared with the pack decode and the live draw buffers

<a id="f558"></a>
### #558 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0143c3c0 — live EE RAM (Goblin Pass dump): positions 0x0143c3c0, uvs 0x0143eab0, inline image index data 0x0130ac20

The staged/RamData render buffers carry the PACK's geometry unchanged: the live EE RAM holds the crate's whole position and uv streams byte-identical to the decoded pack, and its inline texture's index block too.

**Notes:** Route: the live igGeometryAttr1_5 at 0x01308de0 has its _vertexArray (0x0131c930) DRAINED (_vdata 0, _numVerts 0) -- the game copies geometry into its render buffers and frees the array -- so the drawn bytes had to be found by value. Searching the dump for the crate's own stream prefixes found: the full uv stream (415 x {f32 u,f32 v}, 3320 B) contiguous and byte-identical at 0x0143eab0 (referenced by 0x00930550; the 4-byte size 0x26f0 = 2*4980 + 8 sits just before it at 0x0143eaa8), and the position stream (415 x 3 f32, 4980 B) at 0x0143c3c0 = 0x0143eab0 - 8 - 2*4980 (the normals block sits between them, unit-length triples). The 1024-byte 8bpp index block of the inline image is byte-identical at 0x0130ac20. CONSEQUENCE: #522's 'the material is applied in the staged packet stream' does not extend to the crate's GEOMETRY or to its texture data -- what the port draws from the pack is what the game draws. (An earlier search for the vertex values alone missed the buffer because a *second*, reordered copy of similar values sits at 0x00e379dc; the crate's own streams are the ones at 0x0143xxxx.)

**Evidence:** Offline search of the Goblin Pass EE RAM dump for the crate's pack streams

<a id="f559"></a>
### #559 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x01169000 — port/src/dw4asset.c cmd_render (scene path); src/render.c dw4_render_mesh_n; src/gl_render.c

The port's scene path dropped the meshes' vertex normals even though every drawn stage mesh ships a unit-length normal stream; interpolating them (as the engine's LIGHT programs do) is the one measured improvement to the flat-looking Goblin Pass frame.

**Notes:** The pack ships the normals: for all 23 camera-matched rows of NDMW.AFS:614 (the six big terrain meshes, the floor, the props, the character) the second 12-byte stream is unit length to 1.0000 -- the '130 of 669 meshes have normals' of #458 is a property of the LIVE objects, not of the pack. The scene path merged positions and uvs only, so a scene row shaded with the per-triangle face normal while the game interpolates the mesh's own normal. Fixed on both the frame path (--view) and the merged path (--fit); --flat restores the baseline and tools/verify_render.py's 'scene normals' check now pins it (pack 621 id 1141, 3599 verts, must render differently with and without). Measured on the camera-matched Goblin Pass frame (whole-frame mean |delta| against the real frame / floor-box mean (260,330)-(420,420) over 0..255): face normals 31.27 / (72.7,50.2,31.0) -> vertex normals 28.86 / (68.0,47.0,31.0); the real frame's floor box is (69.7,47.5,30.6). The wall darkens (34.8 -> 33.5) and the frame stops looking faceted.

<a id="f560"></a>
### #560 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x01169000 — port/src/render.c light_term/raster_tri, src/dw4asset.c PALETTE; scripts/pack_lights.py

Four other 'flat frame' suspects measured and refuted: one-sided lighting, the per-mesh hash tint, the specular term, and using the stage pack's own light rig are each neutral or worse on the Goblin Pass frame.

**Notes:** (1) ONE-SIDED N.L is a NO-OP here: '--one-sided' changes a handful of pixels (numerically identical boxes) because the key light sits behind the camera -- for the visible surfaces N.L is already positive, so |N.L| and max(0,N.L) agree. The flag is kept (--one-sided/--two-sided) but the finding's 'single most likely cause of flat' is refuted; where it would bite is a surface the light cannot reach, which the port cannot show without culling. (2) WHITE PER-MESH TINT is much worse: whole-frame mean (50.9,38.5,26.7) -> (67.2,56.4,37.5) and the wall box (33.5,32.3,26.7) -> (58.0,64.9,49.6) against the real (40.9,36.5,41.2); the hash palette's darkening happens to land the floor on the real value (67.9 vs 69.7), so it is left as the default. (3) SPECULAR is negligible: zeroing the rig's (1.3,1.3,1.17)@16 leaves the means unchanged (mean |delta| 28.86 -> 28.88). (4) THE STAGE PACK'S OWN RIG is not closer: NDMW.AFS:614 (the stage's own igLightAttr records, scripts/pack_lights.py) is ambient (0.5,0.5,0.55) + key dir (0.3214,-0.866,0.383) diffuse (1.3,1.3,1.17) + the inert white spot, against the built-in rig's (0.4,0.4,0.45) + (0.2424,-0.8192,-0.5198)*(0.8,0.8,1.1). With the stage rig the wall box collapses to (21.4,20.6,14.4) and the wall/floor ratio to 0.36 against the real 0.80 and the built-in rig's 0.63, so the built-in rig stays the default and --lights remains the route. FOG darkens the frame toward the real's mean but also pulls the floor away from the real (67.9 -> 55.7), i.e. it fits the wrong-material wall rather than adding the game's depth cue; no default fog.
