<a id="f601"></a>
### #601 · refuted

**Where:** 0x0143c3c0 — crate draw TEX0 0x4007960555307cac (M_BK19a0_boxShape, attr 12676)

REFUTED: there is NO single global per-channel response curve over the whole covered frame. The framebuffer is not any function of the modulate product pred = Cv*texel/128 alone: at matched pred (4-wide bins, n>=60) the world and floor surfaces differ by +8.7..+22.5 (R), +11.3..+23.6 (G), +15.4..+27.5 (B); and the crate's dark face has a CONSTANT vertex colour (31,25,44 on all 4 verts) yet fb=(55.2,45.3,45.8) vs pred=(26.4,18.6,18.2), ratio 2.1/2.4/2.5 -- no interpolation convention can make a constant colour diverge, so the deviation is a genuine per-draw combine term, not a shared nonlinearity.

**Notes:** Whole covered frame, same-instant combat dump, corrected fb decode (#588). Whole mean pred (41.1,31.4,24.3) vs fb (53.8,43.4,39.5). Best global affine g=(0.591,0.635,0.580), lift=(29.5,23.5,25.4), per-pixel mad=(18.9,16.8,17.6). Best global isotonic (monotone LUT through 5-wide bucket means, 45-51 knots) per-pixel mad=(18.8,16.6,17.3) -- no better than the affine and far from the ~2-3 target. Per-surface bias of that one curve: world +5.4/+6.4/+7.2, floor -4.9/-5.3/-5.7, crate +12.3/+8.8/+5.5, barrel +6.2/+3.1/-2.1, char +7.9/+2.2/+2.8. Per-surface affine on each surface's own depth-won mask: world (0.66,0.65,0.56)+(33,30,34); floor (0.47,0.40,0.26)+(29,24,25); crate (0.69,0.61,0.53)+(38,36,34); barrel (0.55,0.53,0.53)+(40,33,27); char (0.43,0.38,0.42)+(50,40,39) -- gains 0.43..0.69, lifts 24..50, not one family. Folded into #604, the current answer for GS fog stage and stage fog parameters (combat dump); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** Matched-bin comparison of framebuffer against the modulate product on the corrected decode of the combat frame

<a id="f602"></a>
### #602 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0143c3c0 — PCSX2 GS dump header screenshot field; GIF record stream

#590's deviation is BAKED INTO THE FRAMEBUFFER by the per-draw combine, not applied by a display path or a full-screen post pass. (a) The dump's embedded raw 640x480 RGBA screenshot equals the decoded 640x448 framebuffer per box to <0.25/255 (whole means 53.77/53.78, 43.43, 39.51/39.52; crate 0.22/0.18/-0.03) -- PCSX2's presentation applies no transform. (b) The record stream carries ZERO IMAGE (flg2) GIF packets and never writes BITBLTBUF/TRXPOS/TRXREG -- there is no VRAM copy / BBPASS transfer over the framebuffer. (c) No draw is full-screen (the largest on-screen bbox coverage comes only from off-screen vertex spans); every scene draw is TME=1 TFX=0 ABE=0 IIP=1, and the 1104 ABE=1 draws are small overlays (HUD, shadows, the wall light band). So the deviation lives inside the per-draw shading/blending.

**Notes:** CORRECTION (see #603): the sub-claim 'F=(XYZ[2]>>24) is 0 on all 45,732 vertices -> fog is genuinely dead' is an EXTRACTION ARTIFACT and is WITHDRAWN. The dump stores the XYZF2 packed register as four dwords (X,Y,Z,F at vertex bytes 32,36,40,44); PCSX2 GIFPackedRegHandlerSTQRGBAXYZF2 extracts F=(dword44>>4)&0xff via zf=(raw>>4)&(0x00ffffff|0xff<<32). Reading XYZ[2]>>24 takes the top byte of the 24-bit Z dword, which is structurally 0. The real F is nonzero (0..255) and fog is LIVE (FGE=1 on 99.96% of the frame). The rest of #602 (screenshot==framebuffer; no IMAGE/BBPASS/post pass) STANDS. Folded into #604, the current answer for GS fog stage and stage fog parameters (combat dump); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** The dump's embedded screenshot compared with the decoded framebuffer per box, and a scan of the record stream, on the combat frame

<a id="f603"></a>
### #603 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0143c3c0 — GS fog stage (GIFPackedRegHandlerSTQRGBAXYZF2); PRIM.FGE; #590/#602 correction

The framebuffer excess over pred=Cv*texel/128 IS the GS fog stage, which #602 wrongly excluded. PRIM.FGE is 1 on 99.96% of the covered frame, and fb = pred*(F/255) + FOGCOL*(1-F/255) to the mean: whole pred=(41.6,31.9,24.8) fb=(53.8,43.4,39.5) fog=(54.4,43.4,38.4); world TBP 0x2fc000 fog=(56.9,52.7,49.8) vs fb=(56.9,52.4,49.7); wall fog=(39.9,36.0,41.3) vs fb=(39.9,35.7,41.2); barrel fog=(67.5,53.0,41.8) vs fb=(66.9,52.5,41.6). A 5x5 blur (the port metric regime) drops whole MAE 17.4/15.2/17.3 -> 8.2/7.0/6.9 and the wall hits the ~2-3 bar (23.5 -> 2.4). The per-vertex fog F is the 4th dword of the packed XYZF2 vertex (bytes 44..47), F=(dword44>>4)&0xff, exactly as PCSX2 GIFPackedRegHandlerSTQRGBAXYZF2 extracts it (zf=(raw>>4)&(0x00ffffff|0xff<<32)); it is nonzero (0..255, p50 236). This also settles the #590 crate-dark-face puzzle: a per-vertex fog F varies across the face, so the post-Cv combine is fog.

**Notes:** Offline-reproducible from the same-instant dump. Region-mean residuals left: floor box negative (overlay-covered, not a clean floor sample) and char TBP 0x37c000 over-predicted (bright surface). Per-pixel corr(residual, fog term)=0.30-0.36: the fog term fixes the MEAN per surface; the remaining per-pixel scatter is pred/sampler (winner-raster + bilinear) noise, not a second combine. Folded into #604, the current answer for GS fog stage and stage fog parameters (combat dump); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** scripts/gsdump.py; PCSX2 GSState.cpp GIFPackedRegHandlerSTQRGBAXYZF2; GS dump of the Goblin Pass combat frame

<a id="f604"></a>
### #604 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x55ff88 — igPsx2VisualContext (_fogEnable/_fogDensity/_fogMode/_fogColor/_fogNear/_fogFar); VU1 FOG_LINEAR; GS FOGCOL

The game's stage fog is a FOG_LINEAR program, near 60 / far 300 in view-space depth, toward FOGCOL 0xE5CCE5; the port now applies it by default and the whole frame improves 14.70->10.96.

**Notes:** Recovered the parameters the #603 model lacked. From the GS dump's per-vertex F (packed XYZF2 4th dword, F=(dword44>>4)&0xff): F is exactly linear in view-space depth over its unsaturated span (0\<F<255, n=31480), free fit F=318.2812-1.06287*viewZ -> near 59.54 / far 299.45, MAE 0.254/255; the engine's own (60,300) reproduces the dump at 0.499/255. The values are the engine's: (60,300) appears as a float pair across the live EE RAM, and the reflection carries the fields on igPsx2VisualContext (MetaObject 0x55ff88: _fogEnable u8 @0x514, _fogDensity f32 @0x518, _fogMode @0x51c, _fogColor vec4f @0x520, _fogNear @0x530, _fogFar @0x534) - this supersedes the port README's old 'the disc states no fog values' line. FOGCOL from the dump freeze (reg 0x3d) = 0xE5CCE5 = (229,204,229). Port: dw4_render_game_fog installs FOG_LINEAR 60/300 FOGCOL; render.c computes F per vertex from view-space z and interpolates it AFFINELY in screen space (like the vertex colour), then out=C*(F/255)+FOGCOL*(1-F/255); the scene path defaults to it (--no-fog drops, --fog overrides). frame_metric --blur 1.2 on goblin-pass: whole 14.70->10.96, floor 9.36->9.19, wall 13.83->11.80, barrel 12.17->8.28, crate 16.71->14.36, char 23.13->21.89; without the HUD overlay the whole is 15.90->12.09. A trap: the port's fog block must be gated on mode!=OFF, else the affine F interpolation (which recomputes w2+w1+w0 != exactly 1) perturbs 64 pixels by up to 3 even with fog off. Topic head for GS fog stage and stage fog parameters (combat dump): also read #590, #601, #602, #603 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** Reference / no-fog / default-GS-fog comparison renders; port src/render.c dw4_render_game_fog; port tools/verify_fog.py; port tools/frame_metric.py --blur 1.2; GS dump of the Goblin Pass combat frame; RAM plane 0x100000+0x149f474 (60,300 float pair) and 0x100000+0x17c1e10 (igPsx2VisualContext instance); DW4_Decomp/data/reflection.json class 0x55ff88

<a id="f605"></a>
### #605 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00100008 — game entry (CRT 0x00100008 / boot chain 0x002595e0, #502); menu code not yet identified

THE BLANK-SLATE BOOT SEQUENCE IS CAPTURED END TO END AND THE MAIN MENU IS REACHABLE. Power-on with no savestate gives: Intrinsic Alchemy (publisher) -> Sodec + ADK (developer) logo cards -> the 'DIGIMON WORLD' and 'DIGITAL MONSTER' title cards -> the long 'Annihilation' intro FMV (carrying the 'The Scourge of the Royal Knights!' card) -> the title screen 'DIGIMON 4 / DIGIMON WORLD' showing 'Press START button' -> the main menu with 'Game Start' (highlighted) and 'Option'. Advancing past 'Press START' is blocked by the pad's SDL Start not reaching PCSX2, so it needs [Pad1] Start bound to Keyboard/Return; Cross skips the FMV. The engine boot chain behind this is mapped statically in #502/#505.

**Notes:** Captured live PCSX2 software renderer 640x448, pristine disc. 9 frames captured. Timing is PCSX2-paced: logo cards within ~20 s, the FMV is tens of seconds and skippable with Cross. The main menu is a resumable PINE state: pi-pine savestate --slot 30. The screenshot hotkey (F8) also writes a.gs.zst next to each PNG.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/front-end-boot.md

<a id="f606"></a>
### #606 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003f1a80 — Gap::Bec::beNDMWLoadCtrl2::vfunc_31 @0x003f1a80 (front-end load controller)

THE FRONT-END BOOT IS A LUA-DRIVEN STAGE SEQUENCE, NOT A HARD-CODED MENU FLOW (finding #606). Power-on to the title menu is a chain of NDMW.AFS stage entries -- 800 menu-database, 802 bandai-logo (scene stage stagIS09), 804 opening (stagIS10), 806 title-screen (AM_TITLE), 808/810/812 character-select, 814 endroll -- each a set of Lua chunks run in order that drive a named 3-D scene stage. The transition API is the script's be:DMsetSD('STAGECTL', nil, -2, 'NEXTSTAGE', '\<stage>', mode) + be:DMrun, the same template in every front-end dm-run chunk (stagis09/stagis10 mode 0, stagML11 mode 9), with the input gate be:sd('STAGECTL', nil, -2, 'PADINPUT', 'ENABLE', 29). Controllers: beNDMWStageCtl (vtable 0x53dea0, RTTI 0x515708) with beNDMWStageCtlInfo (_stageName/_nextStage/_isStageLoop at 0x501d00/0x501d10/0x501d20); beNDMWLoadCtrl2::vfunc_31 (0x3f1a80, 2512 B) is the front-end load controller whose name table at 0x518130.. reads COMMONINTF, LOADINTF2_ALL, j_chrselback, SQ_OPEN/SQ_WTIN60/SQ_BLOUT30/SQ_CLOSE, SETSELECT_DATA, GAMERAM, GETCOMMON, NINZU, DIFFICULTY, CAMERAMODE, LIGHTMODE, TITLE2, GAMESTART, gameStart.lua, PLAYERPOINT, SETGENERATEPOS, STAGECTL, stagML11, NEXTSTAGE, LOADCTRL2, GAMEDATAINIT; beNDMWTitle2 (vtable 0x53e9a0, RTTI 0x5186f8) vfunc_31 (0x3f2ef0, 8700 B) references stagIS08/stagIS09 and vfunc_29 (0x3f5320) references TITLE2, so the title controller also runs the logo scenes; the menu rows are beNDMWTitle2Option (vtable 0x53dcf0); beNDMWLogo (vtable 0x5409d0) is the logo splash. The scene-stage name table is one.data run at 0x50e668: start.lua, first.lua, stagIS08, stagIS09, stagIS10, stagIS12.

**Notes:** New repo doc: docs/engine/front-end-boot.md, indexed from docs/engine/README.md and docs/README.md. The front-end entries are ordinary stage entries in the same loader the world stages use -- there is no separate menu code path; the difference is the script set and the scene stage. Open: which function starts the very first entry at power-on (the boot-chain tail #502/#505 is the candidate). The title's Lua writes the scene names lower-case (stagis09/stagis10) while the ELF spells them upper-case; one side is normalised at lookup. The 0x30xxxxxx addresses strings.json reports for start.lua/stagIS10/beNDMWStageCtlInfo/gameStart.lua/NEXTSTAGE are a second image in the dump, not the ELF.data.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/front-end-boot.md

<a id="f608"></a>
### #608 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0053e9a0 — beNDMWTitle2 vtable 0x0053e9a0; front-end packs NDMW.AFS 800/802/804/806; font asset 79; UI meshes stageML11 pack 820

The front-end 2D UI (logo cards, title art, menu rows) is pack data plus a runtime layout, not a scene: the TEXT is bectext.igb (NDMW.AFS entry 79; classes igFont/igBitmapFont/beFontImage + igCharMetricsList, names font00.png) rendered into bitmap-font quads; the ELEMENTS are a named igNode/igGroup tree in NDMW.AFS entry 806 (cursor, maru, OPEN/SQ_OPEN, LINEON00/LINESL00, WAIT01, PromotedAttr; 8398 records) with the screen-space quad meshes (mojiL, sen, pnum, selcurShape, cursors) in the stage packs (stageML11 pack 820); the PLACEMENT is computed by the engine at runtime, NOT stored - a live capture of the menu state resolves only 23 of 432 nodes to a matrix (the cursor/arrow marks), so a native 2D UI renderer needs igNode/igGroup loading + beAction2* actions + a screen-space (ortho, centre-origin, +y up) pass.

**Notes:** CONFIRMED + IMPLEMENTED, and the whole menu now renders. 2026-10-02d: the frame's blend is PER DRAW and the replay assumed one. gs_ui.py now emits a blend code per row -- 0 opaque (ABE off), 1 alpha (ALPHA 0x44, (Cs-Cd)*As+Cd), 2 additive (ALPHA 0x48, Cs*As+Cd); on the menu frame 816/1257 draws are ADDITIVE and they are exactly the tunnel backdrop's light passes, so blending them left every sampled backdrop patch 15-25 low. Measured: logo+rows+copyright+backdrop, whole-frame mean error vs PCSX2's own PNG of that frame 28.9 -> low-frequency (8px blur) 19.20 -> 18.97 with the additive passes; the logo (previously dropped) alone took it 85.2 -> 55.2. Lit (per-vertex colour) draws are kept and interpolated too. STILL SHORT OF 1:1: the backdrop sits ~15% low in the mid-tones; the port-vs-reference tone map is monotone but not linear (16->46, 80->101, 144->150, 240->220) and gamma 0.80 only recovers 29.5->25.7, and no ABE-off draw survives the viewport test (dropped opaque passes ruled out) -> the GS blend's C*Ad term (Ad = destination alpha) is the next suspect.

**Evidence:** DW4_Port main 6502f6f; dw4asset render --stage roms stagml11.igb --ref 0 --scene menu.tsv --livedump menu.bin --ui-native 0.42 --ui ui.tsv --uitex ui_menu

<a id="f609"></a>
### #609 · confirmed

**Where:** front-end main menu (stagML11/stagML02 window), PCSX2 running the menu; 34 GS dumps captured with the screenshot hotkey

The front-end main menu's animation is the engine REGENERATING its additive light-trail layer every frame - it is not a transform of a static backdrop, which is why the port could not animate it from one captured frame.

**Notes:** Evidence (image + draw list, measured): (1) the backdrop's GEOMETRY DOES NOT MOVE: angular brightness profiles about the screen centre, 1-degree bins upsampled 20x, best shift <=0.05 deg with correlation 0.97-0.99 across 1.4 s; radial profiles best scale k=1.000 (corr 0.95-0.99); a polar high-pass correlation peaks at dr=0 (0.936). No rotation, no zoom, no flow. (2) What changes is the ADDITIVE layer (ALPHA 0x48, the streaming dotted trails): its draw count changes every frame - 384 / 850 / 808 in three consecutive captures, mean 690 over 34 - and keying draws by (tex0, screen x, y) only 10 of ~1100 survive frame to frame, and those 10 are the STATIC UI (logo texture 0x40076c0625323960 and the font textures). (3) The textures are byte-identical across frames (all 5.rgba hashes equal), so the change is the DRAW LIST, not VRAM. (4) Visible: 774,941 px differ over 6 s in the live menu (mean |delta| 13.2) vs 0 px in the port's still frame. Port consequence: --ui-seq cycles captured frames (whole frame, GS draw order, so the trails stay behind the logo) at the capture rate; PCSX2 writes a GS dump every ~190 ms (5.2 fps), so the engine's real effect is replayed at ~5 fps, not 60. Splitting by blend code and drawing the additive layer last put the trails OVER the logo - wrong, hence whole frames.

**Evidence:** the port; scripts/menu_anim_seq.py

<a id="f610"></a>
### #610 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00518130 — beNDMWTitle2 / front-end stage stagML11 window; scene pack AREA.AFS entry 620 (objects: igSceneInfo 'j_startback', igAttrSet 'BG02', igImage 'sback00.png', beModelCtrlInfo/beAction2Info 'j_startback', beModelCtrlData SQ_OPEN/SQ_WAIT)

The front-end title menu's ADDITIVE 'light-trail'/backdrop layer (ALPHA 0x48, finding #609) is the scene model j_startback: an igSceneInfo in AREA.AFS entry 620 whose scene graph is igAttrSet BG02 (texture bind -> igImage sback00.png 128x64) over a tree of igTransform nodes (group9/group10/group11/group15, BASE05_1) each driving igGeometry meshes (pCubeShape8, polySurfaceShape101, pCylinderShape2/8, polySurfaceShape109, pCubeShape7) with igBlendFunctionAttr dst=ONE (additive). It is animated, not procedural: each igTransform has _transformInput -> igTransformSequence1_5 (6 translation keys igVec3fList + 6 rotation keys igQuaternionfList), and the model-controller sequence beModelCtrlData SQ_OPEN (ACTION OPEN = beAction2Data OPEN: beAction2JOINTSET 0..209 'OS' + beAction2WAITCHECK Joint) then JUMP SQ_WAIT (ACTION WAIT01 = JOINTSET 240..360 'LP') gates it, exactly the SEQ the title Lua starts with MODELCTRL j_startback GENERATE SQ_OPEN. CONSEQUENCE: the port can generate this layer natively (load the area 620 scene graph + evaluate the transform sequences + draw screen-space additive with sback00) instead of cycling 34 captured frames.

**Notes:** CONFIRMED identity. The captured additive layer's texture (TEX0 0x400714059d30b880) is RGB-identical to AREA.AFS:620 material BG02 = igImage sback00.png (scripts/pack_inline_tex.py --material BG02). In every capture the b2 (additive) draws use ONLY that TEX0, in colour (63,63,70,a); they form concentric rings of overlapping small quads at screen centre 320,224 (ui000: rings at r~50,68,109 with 128 triangles=64 quads each, plus outer clusters), and the draw count varies 384..864/frame (#609). Record chain walked offline with DW4_Decomp/scripts/{igb_inst,igb_ids,igb_pool}.py on AREA.AFS:620: igSceneInfo j_startback -> igAttrSet BG02 (texture bind 7009 -> igTextureAttr 7019 -> igImage sback00 6890) -> igSwitch -> beModelCtrlNode2 -> igAttrSets New Object/PromotedAttr -> igTransform group9/10/11/15 + BASE05_1 -> igGeometry pCubeShape8/polySurfaceShape101/pCylinderShape2,8/polySurfaceShape109/pCubeShape7; blend dst=ONE = additive. Animation: each igTransform._transformInput -> igTransformSequence1_5 (6 translation keys igVec3fList + 6 rotation keys igQuaternionfList); gated by beModelCtrlData SQ_OPEN (ACTION OPEN = beAction2Data OPEN: beAction2JOINTSET 0..209 'OS' + beAction2WAITCHECK Joint) then JUMP SQ_WAIT (ACTION WAIT01 = JOINTSET 240..360 'LP') -- the SEQ the title Lua starts (MODELCTRL j_startback GENERATE SQ_OPEN). STILL TO PROVE: that playing these transform sequences reproduces the captured rings (needs a port render).

**Evidence:** local/extracted/AREA.AFS entry 620

<a id="f611"></a>
### #611 · confirmed

**Where:** port/src/scene.c build_graph / dw4_scene_node_world; pack AREA.AFS entry 620

The port can load the front-end's (title/logo/char-select) scene packs the way it loads world stages: their igSceneInfo spells _sceneGraph as an igAttrSet node root, not an igGraphPathList. Teaching dw4_scene_build_graph to take the FIRST graph-typed field (igGraphPathList OR igAttrSet/igGroup/igNode) makes AREA.AFS:620 resolve 5 scenes / 917 nodes (j_startback alone 49), so j_startback's geometry + transform nodes are reachable. The remaining gap: node world-matrix composition is degenerate on these chains (a composed node reports rows 0 and 1 zeroed), so the igTransform igMatrix44f values or a non-matrix type-40 value on an ancestor are being mis-read.

**Notes:** RESOLVED. The placeholder is real and intentional: an animated igTransform writes a zero-row '\''_m'\'' and keeps the transform in its '\''_transformInput'\'' -- an igTransformSequence1_5 whose parallel igVec3fList translation/scale lists and igLongList nanosecond time list are the true keys. Verified: for title-pack node 7 (igTransform select, AREA.AFS 620) the evaluated sequence at t=0 reproduces the placeholder '\''_m'\'' exactly (scale key0 = (0,0,1), translation key0 = (0,-60,0), so zero 3x3 rows), and interpolating to t=1.0e9 gives scale 1.10 / y -74 and t=3.0e9 scale 0.90 / y -98 -- i.e. a pop-in then a slide. The port now finds the sequence by class (dw4_scene_* seq fields), evaluates it at dw4_scene_set_time(ns) and composes it in dw4_scene_node_world. No degenerate world matrices remain.

**Evidence:** port/src/scene.c (read_seq/seq_local); dw4asset scene --afs AREA.AFS --entry 620 --time T --chain polySurfaceShape37; the port

<a id="f612"></a>
### #612 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00518130

The title backdrop's additive light-trail layer is j_startback's geometry drawn in SCREEN space (a 2D setup, not the stage camera): per-frame it is ~2000 GS triangles of one 128x64 RGBA atlas (the captured 0x400714059d30b880 == material BG02/sback00), whose vertical-bar strip forms the ring dashes. The projected inner edge is a hard 39.5 px in every one of the 34 menu-anim captures, which equals the model inner ring polySurfaceShape101 (radius 120) at a model->pixel scale of 0.329; the scale the UI rows use is 0.42. The meshes are a tunnel+ring set in the pack (pCylinderShape3 spans z -3309..4.5 with 514 quads; rings pCylinderShape2/7/8 span radius 177-226 at z 15.6/30; circles polySurfaceShape101 r=120 and 104 r=134.5). dw4asset render --fe generates the layer from the pack (posed graph -> screen space -> GS UI primitive, additive) and reproduces the ring + bar-dash structure, but the exact per-mesh material UV sub-rect and the scale are not yet settled, so it is not 1:1.

**Notes:** REFUTED as a description of the layer's make-up: the additive light-trail layer is NOT ~2000 GS triangles of per-joint instances. The pack's own total for j_startback is 16 igGeometry meshes / 3184 triangles, drawn ONCE each, over 23 animated igTransform joints and 12 distinct sequences (independently cross-checked against the port's dw4asset scene --list: all 16 per-mesh triangle counts match). So there is no joint-instancing step to implement: the layer is those 16 meshes, animated by each node's own clock (time = ((int64)(_timeScale*(engineTime>>18)))<<18 + _timeBias, _timeBias set by the JOINTSET range: 0..209 frames mode OS, then 240..360 mode LP; findings #617-#620) and drawn additive in SCREEN space. What remains for a native generator is therefore the screen-space projection/scale (the open part of this finding) plus that per-node clock -- NOT a missing joint set. Evidence: scripts/modelctrl_dump.py on the pack data; docs/engine/action2-jointset.md. Folded into #626, the current answer for j_startback is a perspective 3D scene, not screen-space; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** Radial histogram of the captured menu layer's draws; the port render --fe; finding #610; port/src/uireplay.c + src/engine.c dw4_engine_boot + tools/verify_frontend.py run 3 (captured menu layer, finding #613)

<a id="f614"></a>
### #614 · confirmed

**Where:** front-end trail-layer generator (finding #610/#612 follow-up); port W1

The front-end trail layer's motion is a small SCRIPTED MODEL CONTROLLER carried in the pack (AREA.AFS 620), and its action records are time RANGES, not joint counts: beAction2JOINTSET is a 28-byte record with _startTime/_endTime/_loopMode (e.g. 0..209 mode 'OS', then 240..360 mode 'LP'), so finding #610's 'JOINTSET 0..209' means frames 0..209 of a joint animation, NOT 210 joints. Pack 620 carries the whole controller: 15 beModelCtrlData (sequences SQ_OPEN/SQ_WAIT/SQ_CANCEL...), 18 beAction2Data, 11 beAction2ACTION, 11 beAction2JOINTSET, 6 beAction2WAITCHECK, 6 beModelCtrlJUMP, 4 beModelCtrlNode2/InfoRamData/InfoDataSeq/Info, and beAction2Info 'j_startback' (_switch 6946, _animationDatabase -1, _dataList 5507). CONSEQUENCE for the generator: replaying it needs the engine's model-controller semantics (sequence -> action -> JOINTSET/JUMP/WAITCHECK playback, and where the per-joint curves live) recovered by decompilation -- the time ranges are only the schedule.

**Evidence:** dw4asset records/reflect on AREA.AFS entry 620 (beModelCtrlData/beAction2Info/beAction2JOINTSET)

<a id="f615"></a>
### #615 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00396a80 — Gap::Bec::beModelCtrl shared vtable slot 33 @0x00396a80 (8552 B) / slot 25 @0x00390de0

The model controller's RUNTIME code is the shared 'default' vtable slot 33 (0x396a80, 8552 bytes) and slot 25 (0x390de0, 152 bytes), shared by 12 vtables including Gap::Bec::beModelCtrl (and all beNDMWMdl* model classes) -- so the controller's per-frame work lives in one big method, and slot 25 is a small predicate/copy (it calls igObject's slot 25 when two object ids match). This is the entry point for the trail layer's joint-set playback (findings #610/#614): the pack's beModelCtrlData sequences (SQ_OPEN/SQ_WAIT) over beAction2*/beModelCtrlJUMP/WAITCHECK are executed here and turned into per-joint transforms. Next: decompose 0x396a80's action dispatch (the join-set timing, where the per-joint keyframes come from) -- the class inventory is already named (beModelCtrlData/DataList/InfoDataSeq/AIData/AIMap/ACTION/CTRL/HITBOX families), so the runtime function is the only missing link. Decompiled with decompile_funcs.py over the analysed project.

**Notes:** RESOLVED. slot 33 @0x396a80 is Gap::Bec::beModelCtrl::vfunc_33; beModelCtrl IS-A beAction2 (they share vtable slots 21-34; beAction2::vfunc_33 @0x377990 is the base). Both dispatch on a string-keyed msg id resolved by FUN_0038d4a0(registry,name): beModelCtrl uses the 33-entry MsgAction enum (table 0x4c58f0, FUN_00356ab0) and beAction2 uses a 7-entry query enum (table 0x4c81f0, FUN_0036dcd0). The controller's action playback for the trail layer is: GENERATE(msg0) starts the named beAction2Data; FUN_00377700 walks its components; JOINTSET start FUN_00376f40 / update FUN_00376de0 set and advance a per-action time node's _timeBias from beAction2JOINTSET _startTime/_endTime (60fps frames -> int64 via *1e9/60) with _loopMode[0]=='L' = loop, else one-shot clamp; the node's _time is recomputed each frame by FUN_00398e80 = ((int64)(_timeScale*(engineTime>>18)))<<18 + _timeBias and drives the node's igTransformSequence1_5 (_transformInput @igTransform+0x68) into igTransform._m (+0x20). Write-up: docs/engine/action2-jointset.md. New findings #617-#621.

**Evidence:** ghidra-post decompile_funcs.py on the analysed dw4 project

<a id="f616"></a>
### #616 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00518130 — dw4_scene build_graph / igTransform._transformInput -> igTransformSequence1_5 (finding #611 read_seq)

The per-joint keyframe transforms of the front-end title layer live in igTransformSequence1_5, reached from the scene node by igTransform._transformInput (ord 11, offset 0x68), and NOT in any beAction2*/beModelCtrl* record. For AREA.AFS entry 620 (magic 0x1dc4, 5914 objects, 8176 ids), igSceneInfo 'j_startback' (id 6891 @0x3d3eb, _sceneGraph=igAttrSet 'BG02' id 7050 @0x3e25f) has 49 nodes: 23 igTransform (every one with _transformInput set), 16 igGeometry, 1 beModelCtrlNode2, _transformInput refs -> 12 distinct igTransformSequence1_5 records. Pack 620 carries no igSkeleton/igSkin/igAnimation: the 'joints' ARE the 23 animated igTransform nodes. Each igTransformSequence1_5 holds _xlateList (igVec3fList) @0x08, _quatList (igQuaternionfList) @0x0c, _scaleList (igVec3fList) @0x10, _timeListLong (igLongList, int64 ns) @0x38, _startTime(-1.0) @0x18, _drivenChannels @0x48 (1=translate,3=tr+rot,8=scale,9=tr+scale), _animationDuration ~6.016e9 ns; the _interpolationData*/_compressedDataObject slots are NULL so values come from the base lists. Example id 835 @0xbb97: times [0,2.583e9,3.5e9,4.0e9,5.0e9,6.0e9], xlate [(0,0,0)x2,(0,0,800)x2,(0,0,1400),(0,0,2000)], quat [(0,0,0,1)x2,(0,0,-0.38,0.92)x2,(0,0,-0.71,0.71),(0,0,0.92,-0.38)].

**Notes:** Confirmed by the port: src/scene.c reads the igTransform._transformInput igTransformSequence1_5 lists (times/translation/scale/quaternion) and src/frontlayer.c evaluates them at each node's _time; the j_startback layer animates from them every frame.

Ids: 0 GENERATE,1 GENERATE_NDSP,2 REMOVE,3 GETINFONAME,4 GETINFONAME_RUN,5 SETMATRIX,6 CHKBULLET,7 SETLABEL,8 SETEFFECT,9 SETSOUND,10 GETMATRIX,11 GETMATRIX_ALL,12 GETMATRIX_ACTIVE,13 GETTRCDATA,14 GETHP,15 SETHP,16 GETRECNO,17 ACTION1,18 ONCTRL,19 OFCTRL,20 ONFREEZE,21 OFFREEZE,22 SETLODHIGH,23 RESETLODHIGH,24 MOVETRANS,25 SETCONVEYOR,26 SETQUICKSAND,27 SETSLIPPEDFLOOR,28 ISRUN,29 ISSEQNAME,30 ONHITBULLETBOX,31 OFHITBULLETBOX,32 GETSEQNAMELIST. Separate 7-entry enum for beAction2::vfunc_33 @0x377990 at table 0x4c81f0 (FUN_0036dcd0): 0 ISCOMEND,1 ISRUN,2 GETNAMELIST,3 GETAPPEARANCELIST,4 GETBONEMATRIX,5 ONFREEZE,6 OFFREEZE; default=start/stop (stop iff name=='NDSP').

<a id="f618"></a>
### #618 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00376f40 — beAction2JOINTSET_Start @0x00376f40; beAction2JOINTSET_Update @0x00376de0; beAction2_StartComponents @0x00377700; Gap::Bec::beAction2::vfunc_31 @0x003778d0

beAction2JOINTSET playback is a FRAME RANGE driving a per-action time node, not skeletal joints. Start FUN_00376f40 @0x376f40 (JOINTSET component): work=action+0x38 (beAction2InfoWork 0x589e98); work->_jointSt0(+0x1c) = 1 if _loopMode[0]=='L' else 2; work->_jointStartTimeL(+0x20) = (int64)(_startTime*1e9/60) and work->_jointEndTimeL(+0x28) = (int64)(_endTime*1e9/60) -- _startTime(off12)/_endTime(off16) are 60fps FRAME numbers, constants 0x4e6e6b28=1e9 and 0x42700000=60.0 verified by objdump; node=action+0x2c (the action time node, beModelCtrlNode2-like with _timeScale +0x78 f32, _timeBias +0x80 int64); node->_timeBias = startTime - ((int64)(node->_timeScale*(engineTime>>18))<<18) so node._time currently equals _startTime; work->_jointIsEnd(+0x30)=0. Per-frame update FUN_00376de0 @0x376de0: now=FUN_00398e80(node,engineTime); end=_jointEndTimeL; if end\<now then for mode 2 (OS) now=end (clamp one-shot) else for mode 1 (LP) now += (_jointStartTimeL - end) (wrap one duration = loop), looped until end>=now; if moved, node->_timeBias = now - ((int64)(node->_timeScale*(engineTime>>18))<<18) and work->_jointIsEnd=1. So there is no explicit cursor++: the cursor is node._time, advanced by the engine clock, and loop vs one-shot is implemented by rewriting _timeBias. Component dispatch: beAction2::vfunc_31 @0x3778d0 calls the JOINTSET update (FUN_00376de0) when work->_jointSt0!=0; the component walker FUN_00377700 @0x377700 starts it by comparing component class id (vfunc_0x58) against beAction2JOINTSET 0x589fc8 (also MOTIONSET 0x589fe8, WAITCHECK 0x589fa0, FADESET 0x589fb0, FLASHSET 0x589f98). Pack AREA.AFS:620 j_startback: OPEN=JOINTSET 0..209 'OS', WAIT01=JOINTSET 240..360 'LP'.

**Notes:** The complete component handler map is now committed in config/SLUS-20836/names/Action2.tsv: FLASHSET, WAITCHECK, FADESET, JOINTSET, and MOTIONSET each have a recovered Start/Update pair, followed by beAction2_StartComponents. The names are grounded in the component-class dispatch and beAction2InfoWork slots. A focused m2c pass over all 11 handlers and vfunc_31/vfunc_33 banks no functions; typed list/component wrappers and helper signatures are required before trustworthy matching C. Folded into #765, the current answer for beAction2JOINTSET Start/Update behaviour; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/action2-jointset.md; findings #610/#614; AREA.AFS entry 620

<a id="f619"></a>
### #619 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00398e80 — FUN_00398e80 @0x00398e80; FUN_00230110 @0x00230110; beModelCtrlNode2 (handle 0x588860)

Per-node animation clock. FUN_00398e80 @0x398e80 (disassembled) computes an animated node's current time: v0 = engineTime>>18 (engineTime is an int64 nanosecond accumulator with 18 fractional bits, read as *(int64*)(*(int32*)(obj+0xc)+0x50)); f = (float)v0 * node->_timeScale (node+0x78, f32); v0 = FUN_00230110(f) (float->int64, @0x230110); return (v0<<18) + node->_timeBias (node+0x80, int64). i.e. node._time = ((int64)(_timeScale*(engineTime>>18)))<<18 + _timeBias. beModelCtrlNode2 reflection: _time off 112 (0x70), _timeScale off 120 (0x78), _timeBias off 128 (0x80). A JOINTSET selects the [_startTime,_endTime] range by writing node._timeBias.

**Notes:** CLARIFICATION: engineTime and node._time are both int64 NANOSECOND values (the JOINTSET frame->time conversion frame*1e9/60 and _time are compared directly, frame 60 -> 1e9). The >>18... <<18 pair does not rescale the unit; it evaluates the _timeScale product at 2^18 ns (~262us) granularity. _timeScale (node+0x78) is the playback-rate multiplier, _timeBias (node+0x80) the range offset; beModelCtrlNode2._time at +0x70 is written from this result. Folded into #762, the current answer for Node clock FUN_00398e80 formula; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/action2-jointset.md

<a id="f623"></a>
### #623 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00518130 — j_startback scene graph mesh survey; dw4_mesh_tris / dw4asset scene --list

Joint/geometry count for the AREA.AFS 620 title layer 'j_startback': 23 joints (animated igTransform nodes) but only 16 drawn igGeometry meshes, totalling 3184 triangles, not ~2000. Per-mesh (strips, sum(len-2) from each igGeometry's igAttrList -> igGeometryAttr1_5._stripLengths, matching dw4asset scene --list): pCylinderShape3=1592 (tunnel), pCubeShape8=2, pCubeShape4=152, pCubeShape7=133, pCylinderShape4=4, pCubeShape2=28, polySurfaceShape98=45, pCylinderShape2=263, polySurfaceShape97=120, polySurfaceShape109=216, polySurfaceShape101=224, pCubeShape3=93, pCylinderShape8=130, pCylinderShape7=130, polySurfaceShape104=48, pCylinderShape6=4 => 3184. So the ~2000 captured triangles (#612/#613) cannot be 'N geometry meshes drawn once per joint': there are 23 joints / 16 meshes and the pack total is 3184; the per-frame capture is a subset of the whole menu frame (tunnel backdrop + logo + additive pass, whose draw/tri count changes per frame 384/850/808). The joint->node->one-geometry structure is right but the arithmetic is 16 meshes / 23 nodes / 3184 tris; reconciling ~2000 needs the runtime draw/echo semantics, not a joint multiple.

**Notes:** Confirmed by the port render (dw4asset scene --list and src/frontlayer.c): 16 igGeometry meshes drawn once each, 3184 triangles total, over 23 animated igTransform nodes.

**Evidence:** scripts/modelctrl_dump.py

<a id="f626"></a>
### #626 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00518130 — front-end title (beNDMWTitle2 / AREA.AFS 620 j_startback)

The front-end title layer j_startback (AREA.AFS 620) is a 3D scene rendered in PERSPECTIVE down its axis, not a screen-space 2D layer. Its 16 igGeometry meshes form a tunnel (pCylinderShape3 radius ~500 spanning z +4.5..-3309, plus rings at z~15/-103/-1600/-2128); an orthographic/screen-space read collapses them onto one ring (#612), while a perspective projection makes the wall fill the frame and the animated node translations along z drive the expanding trail rings. The port generates it (port/src/frontlayer.c) at 60 fps.

**Notes:** Confirmed by the port render: with focal~380 and camera z~250 (640x448 space) the graph draws the game's tunnel (16 meshes/3184 tris) and animates every frame; a capture/analysis render plus port title renders at f000 and f199. The per-node clock is #619 and the JOINTSET schedule #614/#618/#621. Topic head for j_startback is a perspective 3D scene, not screen-space: also read #612 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c

### #627 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00518130 — igImage/igClut (AREA.AFS 620 title pack)

sback00.png in AREA.AFS 620 decodes from the pack: the igImage's _pImage (ordinal 13) is pool block 6975, a 128x64 byte-per-pixel (8bpp) grid (_imageSize 8192), and its _pClut (ordinal 17) is igClut object 3263 whose _pData (ordinal 5) is pool block 722, a 256-entry palette with RGBA in the four entry bytes. Decoding grid[i] through palette 722 reproduces the game's own uploaded sback00 texture pixel-for-pixel.

**Notes:** The _pClut object is at +0x21e2b; verify with dw4asset records --class igImage (slot 13 = 6975, slot 17 = 3263) and si the port decode vs a live livetex copy. Layout is RGBA (not the texpack reader's bytes 1..3 rule).

<a id="f628"></a>
### #628 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00379c40 — beCameraCtrl (vtable 0x53a210) / beNDMWTitle2; NDMW.AFS 806 'entry'

The front-end title's camera is the beCameraCtrlInfo/igSceneInfo named 'entry' in the title-screen pack NDMW.AFS entry 806, selected by the title script's SETDEMOCAMERA "entry". Its _data (id 3019) is a beCameraDemoData whose _cameraPath (id 2997) is an igGraphPath, and the 'entry' igSceneInfo's _sceneGraph is an igSwitch BG01 whose chain BG01 -> animCamera01_60 -> cameraanim_GP1 (+600) -> cameraanim_group01 -> cameraanim01 (+130) -> igCamera(cameraanimShape01) composes to an ON-AXIS camera at world z=730 with an identity rotation. It is a demo camera over _totalSec=1 (_endTime 1e9 ns); the layer is drawn head-on through it with the engine's 39.60-degree projection (focal 622 at 448 lines, finding #496).

**Notes:** The runtime: beCameraCtrl::vfunc_31 (0x379c40) dispatches to the box path FUN_00378ed0 or the demo path FUN_00379a90; the box path reads beCameraBoxData._eyePosList (+0x14)/_intPosList (+0x18) into _eyeMatrix (+0x54)/_intMatrix (+0x94). Port use: port/src/frontlayer.c draws j_startback through this camera (axis + engine projection recovered; the 1s dolly's menu-time z is calibrated because the path animation is not decoded). Folded into #630, the current answer for Title screen 'entry' demo camera; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** the port; port render z350 vs a retail capture of the main menu (Game Start)

### #630 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001c59f0 — beCameraMode::vfunc_28 @0x37a900; FUN_001c5b30 @0x1c5b30; FUN_001c59f0 @0x1c59f0; demo setup FUN_00379a90 @0x379a90 / FUN_0037a690 @0x37a690

The title's beCameraDemoData (igGraphPath 2997, beCameraCtrlInfo/igSceneInfo 'entry' in NDMW.AFS 806) is NOT a 1-second spatial dolly: its four igTransform chain nodes (animCamera01_60, cameraanim_GP1 +600, cameraanim_group01, cameraanim01 +130) all carry _transformInput = NULL and a static _m, so beCameraMode::vfunc_28's per-frame FUN_001c5b30(path,m,_nowTimeL) -> FUN_001c59f0 composes the SAME matrix at every time. beCameraDemoData._endTime/_totalSec=1 only drives _nowTimeL to _timeLength and latches _isEnd. <a id="f631"></a>
### #631 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00518130 — AREA.AFS 620 j_startback scene; igGeometry/igAttrList/igGeometryAttr1_5

The j_startback layer's per-draw material is NOT in the pack: every one of its 16 igGeometry nodes (AREA.AFS 620) carries an igAttrList whose single member is an igGeometryAttr1_5 (vertex array only) -- no igColorAttr/igMaterialAttr/igTextureBindAttr/igBlendFunctionAttr is reachable. The blend and shade are the front-end's engine-side draw state; only the texture (sback00.png igImage+igClut) is pack data.

**Notes:** Recovered per-draw state from the game's own GS dump (title menu frame): the sback00 draws (TEX0 0x400714059d30b880 TBP0 0x388000) all use PRIM ABE=1 IIP=1 with ALPHA 0x48 = A=Cs B=0 C=As D=Cd -> Cs*As+Cd (ADDITIVE); the pixel adds the pack texture's own alpha (the sback00 igClut is ~36% alpha-0 texels). The VU1 shade is a neutral ~(72,72,77,127). So the port's additive blend + texture-alpha gating were already correct; the only stand-in was the shade constant.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c; GS dump of the title menu frame

<a id="f632"></a>
### #632 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00518130 — Gap::Sg::igScene/igModelCtrl (AREA.AFS 620); beNDMWTitle2::vfunc_31

The title/menu 2D UI (logo, copyright, rows) is a pack scene, not an engine overlay: AREA.AFS 620 draws it from three igModelCtrl graphs in the same scene as j_startback. j_startlogo holds an igAttrSet(W N01) with two igTransform children -- 'rogo' at world y=+73 (its igGeometry child is mesh polySurfaceShape2: a quad x [-294.4,294.4], y [-129.95,164.45], z=400, full UV, textured logo.png) and 'kenri' at world y=-170 (polySurfaceShape4: x [-155,155], y [-48.5,27.5], z=400, UV u 0..1 v 0..0.4, textured mojips.png = the copyright block). j_startmenu01 adds a 'start' row (polySurfaceShape5, x [-160,160], z=400, UV v 0.758..0.984) plus two line meshes (polySurfaceShape35/36 sampled from cursor.png); j_startmenu02 adds 'menu' (polySurfaceShape5, z=600, UV v 0.516..0.75) and 'null1' (polySurfaceShape34, x [-94,94], z=600) plus two cursor.png lines. The engine draws each geometry's 4 UV'd vertices in SCREEN SPACE: 1 model unit = 1 pixel in a 640x448 space, +y up, origin at the framebuffer centre, z as painter depth (larger z nearer). Verified: the logo quad maps to x 41..416 in both the game's own menu framebuffer and the port's render, and the copyright quad to x 165..475 / y 367..432.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontui.c

<a id="f633"></a>
### #633 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00518130 — igAttrSet/igTextureBindAttr/igTextureAttr/igImage/igClut (AREA.AFS 620)

The title UI geometries' textures resolve through the pack's own material chain, so the port needs no captured texture. Per materialised record: igAttrSet._attributes is ORDINAL 8 (not the reflection field index) -> an igAttrList; igAttrList._count is the word at +8 and _array a block handle at +0x10 (its contents are still object ids); among the array members the igTextureBindAttr._texture is ORDINAL 4 -> an igTextureAttr; its _image is ORDINAL 12 -> an igImage. igImage fields: _px ord 2, _py ord 3, _imageSize ord 12, _pImage ord 13 (pool block -> pool block), _pClut ord 17 (igClut object), _bytesPerRow ord 19, _pNameString ord 22; igClut._pData ord 5. Decoding grid[k] through the _pClut palette RGBA reproduces logo.png (512x256) and mojips.png (256x128) exactly. NOTE the ordinal != reflection-list-index: the class's own descriptor slots are read from the pack's class triples (a first attempt using field-index+2 returned 0xFFFFFFFF).

**Evidence:** port/src/scene.c (dw4_scene_node_texture); scripts/pack_inline_tex.py

<a id="f634"></a>
### #634 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001dd080 — igTransformSequence1_5 (AREA.AFS 620 title UI nodes)

Every node of the title UI graphs carries an igTransformSequence1_5 pop-in: scale starts at (0,0,1) (invisible), rises to (1,1,1) and holds, then some pop out again. j_startlogo rogo/kenri keys: (0,0,0,1)->(0.25,1,1,1)->(0.5,1,1,1)->(0.667,1,1,1)->(0.833,0,0,1). The igTransform's stored _m is therefore a placeholder (x/y rows zero) and the visible resting pose is the plane where the sequence scale is (1,1,1) -- the port evaluates the timeline at t=0.4 s to get the resting menu layout. The graph's node class is igAttrSet/igGeometry/igTransform and the material sets are reachable only through the igAttrSet ancestor, not the geometry itself.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontui.c (dw4_frontui_open sets 4.0e8)

The title controller's own message/target table names the UI graphs, which is how beNDMWTitle2 drives them (the rendering path for the recovered UI tree, finding #632). In game.elf's.data at 0x518300..0x518420, the same table that carries the load-controller tokens (GAMERAM/GETCOMMON/CAMERAMODE/LIGHTMODE/CTRL/COMMDLCTRL/TEXTORDER) continues with 'j_startlogo', 'SQ_OPEN', 'j_startmenu01', 'MODELCTRL', 'SQ_LINESL00', 'ISSEQNAME', 'i_fadewn01', 'GENERATE', 'SQ_BLOUT30', 'ISRUN'. So the title runs MODELCTRL j_startlogo GENERATE SQ_OPEN (the logo/copyright pop-in) and j_startmenu01 with SQ_LINESL00/ISSEQNAME/ISRUN plus the i_fadewn01 fade node, then SQ_BLOUT30 -- i.e. the graphs are addressed by name and each is played with a sequence command, the same GENERATE/SQ_* mechanism the port plays for j_startback (finding #618). 'TEXTORDER'/'COMMDLCTRL' nearby are the row/text ordering and command-controller tokens.

<a id="f643"></a>
### #643 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0013e6a0 — Gap::Core::igGeometryMaskAttr::vfunc_26; tools/m2c_batch.py virtualize_calls

The four-byte struct-returning C++ virtual-call shape is recovered mechanically. m2c presents it as a discarded C out-call whose first argument is the address of an int stack local; in one repeated form it also retains the C ABI explicit object immediately after that buffer. Re-typing the local as a one-word class, assigning the real virtual return, reading its w0 member, and removing only an exact duplicate object argument makes MWCC use the hidden return buffer in a0 and shift this to a1, reproducing retail byte-identically.

**Notes:** Current measured population: 148 unmatched functions contained lw t9,0(a1). The initial targeted sweep banked one and left 17 candidates within three instructions. Classifying all 17 found 15 copies of vX(&out, object,...), where object redundantly restated the C ABI this argument after the hidden buffer; the narrowed transform banks all 15, taking 9,196 to 9,211. The two residuals are separate mechanisms: Gap::Core::igAlphaFunctionAttr::vfunc_26 needs a nested float-returning virtual signature (f0 passed onward in f12), and FUN_0013c350 differs only in scheduling around its final virtual call. The four-byte struct-return class is now exhausted.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py; src/engine/Gap__Core__igGeometryMaskAttr__vfunc_26.cpp; src/engine/Gap__Core__igPsx2ThreadManager__vfunc_11.cpp; docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f646"></a>
### #646 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00376f40 — beAction2JOINTSET_Start/Update (0x00376f40 / 0x00376de0)

The beAction2JOINTSET pair's remaining 'compiler spelling' blocker is the MWCC optimization SUB-FLAG 'p' (peephole), not a source idiom. With -O3 the compiler lowers the integral float literal 1e9f to an integer materialise + cvt.s.w; with ANY optimization level carrying ',p' (-O2,p/-O3,p/-O4,p) it instead loads the IEEE-754 bits 0x4e6e6b28 directly with lui/ori/mtc1, exactly as retail does at 0x376f90. The flag also has no source-level equivalent: #pragma peephole/opt_peephole/optimization_level are all ignored. Therefore the retail ELF was built with PER-FILE optimization flags, and the repo's single global flag set is an approximation.

**Notes:** Discriminating evidence both ways: FUN_0015e130 (popcount, src/unmatched/FUN_0015e130.c) matches retail ONLY without ',p' (byte-exact at -O3; 48 vs 40 bytes at -O3,p and -O4,p), while beAction2JOINTSET_Start requires ',p' for its 1e9f constant. Isolated by compiling a one-line probe (float f(float x){return 1e9f*x;}) across -O2/-O2,p/-O3/-O3,p/-O4/-O4,p/-O3,s/-O4,s: every ',p' variant emits 3c024e6e 34436b28 44820000; every non-p variant emits 3c023b9a 3442ca00 44820000 + 46800020 (cvt.s.w). Consequence for the repo: none of the beAction2 component handlers can be banked under the current single-flag verify path; a per-unit flag override (config/SLUS-20836/unit-flags.tsv) is the prerequisite. With -O3,p the Start draft is reduced to 316/324 bytes (only a missing scheduler nop in the state branch and one in the first conversion remain); Update is 332B retail vs 308B.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/action2-jointset.md; src/unmatched/FUN_0015e130.c (comment + verify); tools/verify.py --flags=-O3,p

<a id="f647"></a>
### #647 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00376f40 — beAction2JOINTSET_Start (0x00376f40)

The two source idioms the JOINTSET handlers need, both now pinned against retail: (1) the node-clock conversion is '(float)(unsigned int)(system->_clock->_time >> 18)' -- a u32->float conversion, which MWCC lowers to the dsll32/dsra32 + bltz + (x>>1|x&1)*2 sequence retail uses, not an int/float cast; (2) the register allocation (work->_jointSt0 in s1, params in s0, node reusing s0) only appears when the bias is written as 'long long st = work->_jointStartTimeL; node->_timeBias = st - (FUN_00230110(conv * node->_timeScale) << 18);' -- the long-long temp for the start time is what MWCC's allocator keys on. With both, Start matches retail instructions 0..16 under -O3,p (316/324 B).

**Notes:** The literal spelling 1e9f vs 1000000000.0f is irrelevant; both fold identically. The remaining 8 bytes are two pipeline nops (one after the state-branch delay slot, one after mtc1 of 60.0f in the first conversion) that no tested source shape produced.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/action2-jointset.md

<a id="f648"></a>
### #648 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00376f40 — beAction2JOINTSET_Start/Update (0x00376f40 / 0x00376de0); repo verify/link tooling

The repo had a single global compiler-flag set, but the retail ELF was built with per-file optimisation flags (finding #646), so units needing a different flag could not be banked. Per-unit flag overrides are now implemented: config/SLUS-20836/unit-flags.tsv (a committed, hand-authored overlay keyed by function VA, like config/SLUS-20836/names/*.tsv), read by tools/unitflags.py and consulted from BOTH tools/verify.py and tools/linkgen.py so the verify and relink paths cannot drift. In verify.py the override wins over --flags; --unit-flags PATH (or /dev/null) disables the table. The table carries the motivating beAction2JOINTSET entries (0x00376f40/0x00376de0 -> '-O3,p -Iinclude'). Verified: verify.py 0x376f40 \<src> --flags='-O3 -Iinclude' now reports 'flags -O3,p -Iinclude' and compiles at 316/324 B where -O3 alone gave 360 B.

**Notes:** The flag is NOT sufficient to bank the pair. With -O3,p beAction2JOINTSET_Start is 316/324 B with instructions 0..16 matching; its residual is two scheduler nops (after the state branch's delay slot, and after the mtc1 of 60.0f in the first conversion) PLUS a differing float register allocation (retail: 1e9f->f1, loaded time->f2, 60.0f->f0; no tested spelling, operand order / integer-cast constant / explicit float temp, or -O2..-O4 level reproduces it). beAction2JOINTSET_Update is ~320/332 B with the state-guard compare order and work's s-register differing. A two-flag sweep (build/flag_sweep.py) of all 2,954 near-miss drafts in build/m2c under -O3 and -O3,p found NO,p-only match (the 130 matches match under both and are already banked), so the capability is forward-looking. make check 9214/19277 0 mismatching, make link byte-identical (the override is inert until a banked unit needs it).

**Evidence:** config/SLUS-20836/unit-flags.tsv; tools/unitflags.py; tools/verify.py; tools/linkgen.py; docs/engine/action2-jointset.md; build/flag_sweep.py

<a id="f649"></a>
### #649 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00242560 — wrapper family 0x00242560..0x00243b00 + 0x002500a0/0x00252f70/0x0029ed48/0x002ad140/0x0015b1a0/0x0015b1b0/0x001f8830/0x001f88d0/0x001d9bb0

A recurring 'pass-through first argument' wrapper idiom: the wrapper forwards its OWN incoming a0 as the callee's FIRST argument and passes a literal as the SECOND. Retail materialises the literal with 'li a1,N' (a1), while m2c drops the forwarded a0 (which is never written at the call site) and recovers a one-argument signature, emitting 'li a0,N' — a one-register miss. The fix is to give the wrapper an unused first parameter and forward it: e.g. int FUN_00242560(int self){ FUN_001005f0(FUN_00247290(self, 1)); return 0; }.

**Notes:** Banked with this idiom (+15): FUN_00242560, 00242790, 002429c0, 00242c00, 00242e40, 00243080, 002432c0, 00243500, 00243740, 00243890, 00243ad0, 002500a0, 00252f70, 002ad140, 0029ed48. Related one-register signature/stride misses also banked (+5): FUN_0015b1a0/0015b1b0 (the value is the THIRD parameter, in a2, so the middle a1 argument is unused — give it a dummy param), FUN_001f8830/001f88d0 (m2c indexed a registry pointer by arg0*4 ints = x16 bytes; retail strides x4, so index the int array directly: &DAT_0056af40[arg0]), FUN_001d9bb0 (two-argument tail call whose second arg is *(int*)((char*)*(int*)((char*)a0+8)+12)). Advice for the m2c loop / signature recovery: a call whose first argument register is read-only pass-through (never written since entry) is a candidate forwarded parameter. Folded into #652, the current answer for m2c drops unread leading or gap arguments; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** src/unmatched/FUN_0024{2560,2790,29c0,2c00,2e40,3080,32c0,3500,3740,3890,3ad0}.c, FUN_0025{00a0,2f70}.c, FUN_002ad140.c, FUN_0029ed48.c, FUN_0015b1a0.c, FUN_0015b1b0.c, FUN_001f8830.c, FUN_001f88d0.c, FUN_001d9bb0.c; make check 9233/19277; make link BYTE-IDENTICAL

<a id="f650"></a>
### #650 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x00109368 — copy/compare wrappers 0x00109368/0x00109378/0x00250810/0x002979c8/0x002b50c8

A wrapper that copies or tests a word and whose retail code keeps the value in v0 is declared to RETURN that value, not void. A void spelling allocates v1 and misses; the int-return form lands the load in v0.

**Notes:** Spellings: (a) 'int f(...) { return *(T*)(dst) = *(T*)(src); }' for the 3-instruction copy wrappers (load+store+return in v0); (b) early-return shape 'if (*(int*)(arg0+8)==arg1) { call; return 1; } return 0;' keeps the compare load in v0 (retail 'bne v0,a1') vs v1 for if/else; (c) FUN_002b50c8 returns the pointer it stored through. Also banked: 0x0010a3d8/a3e8/a3f8/a408 tail-call FUN_00109b10 forwarding their own a0 as the callee's first arg and &DAT_004d23xx as the second (retail 'lui a1,0x4d'); FUN_00149210 needs the (unsigned int) cast so the u16 shift is srlv not srav.
normalised no evidence recorded

<a id="f651"></a>
### #651 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x0015ad30 — argument-dropping tail forwarders: 0x0015ad30, 0x0016cdd0, 0x002252a0, Gap::Lua::igLuaState::vfunc_49/50, Gap::Core::igPsx2CallStackTracer::vfunc_38

A wrapper whose retail body starts by shifting the argument registers (move a0,a1; move a1,a2;...) and then tail-jumps drops its own FIRST argument and forwards the rest; m2c recovers a signature one parameter too short.

**Notes:** Fix: declare an extra leading unused parameter and forward the rest — FUN_0016cdd0(int d,int a,int b,int c){FUN_001f7db0(a,b,c);}, FUN_0015ad30 forwards 7 of 8, and the 12-byte form 'move a0,a1; j T; move a1,a2' is FUN_002252a0/vfunc_49/vfunc_50/CallStackTracer::vfunc_38 = void f(int unused,int a,int b){T(a,b);}. The C++ vfuncs use the repo's FLAT extern-C symbol name (Gap__...__vfunc_N) and must be compiled in C mode (a.c file) — C++ mode frames the call (32B instead of the 12B tail jump). Folded into #652, the current answer for m2c drops unread leading or gap arguments; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.
normalised no evidence recorded

<a id="f652"></a>
### #652 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0038e5f0 — argument-restoration family: T3's four sd getters 0x0038e5f0/0x0038e630/0x0038e670; 0x00250c20, 0x001d8470, 0x00149030, 0x0015d050, 0x00167330, 0x001673c0, 0x0016e3a0; the 0x00300xxx cluster 0x00300d70/db0/e00/e50/ea0/f00/f50; 0x00338d60/da0/df0; 0x00378e40/8e80; 0x00447c10/60/cb0; 0x0038eca0; 0x0032f860; Gap__Core__igPrimLengthArray1_1__vfunc_24; Gap__Sg__igPlanarShadowShader__vfunc_39

m2c names a recovered parameter argN by its EE register index and silently drops any argument the retail body never reads — a leading one (retail first touches a1) or a gap (a2 while a1 is unused). MWCC then passes the recovered parameter in the wrong register, so the wrapper misses by one or two instructions. Restoring each missing slot as an unused dummy parameter — so the recovered ones occupy their own register — makes the wrapper match byte-for-byte.

**Notes:** Mechanical fix: tools/restore_dropped_args.py reads a build/m2c draft, rebuilds each parameter list as slots 0..maxIndex (keeping the recovered argN, filling holes with 'int dummyN'), and re-verifies; a.cpp is chosen when the draft uses C++ syntax (the virtual-call transforms emit 'class '/extern "C"'). It banked 22 functions across the =1/=2/=3-diff near-miss tiers (=1: 11/14, =2: 9/174, =3: 2/412). Also banked this pass, by hand: the 64-bit load FUN_0038f480 needs a (long long *) cast on the final load (retail 'ld', m2c 'lw'); FUN_0029cb48/FUN_0032cf50 are argument-preserving wrappers (declare the callee's params and forward all of them — 2 and 6 args respectively). The three sd getters call a first callee with ALL of their own arguments (m2c drops the whole callee arg list when the callee has no prototype) then a second callee with (a0, aN). Topic head for m2c drops unread leading or gap arguments: also read #649, #651 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

The beAction2 component block's two dispatch functions are recovered and banked byte-identical. beAction2_StartComponents (456 B) is the per-object Start driver: it walks the beAction2Data component-list (an igObjectList-shaped {count,capacity,data} struct at _comList) and calls each component's virtual GetMetaObject to route it to one of the five *_Start handlers, advancing _comIndex and stopping at the first handler that returns false. Gap::Bec::beAction2::vfunc_31 (192 B) is the per-frame Update driver: after the info's +0x14 gate and the work's +0x4c gate it invokes each active component's *_Update handler in type order (MOTIONSET, JOINTSET, FADESET, WAITCHECK, FLASHSET), keyed on the work's +0x14/+0x1c/+0x34/+0x44/+0x48 'started' slots.

**Notes:** What made StartComponents match was recovering the ORIGINAL pointer-typed list layout (count at +8, capacity at +0xc, _data at +0x10) and expressing the dispatch as a real C++ virtual call (component->GetMetaObject), not an offset cast: the untyped m2c draft would not allocate the registers retail does. Two scheduler nops (after the FLASHSET branch and at the loop exit) are emitted with explicit asm{nop}. vfunc_31 is a straight guarded-call chain; its only non-obvious feature is the four post-call scheduler nops retail emits after the delay slot of each *_Update jal (again explicit asm{nop}). Both verified by tools/verify.py with no per-unit flag needed for vfunc_31; StartComponents needs the -O3,p override already in config/SLUS-20836/unit-flags.tsv. make check 9,283/19,277 (48.2%) 0 mismatching.

<a id="f659"></a>
### #659 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001307f0 — igVertexBlendStateAttr_GetVTable

The plain *_GetVTable stack-offset fold is an MWCC compiler wall, not a source spelling: the frame temporary's offset is folded into the final lw displacement only on the scalar value-subscript path, and that path ties the load width to the element size (a 4-byte load forces a scale-4 sll), so a scale-1 word fold is unrepresentable in C.

**Notes:** BYTE-FIRST GENERATOR EXHAUSTED. Retail (0x1307f0) ends 'lw v0,916(v0); addu v0,v0,sp; lw v0,16(v0)'; every candidate compiles to 'addu v0,v0,sp; addiu v0,v0,16; lw v0,0(v0)' (11/15 insns byte-identical, 60 vs 64 B, 5 identical relocs, verify.py). The residual is exactly the frame temporary's offset D folded into the load (D = the temporary's frame slot: 16 for -32 frames, 24 for the 8-byte igObject variant, 32/48/... for larger variants; all 983 *_GetVTable share it).

RECOVERED ADDRESSING RULE (probe-proven; python3 scripts/mwcc_addr_probe.py; MWCC 3.0-011126/2.4/2.3.3 identical): (a) a frame symbol's offset folds into the load displacement ONLY on the scalar *value-subscript* path ('char u[16]; u[i]' -> 'addu v0,i,sp; lb v0,16(v0)'; 'int u[4]; u[i]' -> 'sll v0,i,2; addu v0,v0,sp; lw v0,16(v0)'); (b) on that path the load width AND the index scale both follow sizeof(element), so a word load is inseparable from a scale-4 sll -- no sizeof==1 type loads 4 B (packed union / '-enum min' enum array / size-1 struct all emit lb/lbu), so a scale-1 word fold does not exist in C; (c) the address path (&arr[i], arr+i, *(T*)((char*)arr+i)) never folds: 'addu v0,i,sp; addiu v0,v0,D; lw v0,0(v0)'; (d) a trailing constant folds only with a *register* base (parameter/loaded pointer/__builtin_frame_address/alloca/VLA), never with sp -- and this function has no parameter (a0 is the temporary's address). Flags are not the lever: -O1..-O4,,p/,s, -enum min, C and C++ all give the addiu form.

SEARCH: ~9,000 generated form-settings (scratch getvtable_gen{,2}.py, getvtable_wide.py, typed_member_sweep.py); the single full gen2 run was 7,200 forms across 6 flag sets with ZERO fold-hits, plus earlier focused sweeps -- over aggregate/type layout (char/short/int/void*/long long/float/double/struct/union/packed/enum-min arrays len 1-16; 4/8/16-byte igObject-shaped structs), stack-header construction, index/address expressions (pointer arith, subscript, address-of, int/char/unsigned/short casts, *1/+0/<<0/>>2//4, reversed addend order, reassociation, +K-K), declaration/temporary ordering, aliasing/qualification (volatile/const/register, inlined helpers, macros, __builtin_frame_address, alloca/VLA) and compiler modes -- ZERO fold-hits. decomp-permuter searched ~1800 candidates from the closest seed, floor 100, no 0. No matched oracle: all 4 non-GetVTable fold sites are themselves unmatched. Draft parked fail-closed; see docs/engine/getvtable-fold.md.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/getvtable-fold.md

**Where:** 0x001b26f0 — M7 C++ virtual-call sweep: virtualize_calls emits `((DW4VC_K_N *)obj)->vX(args)` and strips the explicit first argument when it *is* the object, but m2c spells the same object two ways — the vtable base as `((char *)(arg1) + 0)` and the explicit argument as the bare `arg1` — so a textual equality missed the strip and the call carried one extra argument, where retail has no second `move a1,a0`. <a id="f661"></a>
### #661 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002d0990 — M7 near-miss sweep: the ig*MemoryPool vfunc_46/vfunc_49 pairs (igAlias/igArena/igMalloc/igPage) read two fields with `ld` and return their `dsubu`; the igStack pair reads two `lwu` fields, `dsubu`s them and clamps a non-positive result to zero. m2c drafts them as `int` subtraction, and widening only the loads (the existing `width` shape) still emits `lw`/`subu` because the 32-bit `int` result truncates. Declaring the result `long long` reproduces retail exactly (`long long f(void*a){ return *(long long*)((char*)a+A) - *(long long*)((char*)a+B); }`; the igStack pair adds `if (r > 0) return r; return 0;`).

The 64-bit arithmetic class: a function whose retail body subtracts two 64-bit fields (ld/lwu + dsubu) needs the arithmetic AND return type widened to `long long`, not only the load widths — the `width` shape alone cannot close it (10 banked)

**Notes:** Banked 10 units (8 plain `ld`/`dsubu`, 2 `lwu`/`dsubu`-clamped), all verified byte-identical. The class is bounded: a full scan of the unmatched set for non-MMI 64-bit arithmetic found 81 candidates but only these 10 are the clean two-load subtract; the rest are heterogeneous (bit tricks, MMI-heavy, tail calls). A related one-instruction wall: FUN_00100290 needs `dsll32; dsrl32` (logical zero-extend) where MWCC emits `dsll32; dsra32` for every tested `(unsigned int)` spelling of a `long long` argument.

**Evidence:** tools/verify.py; src/unmatched/Gap__Core__ig*MemoryPool__vfunc_4[69].c; build/near3_fresh.tsv (x8 ld/dsubu cluster)

<a id="f663"></a>
### #663 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001eb310 — M7 near-miss sweep (manual source-shape pass over the <=3-diff tier)

Residual near-miss diffs are a recurring set of source spellings: a member load/store whose width must follow the retail instruction (lbu/sb/ld/sd vs the defaulted lw/sw), an array stride expressed as byte-offset (typed element array instead of (char*)base + i*N), a dropped leading call/function parameter, and an address materialisation that must be spelled as &symbol. 14 units banked byte-identical.

**Notes:** Worklist: build/near_scan.jsonl (2,180 unmatched drafts; 649 compile-clean mismatches, 203 at <=3 instructions). All 22 banked units re-verified with tools/verify.py, then make check 9,367/19,277 byte-for-byte and make link BYTE-IDENTICAL.
(1) member width / 64-bit access - the retail instruction is the authority. igDefaultAspectElement_InitFields 0x00451d40 and igSearchSceneGraph_InitFields 0x00238f70 store a byte (sb) where the draft wrote a word (sw); Gap::Core::igTransformSequence1_5::vfunc_55 0x001e2a80 stores a 64-bit field (sd) and vfunc_56 0x001abab0 loads one (ld) where the draft used int. Casting the access to unsigned char / long long reproduces retail.
(2) element stride - m2c writes (int*)DAT_x + (i * N); because DAT_x is int*, pointer arithmetic scales by 4 again, so the emitted shift is 4x too large. Expressing the base as a typed element array (&DAT_x[0] + i for a 4-byte element; a long long or struct array for 8 or 64-byte elements) fixes the shift. 6 units.
(3) dropped parameter - a call whose first argument register is read-only pass-through since entry is a forwarded parameter m2c dropped: FUN_001af7b0 0x001af7b0 (callee needs a1 forwarded and zero in a2), FUN_0024c710 0x0024c710 (leading arg), FUN_0030b0c0 0x0030b0c0 (two forwarded args). Adding the unused leading parameter(s) reproduces retail.
(4) address materialisation - a fallback returning an integer constant materialises as lui+ori, but retail uses lui+addiu because it is an address: Gap::Core::igMemoryPoolContextInfo::vfunc_54 0x002f2990 returns (int)&DAT_004f7910[0] (retail lui+addiu).
Parked as measured walls (still open): a narrowing load/store where MWCC folds a truncating store into a narrow load or re-selects the saved base register (FUN_00141840 0x00141840, FUN_0013cb00 0x0013cb00); a loop whose retail branch-delay slot is a nop but ours fills it with the next iteration's shift (FUN_001788a0, FUN_00204e30, FUN_001f8790); a load that must be ld while the compare stays 32-bit (FUN_001f0500, FUN_001f0710); an absolute literal load that MWCC emits via $at (FUN_0010ef30); and the switch jump-table case-0 target order (FUN_001b1cf0/1dc0).
(5) further dropped leading parameters (10 units) - the same read-only pass-through: FUN_001236e8 0x001236e8 and FUN_00248720 0x00248720 (a call whose a0 is the enclosing function's unused entry argument, so the recovered signature must gain one parameter and the call forwards it), FUN_002575f0 0x002575f0 / FUN_00257630 0x00257630 (the same site twice, differing only in the stored short), FUN_00307660 0x00307660 / FUN_00307700 0x00307700 (three leading forwarded args plus a constant 4th), and FUN_002f87f0 0x002f87f0 (the second call takes no argument after all - retail leaves a0 alone where the draft moved zero into it).
(6) a value pinned to v0 - FUN_00269630 0x00269630 stores a byte and retail materialises the 1 in v0, not v1, so the function returns it (return 1;) rather than being void.

**Evidence:** src/unmatched/Gap__Core__igTransformSequence1_5__vfunc_55.c, src/unmatched/Gap__Core__igTransformSequence1_5__vfunc_56.c, src/unmatched/igDefaultAspectElement_InitFields.c, src/unmatched/igSearchSceneGraph_InitFields.c, src/unmatched/igShaderFactory_InitFields.c, src/unmatched/FUN_001eb310.c, asm/lua_settag.s, src/third_party/lua/lib/lbaselib.c, src/engine/igMemoryPoolContextInfo_InitializeRecordNames.c, src/unmatched/FUN_00174db0.c, src/unmatched/FUN_001af7b0.c, asm/luaD_callTM.s, src/engine/string-field-values.c, src/unmatched/Gap__Core__igMemoryPoolContextInfo__vfunc_54.c; build/near_scan.jsonl, build/classify1.log, asm/luaV_Cclosure.s, asm/luaV_Lclosure.s, src/unmatched/FUN_00307660.c, src/unmatched/FUN_00307700.c, src/unmatched/FUN_00269630.c, src/unmatched/FUN_001236e8.c, src/third_party/lua/lib/lbaselib.c, src/unmatched/FUN_002f87f0.c

 igShadeModelAttr _mode=1; igCullFaceAttr _enabled=0; igDepthStateAttr; igFogStateAttr x2 (one on)
 igTextureStateAttr _enabled=1; igTextureMatrixStateAttr _enabled=1
 igMaterialAttr x2 _diffuse=[1,1,1,1] _ambient=[1,1,1,1] _emission=[0,0,0,1] _specular=[0,0,0,1]
 igColorAttr x265 _color=[0.9,0.9,0.9,0.95] on igTransform (menu, group10, group11, null1),
 beModelCtrlInfo j_startback, beAction2Info j_startmenu02 and every igGeometryAttr1_5
 igTextureAttr x12 magFilter=1 minFilter=1 wrap=1 -> sback00.png, logo.png, base*.png, moji*.png

Consequence: the title tone is pack data, not an unknown engine value. The port hard-codes a
constant shade (frontlayer.c: the neutral grey 72,72,77,127) and one alpha blend, so the
additive pass over sback00 - the blue panel streaks and the bright core - is missing. That is
the "traces around the centre aren't there" the operator reports.
Evidence: igb_ids/igb_inst walk of AREA.AFS entry 620.
normalised no evidence recorded

<a id="f665"></a>
### #665 · unconfirmed

**Where:** AREA.AFS entry 620 (front-end UI pack)

The front-end pack's colour belongs to the layer: FALSE for the layer's 16 meshes -- the 265
igColorAttr records are the font attribute sets' colour and dw4_scene_load resolves none for the
layer, so the layer's shade is engine draw state (#631) and the pack colour is the text tint.

**Notes:** CORRECTS #664. The 265 igColorAttr records in the front-end UI pack (AREA.AFS entry 620) are the
265 *font* attribute sets, not the layer. Verified with the port's own loader (dw4_scene_load over
the entry-620 blob): 917 nodes, 301 of them igAttrSet, and exactly 265 resolve a colour -- every one
named font00/font01/.../font000..., _color = [0.9, 0.9, 0.9, 0.95]. Not one of the layer's 16 drawn
meshes (pCylinderShape2/3/4/6/7/8, pCubeShape2/3/4/7/8, polySurfaceShape97/98/101/104/109) resolves a
colour, so #631 ("the layer's blend and shade are the front-end's own draw state, not pack fields")
STANDS.

How #664 went wrong: the pack's reference ids were resolved in the wrong id space (the object table's
remap ids instead of the dir-entry ids the loader uses), so unrelated u32s "resolved" to igColorAttr.
Re-running the reverse scan with the loader's own resolution gives 265 colour records with ZERO
references from any record field; they are referenced from igAttrList member blocks (282 of them),
whose owners are the font attr sets.

Port consequence: shading the layer from the pack's colour is a no-op (0/16 meshes have one). What
is real, and now used: the pack binds a texture per node (sback00 for the layer's 16, logo.png for
polySurfaceShape2, mojips for the panels, cursor for the ticks), resolved by dw4_scene_node_texture.

Render workbench (verifies any of this): build a roms dir from the ISO and run
`dw4 --roms \<dir> --boot --headless --frames 600 --out-dir \<dir>`; the title writes frame_0000.png
(about a second). `DW4_TITLE_LAYER=0` / `DW4_TITLE_BACKDROP=0` isolate each composite,
`DW4_FL_TEXDUMP=1` dumps the decoded sback00. The title frame today shows the ring/spokes/panels
correctly but a hard white square over the centre; disabling the layer blacks the frame out, so the
square is one of the layer's own 16 meshes sampling the texture's bright area -- not the backdrop or
the UI, and not a missing texture (sback00 itself has no white block).

<a id="f666"></a>
### #666 · confirmed

**Where:** AREA.AFS entry 620 — dw4_frontlayer_draw (port/src/frontlayer.c)

The layer's texture is sampled u-mirrored: that is the missing trace art

**Notes:** The front-end layer's texture is sampled u-mirrored, and that is what the port's missing "trace lines"
were.

The pack's trace art is geometry, not texture: wireframes of AREA.AFS entry 620's layer meshes show
polySurfaceShape97 (144v/120f), polySurfaceShape98 (61v/45f) and pCubeShape2 (46v/28f) are the thick
circuit-trace line strips, polySurfaceShape109 (340v/216f) the rune ring, polySurfaceShape101 the ring
outline. The port draws all of them, and painted them black.

Why: each of those meshes has a UV range of u 0.07..0.49 at v = 0.9844 (one row, the texture's last).
Read straight that lands in the black left half of sback00's bottom row; the game's traces are the
vivid blue at x >= 64 of the same row. Sampling u mirrored (u' = 1 - u) puts the traces, the rune ring
and the violet panel art on their real texels and the port's title frame becomes the game's blue
circuit ring. pCubeShape8 -- a 413x413 camera-facing quad at z = -1200 whose four UVs are all
(0.9844, 0.9219) -- sampled one pure-white texel and was the port's "solid white block" over the
centre; mirrored it samples a dark texel and the block is gone.

So the layer's material is one flat shade, its texture was read u-mirrored, and the earlier readings of
the layer's appearance (#631, #664/#665) were chasing a colour that is not in the pack. correction (#720): the stream samples the layer's texture unmirrored with REPEAT wrap; the mirror compensated for the t=0 clock and a clamping sampler. Folded into #720, the current answer for j_startback texture sampling (u-mirror vs unmirrored REPEAT); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c

<a id="f667"></a>
### #667 · confirmed

**Where:** AREA.AFS entry 620 — read_seq (port/src/scene.c)

igTransformSequence1_5 channels are identified by descriptor slot, not by read order: slot 2 translation, slot 4 scale

**Notes:** An igTransformSequence1_5 carries two igVec3fList channels and a node may carry only ONE of them. The
slot decides which, not the order seen: slot 2 is _interpolationDataTranslation and slot 4
_interpolationDataScale (reflection offsets 60 then 68; slot 3 is the igQuaternionfList rotation, slot
11 the igLongList times).

Reading whichever vec3 list came first as the translation therefore silently turned a kind='scale' list
into a translation. Every j_startback trace group (group8) carries ONLY the scale --
(1,1,1) at 0 s, (1,1,1) at 2.500 s, (0,0,1) at 2.517 s, held to 6 s -- so the traces were read as a
sub-millimetre translation and never collapsed. group9 (pCubeShape3, the flash) carries only
(0,1,0)/(1,1,1)/(0,1,0) at 2.500 s. With the slot-based assignment the port's opening timeline becomes:

 0.000-2.500 s the trace groups at scale 1 (full), the flash node collapsed (0,1,0)
 2.500 s the traces collapse (0,0,1) and the flash pops (1,1,1) for one keyframe
 2.583-3.000 s group10/group11 translate the two panels in from x=0 to x=-200/+200
 2.583-3.500 s group15 dollies z 0 -> 800 (the tunnel rushing in)
 4.000-6.000 s the SQ_WAIT loop

so the port's white flash now fires at t = 2.50 s exactly as the retail capture's frame 296 does --
see the port-vs-game opening comparison after the slot fix.

Still open: what hides the non-trace meshes during the first ~2.4 s. Those nodes carry only
translations (group15 (0,0,0)->(0,0,800)), and treating a sequence with no scale channel as scale 0 was
tested and refuted: it blackens the whole layer, traces included.

**Evidence:** port/src/scene.c

<a id="f668"></a>
### #668 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00b083 — igAlphaFunctionAttr in the j_startback attr chain (AREA.AFS 620)

The trace-phase hide of the tunnel is the layer's igAlphaFunctionAttr (sback00 material, _func=4 _refValue=0.6): a GS alpha test discards the tunnel/ring until the flash.

**Notes:** The material provenance remains unsupported: AREA.AFS entry 620 registers no igAlphaFunctionAttr carrying _refValue=0.6. The later task-4D attempt to revive this mechanism (#707) was also wrong: it divided 128*128 by 255. GS TFX_MODULATE uses 0x80 as 1.0 and computes min(0xff,(At*Av)>>7), so texture alpha 128 and vertex alpha 128 produce fragment alpha 128, which passes GREATER AREF 76. The original port probe was not a valid GS comparison because its texture/vertex alpha domains were inconsistent, but correcting that probe does not make the recorded alpha test hide the tunnel. Folded into #708, the current answer for Trace-phase tunnel hidden by GS alpha test (refuted); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** Port renders of the at_0.0, at_0.3 and at_0.6 runs

<a id="f669"></a>
### #669 · refuted

**Where:** 0x03d8e7 — igCullFaceAttr / GS back-face cull

The engine hides the tunnel by culling back faces: from outside the tube (t<2.583 s) its wall faces away, once group15's +800 z translation puts the eye inside it the wall faces the eye and draws.

**Notes:** Implementation test: DW4_FL_CULL 1/2 (screen-space signed area) and 3/4 (view-space geometric normal vs the eye, from the mesh winding) in port/src/frontlayer.c. 1 and 4 drop every triangle, 2 and 3 keep every triangle: all meshes project with one winding sign, i.e. every wall triangle's normal points at the eye. Geometric reason: for a tube around the view axis an inward normal N=(-cos,-sin,0) has N.P = -R < 0 for every point (and an outward normal +R) regardless of where the eye sits on the axis, so back-face culling of a tube is all-or-nothing and can never distinguish outside/in. Culling is not the mechanism.

**Evidence:** Port renders of the DW4_FL_CULL off, 3 and 4 runs; port/src/frontlayer.c

<a id="f670"></a>
### #670 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x03d48f — beModelCtrlNode2 (scene node 861) -> igAttrSet 'New Object' / 'PromotedAttr'; beAction2::vfunc_33 case 3 GETAPPEARANCELIST; beModelCtrlNode2._overrideAttrList

The tunnel (pCylinderShape3, node 864 under igTransform group15 / igAttrSet 'New Object' 862) is withheld from the draw during the trace phase by the model controller's appearance/actor submission, not by any scene-graph transform: it is the ONLY geometry under its igAttrSet, while every mesh the game DOES show (traces, rune ring, radiating lines) hangs under the sibling igAttrSet 'PromotedAttr' (865).

**Notes:** Narrowing from three sessions of tests. (1) Ground truth: the game's trace phase really has NO tunnel or ring - the retail opening frames 286..292 brightened x6 stay black inside the rune ring (max R=18), so it is a hide, not a dim. (2) The camera cannot do it: eye (0,0,730), focal 622 reproduces the game's rune-ring radius (217-unit disc at z~15 -> 186 fb px) exactly, and the traces and the tunnel are both on the same axis, so no eye position shows one and hides the other. (3) The packed transforms cannot do it: group15's _m is identity and its sequence drives translation only (0,0,0 -> (0,0,800) at 3.5 s), and the 'no scale channel means scale 0' rule is already refuted (it kills the traces too). (4) The igSwitch (node 860, d=0x03d8e7, _childMask 1-bit id 6940; the same object the action-2 controller holds as _switch=id 6946) has ONE child (beModelCtrlNode2 861), so it gates the whole layer, not the tunnel. (5) New pack fact that stands out: the layer's 16 igGeometry nodes split into igAttrSet 'New Object' @0x03d48f (tunnel only) and 'PromotedAttr' @0x00ae23 (everything visible). Layer mesh topology (from dw4asset scene --out-dir): all the visible meshes are flat discs/strips at z~12-30 (polySurfaceShape109 r217 z15, pCylinderShape2 r227 z15.6, pCylinderShape7/8 r~220 z30); only pCylinderShape3 is deep (z 4.5..-3309, r 500) and only pCylinderShape101/104/6 are mid-depth. Next step: decode how beModelCtrl submits appearances (igAppearanceList/igActorList, beAction2 GETAPPEARANCELIST) and whether 'New Object' is submitted only from the flash.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c DW4_FL_SEQ/DW4_FL_NODES dumps; dw4asset scene --afs AREA.AFS --entry 620 --graph

<a id="f671"></a>
### #671 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00ae53 — Gap__Core__igGeometry / GS trace phase

The opening trace phase DOES submit pCylinderShape3. Per-vertex UV coverage of the trace-phase GS dump draw 1 is 1217/1223 against the pack pCylinderShape3 histogram and draw 2 is 357/421; cylinder-exclusive UV pairs appear with the expected multiplicities. pCubeShape4/7/8, polySurfaceShape101 and pCubeShape3 also appear. The visual mismatch is therefore not explained by missing submission; it must be reconciled with controller-driven pose/transform or another per-draw mechanism.

**Notes:** The original material inference in this row is superseded by #702/#703: effective state is textured/additive/Gouraud and the port material probes do not reproduce the original while the static tube pose is drawn. #687 provides the stronger UV-multiset statement.
