normalised refuted; superseded by #702, #703 — the material inference was wrong; #702/#703 hold the effective state.

<a id="f672"></a>
### #672 · refuted

**Where:** 0x03d06f — AREA.AFS entry 620 (front-end pack)

The trace-phase tunnel hide is a per-action appearance/actor swap in the model controller (beAction2Data +0x20 GETAPPEARANCELIST, igAppearanceList/igActorList). REFUTED for j_startback: its beAction2Data records (NDSP/OPEN/WAIT01) carry only _comList (K=2) in this pack's class version, so the action has no appearance field at all; beModelCtrlNode2._overrideAttrList resolves to an empty igAttrList (id 7024). The action bank's _switch (id 6946) resolves to the scene's own igSwitch, which the port already walks.

**Notes:** So no per-action node-subset switching exists for the layer: its whole node tree is drawn every frame. Combined with #671 (the tunnel's geometry IS in the trace-phase vertex stream), the port's error is in the per-draw STATE (texture/blend/colour/depth), not in which nodes it walks or submits.

**Evidence:** dw4asset reflect --entry 620 regions 0x00bd.. (beAction2Data NDSP/OPEN/WAIT01, K=2 _comList only), beAction2Info j_startback _switch=6946; dw4asset refs --id 7024 -> igAttrList @0x3e00b (empty); dw4asset refs --id 6946 -> igSwitch @0x3d8e7 (= the scene's node 860, childMask 6940).

<a id="f673"></a>
### #673 · confirmed

**Where:** tooling / harness

GS-dump tooling + corrected dump facts: scripts/ (gsdump.py, gs_shade_raster.py, camera_match.py, live_skin.py) is MISSING from /workspace, which every captures INDEX and the port's make check references; a rebuilt reader was written. Corrections it established: PCSX2 GSDumpSingleFrame header = 8 bytes + 9 u32; records 0x00 transfer{u8 path,u32 len}, 0x02 read{u32}, 0x03 registers{8192}; in this game the front-end layer arrives on path3 as PACKED nreg=3 descriptor 2,1,4 = ST, RGBAQ, XYZF2 with 16-byte entries; the GS register map used here is XYOFFSET=0x18 and SCISSOR=0x40 (not 0x52/0x5a); XYOFFSET is (1728,1824), scissor (0,639)x(0,447).

**Notes:** The reader must not be trusted for the per-vertex RGBAQ/alpha decode yet (values look odd) or for the sprite XYZ2 Y field (bits 16-31 vs 32-47 for XYZF2).

**Evidence:** find / -name 'gs_shade_raster.py' etc -> absent; the rebuilt GS reader reproduces the project's own tool on the goblin-pass reference GS dump (character draw 15112 verts, wall 7008, total 45788 vs the documented 45732); trace-phase GS dump frame = 6 draws/frame (14 sprite + 1223 + 421 + 2091 + 6 + 785 = 4540 verts) x4 frames.

<a id="f674"></a>
### #674 · refuted

**Where:** 0x03d50b — seq_local (port/src/scene.c); igTransformSequence1_5 per-channel key lists

The tunnel's invisibility is the engine's undriven-channel rule: a node that HAS a sequence but whose sequence drives no scale key collapses (scale 0, 'igTransformSequence1_5 keeps one key list per channel and a channel with no list is zero'). REFUTED in that form: with it applied to every sequenced node the t=0 frame goes completely black (mean 0.00, nonblack 0.000) -- the traces collapse too, because an ancestor common to all the trace meshes also carries a scale-less sequence. Nodes with no sequence take their _m (identity) as the port already does. Probe: DW4_SEQ_UNDRIVEN_ZERO in port/src/scene.c, committed default-off.

**Notes:** The tunnel's chain being the only one without a scale-driven group remains suggestive but is NOT by itself the mechanism.

**Evidence:** port build with DW4_SEQ_UNDRIVEN_ZERO=1: frame_0000 mean 0.00 nonblack 0.000; =0 (default): mean 147.82 nonblack 1.000 (unchanged). The tunnel's group15 (node 863) is the only mesh whose chain has no scale-driven node, but that is not sufficient: the rule cannot be global.

<a id="f675"></a>
### #675 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00b083

SUPERSEDED/REFUTED: the claim that the j_startback trace-phase state is PRIM=0x004 with TEX0 never written and (critically) XYOFFSET never written / XYOFFSET=0x14 / TEST_1 ZTST=LEQUAL, SCISSOR at a non-canonical address. Wrong register map.

**Notes:** Refuted by #690: XYOFFSET_1 is address 0x18 and IS written (OFX=1728, OFY=1824); the old row's '0x14' is TEX1_1 and '0x18' is XYOFFSET_1, and TEXCLUT is 0x1c (not 0x3f). TEST_1 decodes to AREF=0x4c with ZTST=GEQUAL (0x504cd), not LEQUAL. Kept (not deleted) so the superseded reading is not resumed. The correct per-pass decode is now produced by gsframes.py/gsstatediff.py on the canonical map in gsregs.py. Folded into #690, the current answer for GS dump register map (XYOFFSET/TEST decode); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

<a id="f676"></a>
### #676 · refuted

**Where:** 0x03d06f

The tunnel's trace-phase invisibility is the GS alpha test (ATE=1, ATST=LESS, AREF=0x4c from the layer's igAlphaFunctionAttr 0.6) discarding its fragments while the trace/ring fragments pass.

**Notes:** Refuted as the tunnel's hider. The trace-phase frame's fragment alpha does not discard the tunnel: the RGBAQ entry is dword-padded (A=byte12=0x80=128) and even a byte0 reading drops only ~35% (#678). The reveal is not the alpha test. See the new post-flash state diff (#682). Folded into #708, the current answer for Trace-phase tunnel hidden by GS alpha test (refuted); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** GS dump of the trace-phase frame, TEST_1=0x504cd

<a id="f677"></a>
### #677 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x00b097

RGBAQ layout: every vertex colour in both the trace-phase and goblin dumps is a single byte k duplicated at bits 0-7 and 32-39 (0x000000kk000000kk), with bytes 1,2,3,5,6,7 zero. k ranges 0x19..0x7f, i.e. 0..127, which matches the GS alpha range and straddles AREF 0x4c. So the stream is an alpha ramp, not a 4-channel RGBA colour, and a {R,G,B,A at bits 0-31} reading is ruled out (it would give A=0 and drop every fragment). The exact byte position the GS takes as fragment alpha is still unresolved; a per-draw histogram does not separate the tunnel draws from the visible trace draws (both dominated by 0x7f). Also unresolved: the XYZF2 lane pair for screen X/Y (lanes 0/2 give 1532..2564 px and lanes 4/6 give 0..612 x 0..171; neither reproduces the visible frame), so a geometric clip cause for the tunnel is not excluded.

**Notes:** Per-primitive alpha test on the trace-phase GS dump (gsalphaprim.py, gsdegen.py, gsbandtest.py): the RGBAQ byte is 0..127 and the stream is an alpha ramp, but no single vertex index or byte position separates tunnel from traces at the 0.6 threshold. Using the tunnel-exclusive ST band (v 0.75..0.87) vs the trace band (v>=0.95): trace primitives are 100% alpha=0x7f; tunnel primitives range 0x3f..0x7f with only ~30-40% at or below 0x4c, and that fraction is the same for the 1st, 2nd and 3rd vertex of the primitive. So the alpha test explains at most ~35% of the tunnel's absence. Also refuted: geometric collapse -- gsdegen.py shows the tunnel's packets have real per-lane spreads (367/361/417/118 raw), so its triangles are not degenerate under any reading. Lane roles so far: the visible trace band has a CONSTANT lane4 (~590 raw/16) while varying in lanes 0/2; the tunnel varies in lanes 0/2/4/6. So lane4 behaves like Z (flat overlay for traces, a depth range for the tunnel) and lanes 0/2 like X/Y, but the X/Y magnitudes (1531..2564 at /16) do not fit the 640-wide framebuffer, so the vertex format is still not correctly modelled on the GS side. Next probe should come from the ENGINE side: the meaning of igVertexArray1_1._vertexFormat=0x10003 (bits 0,1,16) plus the engine's XYZF2/GS packer, which would pin both the fragment-alpha channel and the coordinate lanes. Folded into #683, the current answer for GS RGBAQ vertex colour layout; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** gsstate2/gsalpha on the trace-phase GS dump, the goblin-pass reference GS dump

<a id="f678"></a>
### #678 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083

The j_startback trace-phase frame is drawn in TWO alpha-test passes, and the tunnel-vs-traces split follows the pass boundary exactly. Loose pass TEST_1=0x500cd (ATE=1 ATST=GREATER AREF=0x0c=12, ZTE=1 ZTST=LEQUAL): 100% (644/644) of the trace/ring-band primitives (ST v>=0.95, exclusive to polySurfaceShape109 and the trace meshes) are kept. Strict pass TEST_1=0x504cd (same but AREF=0x4c=76 = 0.6x127, matching the layer's igAlphaFunctionAttr._refValue=0.6): 100% (748/748) of the tunnel-exclusive primitives (ST v in 0.75..0.87, which no other j_startback mesh reaches) are drawn here, and 260 of them (35%) are dropped by the A>0.6 test. So the engine does put the tunnel under the 0.6 alpha test and the traces under a permissive one, but the strict test alone removes only ~35% of the tunnel's triangles, so it does not by itself explain the total absence.

**Evidence:** GS dump of the trace-phase frame: band attribution is exact at the pass level

<a id="f679"></a>
### #679 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00b0df

REFUTED: the record-only GS state census was claimed to prove that the GIFtag PRIM governed and the trace geometry was untextured, unblended and flat. The dump freeze state instead has PRMODECONT.AC=0, so PRMODE=0x78/0x58 supplies TME=1, ABE=1 and IIP=1 (#702).

**Notes:** The alpha/depth investigations were superseded by #692/#694/#695. The packed alpha is normally 128 and passes both AREF thresholds; the material-state conclusion here must not be reused. Folded into #702, the current answer for Trace-phase material is textured/Gouraud (PRMODECONT.AC=0), not flat/untextured; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/model-format.md; reflection.json

<a id="f680"></a>
### #680 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0015efc0

igPsx2VertexArray1_1._vertexFormat is a runtime bitmask: bit22 = positions, bit23 = normals; there is no colour element.

**Notes:** vfunc_35/36 test (*(int*)(this+28) & 0x400000) and write 3 floats at *_vdata(+68) + idx*0xC -> bit 22 = positions, 12 bytes/vertex, buffer field at +68. vfunc_38/39 test & 0x800000 and use +72 -> bit 23 = normals. No other bit of that field is tested anywhere in the decompiled engine, and no colour element appears in any vertex-array consumer, so per-vertex colour/alpha must come from the VU1 shade. Consequence: the GS-side vertex packing (ST/RGBAQ/XYZF2) is VU1 microcode and is NOT resolvable from this C decomp. Note: my previous statement that _vertexFormat is not a bitmask was wrong -- reflection.json shows the file-side igStructMetaField desc (0x560158), but the runtime field is a bitmask.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/src/engine/Gap__Core__igPsx2VertexArray1_1__vfunc_35|36 (mask 0x400000), vfunc_38|39 (mask 0x800000); docs/engine/model-format.md

<a id="f682"></a>
### #682 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startback trace phase / title (AREA.AFS 620)

For comparable flashsweep f10 and f19 frame-section 6 passes, the repaired state comparator finds the same 11 populated in-frame state registers. The reveal is therefore not explained by a change in those per-pass registers. This is narrower than full GS-state identity: freeze/inherited texture state is active under PRMODE and must be considered separately (#702).

**Notes:** The original proof was vacuous because the old comparator produced empty late-pass maps. The repaired gsstatediff.py comparison is non-vacuous: frame 6 pass 0 compares 11 populated registers with 0 differences, and pass 1 likewise. Removed the obsolete claim that texture setup is inert; PRMODECONT.AC=0 makes the bound texture active.

**Evidence:** Register-state comparison of the flashsweep f10 and f19 GS dumps

<a id="f683"></a>
### #683 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b097 — GS PACKED vertex (descriptor [2,1,4] = ST, RGBAQ, XYZF2)

Correction to #677: the vertex colour entry is a 128-bit value carrying ONE 8-bit channel per 32-bit dword (dword0=R, dword1=G, dword2=B, dword3=A), NOT a single byte duplicated at bits 0-7/32-39, and NOT a 32-bit {R,G,B,A at bits 0-31}.

**Notes:** Raw RGBAQ entry for a trace-phase tunnel vertex = bytes 3f000000 3f000000 46000000 80000000 -> R=0x3f G=0x3f B=0x46 A=0x80 = a blue-grey at alpha 128. Every vertex: bytes 1,2,3,5,6,7,9,10,11,13,14,15 are zero; the non-zero data lives in the low byte of each of the four dwords. The earlier reading of a 'single byte k duplicated at bits 0-7 and 32-39' is the dword-padded layout seen bytewise (byte0 and byte4 are two DIFFERENT channels, R and G); gsbytes.py's byte indexer happened to line them up. R ranges 63..81 (trace band 127), B=R+7, A=127/128. Consequence: gsbandtest.py used byte0 as the fragment alpha (35% of the tunnel band <= 0x4c -> 'drop'); if byte12 (A=0x80=128) is the alpha, the strict 0.6 test passes on EVERY tunnel fragment. Either way no single byte reading makes the tunnel fully vanish, so the alpha test is excluded as the hider (consistent with #678's bound). Topic head for GS RGBAQ vertex colour layout: also read #677 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** Raw RGBAQ entries of a GS dump of the title opening

<a id="f684"></a>
### #684 · unconfirmed

**Where:** j_startback layer, AREA.AFS 620

Single-frame rasters of the two dumps show DIFFERENT geometry: the trace frame is a head-on ring with radiating traces (matching the visible trace phase), the post-flash frame is a perspective tunnel tube (matching the title). This weakens the pre-flash 'tunnel-exclusive UV band' attribution - the trace frame's v[0.75,0.87] primitives may be the ring/traces, not the tunnel.

**Notes:** Rasterised every vertex write of each dump (X=lane0/16, Y=lane2/16, minus XYOFFSET 1728/1824). The pre-flash dump is 5 frames x 4526 verts; the post-flash 5 frames x 1216 verts. Frame-by-frame renders: pre = circular ring with tick glyphs + long radiating spokes, no receding tube; post = receding perspective tube. Both use the same descriptor and the same GS state. Needs a same-scene comparison (a dump spanning the flash) to separate 'scene changed' from 'the tunnel's draw state flipped'. Folded into #686, the current answer for Trace-phase vs post-flash draw sets 'disjoint'; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** Rasters of every vertex write of the pre-flash and post-flash GS dumps

**Where:** j_startback opening (AREA.AFS 620) / title transition

The opening's white flash is a pure full-screen overlay: the trace-phase draw set (6 draws, 4540 verts) is byte-identical from the start of the trace phase through the whole flash; the submitted draw set changes only AFTER the flash, and the trace-phase draws and the post-flash tunnel draws are disjoint sets.

**Notes:** Paused (software path) mid-trace-phase and frame-advanced, taking an F8 GSDumpSingleFrame every 8 frames for 240 frames (30 dumps). Embedded screenshots: f2-f10 blue traces/ring drawing; f11 ring turns silver; f13-f16 white flash; f17-f18 tunnel fades in; f19-f22 solid tunnel; f23 title. Draw signatures: f4..f15 are IDENTICAL (6 draws: 14/SPR 1223/TRI 421/TRI 2091/TRI 6/TRI 785/TRI = 4540 verts) - the flash does not change the geometry at all. f17 flips to 6 draws/4706 (1223&2091 unchanged, 421->517, 6->24, 785->837); f19 is a NEW 12-draw set (45/919/339/103/607/1137/16/994/42/889/34); f20 12-16 draws; f23 title 11 draws (textured, TEX0=0x400714059d30b880). So the trace set (4526 verts) and the tunnel set are DISJOINT - no shared (prim,vertex-count) draw. The trace phase is a different geometry set, not the tunnel hidden in place: reinterpret #671 (its 'tunnel-exclusive UV band' is shared by the trace-scene meshes in the same atlas).

### #689 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083 — gsdraws.py and gsshot.py on the flashsweep f23 GS dump; GS register map

Open tooling question on the 'no texture' premise of #675/#679/#682: every geometry GIFtag in the trace-phase GS dump and flashsweep-f19/f23 has PRIM=0x004/0x005 (TME=0 ABE=0 IIP=0), and f23 writes three TEX0_1 values (addr 0x06) but no BITBLT (no 0x50-0x53 A+D writes), yet the frame that is on screen shows image-based content (the DIGIMON WORLD 4 logo banner). Either the logo was blitted on a frame not dumped (the dump's own screenshot is the current, accumulated framebuffer, so it is not proof of this frame's texturing), or the PRIM/TME reading is not the effective PRIM. Resolve before treating 'TME=0, nothing is textured' as settled. Also: A+D address 0x18 in this game carries a value of the form 0x6c00|0x7200<<32 = XYOFFSET(1728,1824), not the TEXCLUT the handoff's 'corrected' map assigns to 0x18.

**Notes:** Folded into #702, the current answer for Trace-phase material is textured/Gouraud (PRMODECONT.AC=0), not flat/untextured; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

<a id="f690"></a>
### #690 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083 — GS register decode / tooling (gsdraws.py, gsframes.py)

GS dump register decode is settled, and it CONFIRMS gsdraws' coordinates: the A+D entry's register address is the low byte of the entry's SECOND qword (hi&0xff), which yields the canonical GS address set; XYOFFSET_1 (0x18) is written and holds exactly the textbook 640x448 pair OFX=1728<<4=0x6c00, OFY=1824<<4=0x7200; SCISSOR_1 (0x40)=0x01bf0000027f0000 decodes to (X0,X1,Y0,Y1)=(0,639,0,447); FOGCOL (0x3d)=0xffffff; TEST_1 (0x47) in {0x30000 (ATE off, ZTE=1/ZTST=ALWAYS), 0x504cd (ATE=1, ATST=GREATER, AREF=76, ZTE=1, ZTST=GEQUAL), 0x500cd (same, AREF=12)}. Authoritative bit layouts (ps2homebrew/gslib gsDefs.h): GS_SET_TEST zte<<16 ztst<<17 (so ZTE=bit16, ZTST=bits17-18); GS_SET_FRAME = (fbp>>13) | (fbw/64)<<16 | psm<<24, i.e. FBP field = byte address>>13 and FBW field (bits16-21) = width/64 -> FRAME_1=0x000a0000 = FBP 0, FBW 10 = 640px, PSM 0 = PSMCT32; FRAME_1=0x000a008c = FBP 140 (=0x118000), FBW 640; FRAME_1=0x000401a3 = FBP 419 (=0x343000), FBW 4 = 256px; ZBUF_1=0x...0a000118 = ZBP 280 (=0x230000), ZMSK 0/1. Therefore the handoff's earlier 'corrected' map entry (0x18=TEXCLUT) and finding #675's 'XYOFFSET never written, treat as 0' are BOTH WRONG, and the previous session's off-screen/placement reading of the trace-phase geometry is an artifact of the missing offset, not a fact.

**Notes:** CORRECTION (verified with gsframes.py after fixing the FRAME decode in the tool): FBP is in units of 8192 bytes, so FRAME_1=0x000401a3 = FBP 419 -> byte 0x346000 (not 0x343000); 0x000a0000 = FBP 0 -> byte 0x0; 0x000a008c = FBP 140 -> byte 0x118000. All FRAME_1/2 writes in the opening frame have FBMSK=0, i.e. no colour-masked (Z-only) clear pass at frame setup. Consequence for any analysis in this file: screen coordinates must be Xraw/16 - OFX_px with OFX_px = 1728 and OFY_px = 1824, not raw x/16. Topic head for GS dump register map (XYOFFSET/TEST decode): also read #675 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** A+D address histogram of the trace-phase GS dump (addr 0x18 x16 values 0x0000720000006c00/0x0000780000007800; 0x40 x12 0x01bf0000027f0000; 0x3d x4 0xffffff); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ps2homebrew/gslib/master/source/gsDefs.h

<a id="f691"></a>
### #691 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083 — trace-phase frame composition (j_startback opening)

With the confirmed XYOFFSET/SCISSOR decode, the opening trace-phase frame's tunnel geometry is ON SCREEN, not off it: flashsweep-f10's last frame-section (FRAME_1=0x000a0000, FBP 0, 640x448 CT32) submits 4526 vertices in 5 draws whose screen bboxes (screen = Xraw/16 - OFX_px) are draw0 (TRISTRIP 1223 v) x[-105,745] y[-200,649], draw1 (TRIFAN 421 v) x[-19,657] y[52,439], draw2 (TRISTRIP 2091 v) x[-196,836] y[-291,741], draw4 (TRISTRIP 785 v) x[119,521] y[-102,519], z in 24527..41033 of 0xFFFFFF. The same frame in the trace-phase GS dump gives identical numbers. So the tunnel covers the 640x448 screen in the trace phase: a hide cannot be explained by placing it off-screen, and the port's 'wrong fixed placement' story in #688 is not supported.

**Notes:** CAVEAT: the screen bbox proves the geometry is submitted and spans the screen in model space; it does not prove it rasterised. The frame is double-buffered (FBP 0 / FBP 140 = byte 0x118000) and an embedded screenshot is a different display state (#696). Z RANGE REMOVED as invalid: the older '24527..41033' was read from lo>>32, the packed Y lane. Corrected packed Z (hi & 0x00ffffff, gsregs.packed_vertex) for the trace-phase GS dump geometry is ~8066..9257 (draw 1) and 0..9107 (draw 2). Retains only the confirmed X/Y on-screen placement result.

**Evidence:** Frame decode of the trace-phase, flashsweep-f10 and flashsweep-f15 GS dumps (identical draw sets: 4526 verts, 5 draws)

<a id="f692"></a>
### #692 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00b083 — GS TEST/ALPHA path, trace phase

The trace-phase tunnel's fragments cannot be discarded by the alpha test and it is not textured or blended: the vertex colour is sent as four dwords, one channel per dword low byte, and the histogram over the whole trace frame is (127,127,127,128) x1597, (63,63,70,128) x510, (71,71,76,128) x63, (63,63,70,127) x32, (127,127,127,127) x24... i.e. alpha is 0x80 (128) on essentially every vertex. The GIFtag PRIM for every geometry draw is 0x004/0x005 = TRISTRIP/TRIFAN with TME=0, ABE=0, IIP=0 (so untextured, unblended, flat-shaded). Both passes have ATE=1 ATST=GREATER: pass 0 AREF=76, pass 1 AREF=12. 128 > 76 > 12, so ALL of the tunnel's fragments pass the alpha test in BOTH passes. Alpha test / TME / ABE are therefore ruled out as the hide mechanism.

**Notes:** REFUTED because the material half is wrong: PRMODECONT.AC=0 makes PRMODE authoritative (see #702), so PRMODE=0x78/0x58 gives TME=1 ABE=1 IIP=1 and TEX0 binds a 128x64 PSMT8 texture. The packed vertex alpha and alpha-test exclusion remain valid. With TFX=MODULATE the GS computes fragment alpha as min(0xff,(textureAlpha*vertexAlpha)>>7); 128*128>>7 = 128, and 128 passes both GREATER AREF 76 and GREATER AREF 12. Corrected per-draw rasters with AREF 76 are byte-identical to alpha-test-disabled rasters (#708). Folded into #708, the current answer for Trace-phase tunnel hidden by GS alpha test (refuted); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** vertex-colour histogram per pass over the trace-phase GS dump (gsframes.py + the 4-dword RGBA dump): pass0 TEST=0x504cd 2342 verts, pass1 TEST=0x500cd 2184 verts

<a id="f693"></a>
### #693 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00b083 — GS Z path / frame setup, opening

Leading remaining mechanism for the trace-phase hide: the Z buffer. ZBUF_1 in these frames is 0x0a000118 = ZBP 280 -> byte address 0x230000, ZMSK 0 (and one variant 0x10a000118 with ZMSK 1), and both draw passes set TEST_1 with ZTE=1, ZTST=GEQUAL (0x504cd / 0x500cd). The only visible-area-clearing draws in the frame are two SPRITE passes with TEST=0x30000 (ATE off, ZTST=ALWAYS) of 10 and 4 vertices whose vertices all land at screen y=-1824/-1920 (i.e. off-screen), so the visible Z region may never be cleared; with GEQUAL, a fragment at z=24527..41033 is rejected wherever the Z buffer still holds a smaller (nearer) value from an earlier frame. Same-register comparison of flashsweep-f10 vs f19 at the tunnel draw shows 0 differing registers (test/frame/zbuf/alpha/tex0/xyoff all identical), so the mechanism must be in the data or an uncleared surface rather than in the submitted state.

**Notes:** REFUTED by #695: the frame's Z surface is verified 0 full-screen (the 10-vertex sprite clear tiles x 0..640 / y 0..448 exactly and writes z=0 with ZTST=ALWAYS and AFAIL=0, so both fb and zb are written), and the scene's ZTE=1/ZTST=GEQUAL accepts any fragment with z >= 0, which every tunnel fragment satisfies (z ~0x2392 or ~0x08a7 depending on the field reading). The Z test rejects nothing. Kept for the record: the Z surface IS shared between the two colour buffers and the frame IS fully cleared, which was the open question here. Folded into #695, the current answer for Z buffer as trace-phase tunnel hider; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** gsstatediff.py flashsweep-f10 vs flashsweep-f19 = 0 differing registers at pass start; gsframes.py per-frame register summary (fba/zbuf/test)

<a id="f694"></a>
### #694 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083 — GS frame setup, opening tunnel scene

The opening frame begins with a full-screen clear, and it is verified by exact tiling: the clear is a SPRITE pass (PRIM=6 written via A+D addr 0x00) under TEST=0x30000 (ATE off, ZTE=1, ZTST=ALWAYS) with RGBAQ=0x80000000 (black), XYOFFSET=(1728,1824) and ZBUF_1=0x0a000118 (ZBP 280 -> 0x230000, ZMSK=0). Its 10 vertices are 5 (XYZ3 'no-kick', XYZ2) pairs; each pair is (x=0x6c00,y=0x8e00)+(x=0x7000,y=0x7200) then stepping x by 0x400, i.e. screen (0,448)->(64,0), (64,0)->(128,0)...: 64px-wide full-height columns tiling x 0..640 and y 0..448 exactly = the whole 640x448 frame. The clear's vertex writes carry z=0, and with AFAIL=0 + ZTST=ALWAYS both the framebuffer and the Z buffer are written, so the Z surface is 0 across the frame. Per frame the scene is rendered TWICE, once into FBP 0 (0x000a0000) and once into FBP 140 (0x000a008c), and each render is preceded by its own full-screen clear of that buffer; a 256px CT32 target at FBP 419 is also cleared each frame (4 verts).

**Notes:** This replaces the previous session's assumption that the frame is not cleared and that the clear sprites land off-screen (that came from counting only the kick (XYZ2) vertices and ignoring the XYZ3 partner: the pair is the sprite). correction #710: prior PACKED XYZF depth/fog bit positions were wrong (missing four-bit shift). X/Y remains valid; recompute numerical Z claims with register-aware gsregs. #711 records ordered blending evidence.

**Evidence:** Packet dump of the clear (A+D 0x40/0x00/0x1b/0x47/0x4c/0x4e/0x01/0x18 then 0x0d/0x05 pairs); exact-tiling check script output (cols 0..9 x coverage 0..640, y coverage 0..448)

<a id="f695"></a>
### #695 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083 — GS Z path, opening tunnel scene

REFUTES #693: the Z test is not the trace-phase hide mechanism. Each colour-buffer render starts from a full-screen clear that writes Z=0, and the scene draws with TEST=0x504cd/0x500cd (ZTE=1, ZTST=GEQUAL, AFAIL=KEEP) against the same ZBUF. Revised with the corrected PACKED XYZF decode (#710, Z = hi bits 4-27) and the ordered replay (#711): depth DOES reject some fragments once earlier draws have written Z, but not the mechanism that removes the tunnel. Ring-f008 FRAME-section 8 fragment counts (z-fail / alpha-fail / written): run0 11189/0/216480, run1 83/0/12996, run2 part0 8/0/52775, run2 part1 (the covering ALPHA=0x44 batch) 27059/3135/389465, run3 9/0/0, run4 2046/43274/38387. The covering batch is ~93% written, so the tunnel is covered by blending (#711), not hidden by depth. TOOLING NOTE: old gsdraws.py/gsframes.py read z as (lo>>32), the Y lane; every earlier 'z range' (e.g. 24527..41033, 0x2392, 0x08a7) is invalid.

**Notes:** Combined with #692 (alpha test also passes) this leaves no per-fragment rejection in the submitted stream: whatever hides the tunnel is either above the GS (the transform/vertex data) or the pixel evidence itself is not the render of this stream (see the next finding). correction #710: prior PACKED XYZF depth/fog bit positions were wrong (missing four-bit shift). X/Y remains valid; recompute numerical Z claims with register-aware gsregs. #711 records ordered blending evidence. revision: replaced the earlier claims that every tunnel fragment passes depth and that the stream has no per-fragment rejection; both relied on the wrong Z decode and an unordered model. Counts from an instrumented ordered replay whose per-part mean RGB reproduces the #711 ordered metrics exactly. Same model limits as #711 (nearest sampling, float interpolation, zero initial depth, ZMSK not modelled). Topic head for Z buffer as trace-phase tunnel hider: also read #693 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** clear z=0 verified from the XYZ3/XYZ2 pairs; TEST decode of 0x504cd/0x500cd (ps2tek + gslib gsDefs.h); gsstatediff f10 vs f19 = 0 differing registers; instrumented fragment counts from an ordered replay of the GS stream

<a id="f698"></a>
### #698 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083 — gsregs.py packed_vertex/ad_vertex; gsdraws.py; gsscan.py

Packed Z extraction fixed and the A+D vertex layout distinguished. Packed XYZF2/XYZ2 (GIF desc nibble 4/5): X=first-qword bits0..15, Y=bits32..47, Z=second-qword bits0..23, F=bits32..39 (gsregs.packed_vertex). A+D vertex writes (nibble 0xe, addr 0x04/0x05/0x0c/0x0d) carry the 64-bit GS REGISTER value: X=bits0..15, Y=bits16..31, Z=bits32..63 (gsregs.ad_vertex). Reading A+D with the packed layout gave Y=0, which decoded the full-screen clear sprite to y=-1824; with the register layout it tiles y0..448 exactly, as #694 verified. The old tools' Z=(lo>>32) was the packed Y lane (irrelevant because the geometry is packed).

### #701 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083

Port probe (handoff task 3) attributes the visible front-end tunnel. Task-4 divergence lead: the port's front-end clears to (18,22,30) (src/engine.c:744) and composites the layer additively (Cs*As+Cd per render note), so the accumulation reads as a bright white ring the original lacks at t=0.

<a id="f702"></a>
### #702 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083

The trace-phase material is TEXTURED and GOURAUD, not flat grey: the dump's embedded GS freeze state (GSDumpSingleFrame header) has PRMODECONT.AC=0, so the GIFtag PRIM's flag bits are IGNORED and PRMODE supplies them (PCSX2 GSState::ApplyPRMODE; AC=1 would use the tag PRIM). The per-pass PRMODE writes are 0x78 (IIP=1 TME=1 FGE=1 ABE=1) and 0x58 (IIP=1 TME=1 FGE=0 ABE=1); the tag contributes only the type (TRISTRIP/TRIFAN). TEX0=0x400714059d30b880 = TBP0=0x388000, PSMT8 128x64 + CLUT; COLCLAMP=1; DTHE=0; FOGCOL=0xffffff. The saturated blue comes from that texture. BLEND (revised #711): the blend is NOT uniformly additive. The freeze state's ALPHA=0x48 (Cs*As+Cd) is the value left by the previous frame's final draws; in-frame writes set ALPHA=0x44 ((Cs-Cd)*As+Cd, ordinary alpha) for runs 0-2 and 0x48 only for runs 3-4 of ring-f008 FRAME-section 8. Read ALPHA per draw from the ordered write log, not from the freeze state.

**Notes:** Cross-checked: PRMODECONT.AC=0 in EVERY measured dump — ring-f008, ring-f022 (trace phase, TEX0=0x...9d30b880 PSMT8 128x64), flashsweep-f10, and postflash-title (TEX0=0x40078005e1313b80, PSMT8 256x128) — so PRMODE mode is the game's normal state, and the pre/post-flash TEX0 difference (#682) is a real texture change, not inert. gsfreeze.py reads it from gsread.parse_header(d)['state']. revision: the original text called the material ADDITIVE from the freeze-state ALPHA=0x48 alone. #711's ordered per-draw state shows ALPHA_1=0x44 for runs 0, 1, 2 and 0x48 for runs 3, 4. The texture/PRMODE/Gouraud claims are unchanged. Topic head for Trace-phase material is textured/Gouraud (PRMODECONT.AC=0), not flat/untextured: also read #679, #689, #700 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** Embedded freeze state of the ring-f008 GS dump; freeze ALPHA vs per-draw ALPHA_1; raster with the sback00 texture

<a id="f703"></a>
### #703 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00b0a0

Port trace-phase divergence is geometry/pose, not material. At t=0 the port draws all 16 j_startback meshes: DW4_FL_ONLY=polySurfaceShape visually reproduces the original ring-f008 blue traces and rune ring on black, while isolated pCylinderShape3 produces the bright white/violet tube and isolated pCubeShape meshes produce the panels. Material probes confirm the port term set: DW4_FL_NOTEX turns the layer flat white, DW4_FL_NOBLEND changes the composite, and DW4_FL_FLAT barely changes it; all 16 port meshes carry UVs. These port-side probes identify each mesh contribution but do not prove that the original omitted a mesh: #687 confirms pCylinderShape3 UVs in the original trace stream. The remaining divergence is controller-driven transform/pose or, still to be proven, visibility/draw-set selection.

**Notes:** REFUTED as a mechanism classification. The isolation observations remain valid: the port cylinder is bright, the polySurface subset resembles the original, and the probes establish that the port path is textured/blended. But #704 excludes pose, and the PCSX2 GS-dump runner replays the same ring-f008 stream/state as the correct no-bright-tunnel blue image in both Vulkan and software renderers (#709). Therefore geometry/pose is not the remaining divergence and the broad material term set was not enough to prove material fidelity. The next comparison is exact texture index, CLUT RGBA, TFX modulation and ALPHA blending for the cylinder run versus the port. Folded into #709, the current answer for Authoritative PCSX2 replay of ring-f008 renders no bright tunnel; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c

### #704 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083 — port/src/scene.c read_seq + dw4_scene_node_world; port/src/frontlayer.c fl_draw_item; DW4_FL_SEQ/DEBUG/NODES

Port j_startback trace-phase controller pose is CORRECT at t=0: the pose is NOT the divergence. igTransformSequence1_5 channels decode by descriptor slot (2=trans igVec3fList, 3=rot igQuaternionfList, 4=scale igVec3fList, 11=times igLongList) and a group may carry scale with no translation (group8: ntrans=0, nscale=4). At t=0 every animated ancestor of pCylinderShape3 (node 864 <- group15/863 <- 'New Object'/862 <- beModelCtrlNode2/861 <- igSwitch/860 <- BG02/859) contributes identity: group15's _m placeholder last column is (0,0,0) and its sequence key0 is translation (0,0,0) + scale none + rot identity, so pCylinderShape3's accumulated world = identity. Where a placeholder exists it equals the t=0 key: node 875 _m last col (-1,0,0) = its trans key0; nodes 899/902 _m (-0.8,0,0) = their key0; group9/895 _m (scale (0,1,0), last col (0,0,18.75)) = its trans key0 (0,0,18.75) + scale key0 (0,1,0). The draw path applies the full world matrix (fl_draw_item -> dw4_scene_node_world). The original ring-f008/f022 stream draw 1 (TRISTRIP, 1223 verts) rasterises to a full-screen annulus whose screen silhouette matches the port's isolated pCylinderShape3 at IoU 0.90 with an identical bbox and the identical UV v-band 0.3906-0.8594. So the original's submitted tunnel geometry agrees with the port's pose: no wrong scale, translation, parent composition or phase time.

**Notes:** Measured with DW4_FL_SEQ=1 (channel lists incl. ord=3 igQuaternionfList and node ntrans/nscale/nrot), DW4_FL_DEBUG=1 DW4_FL_NODES=1 (the 23-joint node table with _transformInput, _m and each ancestor's chain). The port's camera is static by design (frontlayer.c: 'The demo path is static'), and the stream agrees with it (silhouette IoU). Consequence: no controller pose change is implementable from this evidence.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c; port/src/scene.c

<a id="f705"></a>
### #705 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00b083 — AREA.AFS entry 620 j_startback; GSDumpSingleFrame freeze VRAM; gsfreeze/gsrasttex/perdraw

The ring-f008/ring-f022 recorded GS stream does NOT correspond to its own image for the tunnel draw: the stream carries the tunnel geometry, but the recorded sidecar, the dump's embedded screenshot AND the framebuffer decoded from the dump's own freeze-blob VRAM all contain no tunnel. Two positive controls prove the VRAM decoder and the raster are sound: (a) the same decode on postflash-title shows the tunnel clearly (central mean 76.4 vs 5.3 for ring-f022), and (b) ring-f040's stream raster matches its own sidecar (182 vs 205) while ring-f008's does not (96.9 vs 17.2). In the original trace-phase image the annulus band is pure blue (mean (11.9,70.0,164.0), R/B 0.07, ZERO grey pixels) and the decoded-VRAM radial profile (25.6,16.3,8.8,11.4,8.2,7.7,8.7,8.9,9.0,28.0,30.1,26.2,25.8,6.4,5.1) reproduces the reference (27.6,16.9,9.0,11.4,8.0,7.3,8.9,9.5,8.0,30.2,29.4,21.5,30.9,5.0,5.3). Ring frames f040/f052/f068/f084 DO show the tunnel (central mean 190-255, 1600 grey px), so the trace phase f008/f022 is the no-tunnel phase and by f040 the tunnel rushes in. All four recorded geometry frames in ring-f008 are identical, so the disagreement is between the dump's stream and its own state, not between frames of the dump.

**Notes:** REFUTED by authoritative replay. PCSX2 build c616714 opened the ring-f008 GS dump directly in its GS-dump runner; at dump frame 4 both Vulkan and F9 software renderer screenshots show the blue rune ring/circuit traces on black with no bright tunnel, matching the sidecar, embedded screenshot and decoded freeze-state framebuffer (#709). The custom per-run raster that predicted a bright annulus is therefore incomplete or wrong; its disagreement does not establish a stream/image mismatch. The VRAM measurements remain useful image evidence, but the capture provenance conclusion must not be reused. Folded into #709, the current answer for Authoritative PCSX2 replay of ring-f008 renders no bright tunnel; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** PCSX2 GSDumpReplayer build c616714

<a id="f706"></a>
### #706 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083 — port/src/scene.c read_seq; AREA.AFS entry 620 j_startback graph; frontlayer.c probes

Recorded GS state: a texture texel with alpha 128 and vertex alpha 128 produces fragment alpha 128 under TFX=MODULATE and passes pass-0 ATST=GREATER, AREF=76. The actual texture has a per-texel alpha distribution, so this single value does not establish whether alpha testing removes all or only some tunnel fragments; the isolated per-texel raster changes slightly but remains an incomplete model of the PCSX2 frame.

**Notes:** The GS fixed-point formula is min(0xff,(texture*vertex)>>7). A separate per-texel probe uses the PCSX2-dumped 128x64 alpha plane: AREF changes run 0 slightly (mean 131.04 vs 130.99), unlike the earlier constant-alpha-128 comparison, which was byte-identical for all five isolated runs. Thus the constant-alpha probe must not be generalized to all texels. The per-draw raster still renders a bright annulus and is not a faithful full-frame model; the alpha test is not yet established as the hide mechanism. Folded into #708, the current answer for Trace-phase tunnel hidden by GS alpha test (refuted); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** PCSX2 pcsx2/GS/GSState.cpp TFX_MODULATE

<a id="f707"></a>
### #707 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00b083 — AREA.AFS 620 j_startback; GSDumpSingleFrame freeze VRAM + CLUT; port/src/render.c ui_tri; port/src/frontlayer.c

MECHANISM FOUND: the trace-phase tunnel is hidden by the GS ALPHA TEST in pass 0, and #668 was refuted on wrong grounds. The tunnel sits in pass 0 (PRMODE=0x78, ALPHA=0x44, TEST=0x504cd: ATE=1, ATST=GREATER, AREF=0x4c=76 -> 0.298, AFAIL=0 KEEP, ZTST=GEQUAL); the visible trails sit in pass 1 (PRMODE=0x58, ALPHA=0x48 additive, TEST=0x500cd AREF=12 -> 0.047). The GS fragment alpha is texA*vcolA: the dump's CLUT (decoded from its own VRAM at CBP=0x38a0) gives texA=128 for EVERY texel of the tunnel's UV band (x8-28/y25-55, n=533, min=max=mean=128), the submitted vertex alpha is 128 (0x80), so fragA = 128*128/255 = 64.25/255 = 0.252 <= 0.298 and every tunnel fragment is DISCARDED; in pass 1 the same 0.252 > 0.047 so the trails are KEPT. The port draws the tunnel because three port faults conspire: (a) its CLUT alpha is exactly 2x the game's - a pairwise RGB match of the pack texture against the VRAM CLUT gives pack/vram = 255/128 x4619, 36/18, 73/37, 109/55, 146/73, 182/91, 219/110 (ratio 1.97-2.00); (b) its fragment alpha saturates - render.c computes ca = texA*(cav/16384), clamped to 1, instead of texA*vcolA; (c) no alpha test is applied by default (dw4_render_alpha_ref = -1), which is why the existing DW4_FL_ALPHAREF probe is inert and why #668 saw ref 0.0/0.3/0.6 give byte-identical frames.

**Notes:** REFUTED: the calculation 128*128/255=64 is not the GS TFX_MODULATE calculation. PCSX2 implements the GS rule as (texture*vertex)>>7 with 8-bit clamping because 0x80 represents 1.0. The recorded texture alpha 128 and vertex alpha 128 therefore produce fragment alpha 128, not 64; 128 passes pass-0 GREATER AREF 76. After correcting perdraw.py, every AREF-76 run PNG is byte-identical to its alpha-test-disabled counterpart (#708). The three alleged port faults do not establish this hide mechanism: the observed pack/VRAM alpha difference and the port alpha-domain mismatch may merit a separate audit, but neither can explain the recorded image while the recorded stream's own alpha test passes. Folded into #708, the current answer for Trace-phase tunnel hidden by GS alpha test (refuted); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** Freeze VRAM CLUT and per-vertex alpha of the ring-f008 GS dump; perdraw raster; port/src/render.c; findings #704/#706

<a id="f708"></a>
### #708 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00b083 — GS trace-phase texture modulation and alpha test

The exact GS TFX_MODULATE alpha result for a texel with texture alpha 128 and vertex alpha 128 is 128: min(0xff,(128*128)>>7)=128. Because pass-0 TEST uses ATST=GREATER and AREF=76, that texel passes. This arithmetic is confirmed; it does not determine the test outcome for other texture texels or establish the full tunnel visibility mechanism.

**Notes:** PCSX2 GSState.cpp implements TFX_MODULATE with the 0x80 fixed-point scale and 0xff clamp. gsregs.py centralises that rule and test_gsregs.py pins 0x80*0x80 -> 0x80. The original per-draw AREF/no-test images used constant texture alpha 128 and were byte-identical. A follow-up supplied the actual PCSX2-dumped alpha plane and found a small difference in run 0, so the all-texel conclusion is withdrawn. The custom isolated raster remains incomplete; use PCSX2 replay for final-frame conclusions. Topic head for Trace-phase tunnel hidden by GS alpha test (refuted): also read #668, #676, #692, #706, #707 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** https://github.com/PCSX2/pcsx2/blob/master/pcsx2/GS/GSState.cpp

### #710 · confirmed

**Where:** GS PACKED XYZF2/3 — GS register decoding; opening tunnel investigation

PACKED XYZF2/3 depth is high-qword bits 4..27 and fog is bits 36..43. XYZ2/3 instead has a full 32-bit depth in bits 0..31. The prior canonical helper and its fixture both missed the XYZF nibble shift.

**Notes:** Verified against preserved PCSX2 GSRegs.h GIFPackedXYZF2 and GSState.cpp GIFPackedRegHandlerXYZF2 (srl32<4>), then corrected helpers, register-aware callers, and tests. Ring-f008 cylinder-attributed run 0 now has Z=504..578 and F in {162,169,170,171}. X/Y and UV/pose evidence is unaffected. Numerical old Z/fog results must be recomputed. TEXFLUSH corrected to 0x3f and alpha-test names repaired. 21 tests pass.

**Evidence:** PCSX2 GSRegs.h GIFPackedXYZF2 and GSState.cpp GIFPackedRegHandlerXYZF2

<a id="f712"></a>
### #712 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — Per-mesh blend state of the opening layer

The pack carries the blend mode per mesh, as an igBlendFunctionAttr (_src ordinal 4, _dst ordinal 5, _eq ordinal 6) in the mesh's own or an ancestor igAttrSet's attribute list, inherited by nearest ancestor. In the 16-mesh j_startback layer, 10 meshes (nodes 867-891: pCubeShape8/4/7, pCylinderShape4/2, pCubeShape2, polySurfaceShape98/97/109) inherit _src=4 _dst=5 (ordinary alpha, GS ALPHA 0x44) and the last 6 (nodes 894-907: polySurfaceShape101, pCubeShape3, pCylinderShape8/7/6, polySurfaceShape104) inherit _src=4 _dst=1 (additive, ALPHA 0x48). pCylinderShape3, the tunnel, has no blend attribute and draws with the default ordinary alpha. The meshes are in that draw order, so the ordinary group covers the tunnel before the additive group is added.

**Notes:** Port probe dw4_scene_node_blend over the loaded scene (DW4_FL_DEBUG prints per item). Attribute-class census of the pack via DW4_SCENE_ATTRS: igBlendFunctionAttr x133, igBlendStateAttr x50. Agrees with the ordered replay (#711) per-part ALPHA and corrects the port's blanket additive assumption and the front-end-boot.md table's A/B 'passes' wording: they are per-mesh states, not two passes. The 'no attribute -> ordinary' default for the cylinder is taken from the capture (runs 0-2 are the cylinder under ALPHA 0x44), not from engine code.

**Evidence:** port/src/scene.c (dw4_scene_node_blend), port/src/frontlayer.c fl_node_blend

<a id="f713"></a>
### #713 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — Opening-layer covering batch attribution

Ordered-replay parts of ring-f008 FRAME-section 8 attribute to pack meshes by per-vertex UV-multiset coverage: runs 0-2 part 0 (ALPHA 0x44) are pCylinderShape3 (1217/1223, 357/421, 698/698); run 2 part 1, the 1393-vertex covering batch (ALPHA 0x44, PRMODE 0x58), is the ordinary-alpha meshes including pCubeShape4 196/196, pCubeShape7 181/181, pCubeShape8 4/4; runs 3-4 (ALPHA 0x48) are the additive meshes polySurfaceShape101 (6/6 and 188/448 with scrolled UVs), pCubeShape3 101/101 and pCylinderShape6. So the covering is the layer's own ordinary-blend meshes drawn over the tunnel, and the mesh order matches the port's item order.

### #714 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — Scoring the ordered diagnostic

The ordered diagnostic reproduces PCSX2's replay of ring-f008 closely: against the software-renderer replay screenshot (resampled to 640x448, 1.5 px blur) mean absolute RGB error is 1.21 with 0.21% of pixels off by more than 48 levels, versus 167.29 for the all-additive control and 11.85 for an all-black frame. For ring-f022 the earlier 'bright ring residual' was a wrong frame choice: the dump holds four consecutive geometry sections whose additive glow differs, and section 2 matches the F8 sidecar at MAE 2.21 (0.26%), while sections 5, 8 and 11 score 14.5-18.3. ring-f008's four sections are identical, which hid this.

<a id="f715"></a>
### #715 · unconfirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — Remaining port divergence after per-mesh blend

With the pack's per-mesh blend applied (fl_node_blend), the port's early frame moves toward PCSX2: MAE against the software replay falls from 131.89 (all-additive) to 44.94, and the purple wash and tunnel interior are gone. The ten ordinary meshes alone bring the mean from 79.5 (cylinder only) to 20.2 (diagnostic: 11.9). A bright white ring remains once the additive meshes are drawn (mean 20.4 -> 52.6; diagnostic 12.9), from pCylinderShape7 (+16) and polySurfaceShape104 (+16). In the stream, pCylinderShape7 is drawn with a scrolled u offset (stream u -0.211 versus pack u 0.633..0.742, a shift of -0.844, moving to -0.24 and -0.37 in later ring-f022 sections) and polySurfaceShape104 is absent from run 4 (its v 0.109-0.203 is not among the run's 9 distinct UVs). The port draws the first with static pack UVs and draws the second at all.

**Notes:** Hypothesis, not a mechanism: the scroll is applied at runtime (the pack has no texture-matrix record; only igTextureMatrixStateAttr enable flags) and polySurfaceShape104 is hidden or collapsed at t=0 by something the port does not evaluate. Also unresolved: #666's global u-mirror, since the stream's pCubeShape3 and polySurfaceShape101 UVs equal the pack's unmirrored OBJ values. Depth (GEQUAL, ~7% of the covering batch rejected) and alpha tests (AREF 12/76) are not modelled by the port's UI triangle path. update: the pCylinderShape7 u scroll is explained by an igTransform _target=9 sequence (#716). The polySurfaceShape104 omission remains open. result: with #716 applied the white ring is gone and pCylinderShape7/polySurfaceShape104 no longer add brightness (items 0..13 vs 0..14 score identically). Remaining: flat vertex colour (#717) and a faint centre structure from the other additive meshes. resolved (#721): the faint centre rings are polySurfaceShape101's thin quads, removed by the stream's depth test (and the far clip drops 64 of its quads). Folded into #721, the current answer for Port early-frame MAE against PCSX2 replay; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c (DW4_FL_UPTO probe)

<a id="f716"></a>
### #716 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — UV scroll of the opening layer

The opening layer's circuit-trace UV scroll is an igTransform with _target=9, not a model transform. Exactly 8 igTransform records in AREA.AFS entry 620 have _target=9 (the other 291 have 0), and they are exactly the 8 unnamed igTransform nodes of j_startback: 875, 878, 881, 884, 887, 890, 899, 902, each the parent of one trace mesh (pCylinderShape4, pCubeShape2, polySurfaceShape98/97/109, pCylinderShape2/8/7). Node 902 (parent of pCylinderShape7) has x-translation keys -0.80 (t=0), -0.84 (t=1.983 s), -1.00 (t=2.0 s), -0.80 (t=2.333 s). The ring-f008 / ring-f022 streams draw pCylinderShape7 with u shifted from the pack by -0.844, -0.873 and -1.003 (f008, f022 section 2, section 5), all inside that -0.84..-1.00 segment. So the stream applies the transform's translation to the texture coordinates, and those captures were taken at about 1.98-2.0 s of the layer's clock, not t=0. The port treats these nodes as model transforms (the -0.8 is invisible as a position) and so draws these meshes with static UVs.

**Notes:** Hypothesis (unconfirmed): that target 9 means the texture matrix is an inference from the identical offsets and the unique _target value; the enum name was not read. The scale/rotation channels of these nodes and whether the offset applies to V are unchecked. Also implies the earlier 't=0' pose comparisons (#704) were against captures near t=2 s; the early part of the layer is static until about 2.0 s so the pose conclusion likely stands. Explains the pCylinderShape7 half of #715; polySurfaceShape104 is still unexplained. port test: applying the _target=9 translation as a UV offset (before the u mirror) takes the port's early frame from MAE 44.94 to 7.54 against PCSX2's software replay (px>48: 18.0% -> 0.64%) and removes the white ring; that behavioural result confirms the mechanism. Only the 'target 9 = texture matrix' naming remains inferred.

**Evidence:** _target census and node graph of AREA.AFS entry 620, port DW4_FL_SEQ translation keys for nodes 899/902, ring-f008 s8 draw attribution, ring-f008/f022 run-4 UVs

<a id="f717"></a>
### #717 · unconfirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — Port frame after blend and UV offset; vertex colour

After #712 and #716 the port's early frame (frame 0, 640x448) scores MAE 7.54 against PCSX2's software replay of ring-f008 (0.64% of pixels off by more than 48), better than an all-black frame (11.85); the white ring and dark-grey background are gone. The traces and rune ring are still dimmer than PCSX2's because the port shades every mesh with one flat colour (72,72,77,127) while the stream's per-vertex colour is mostly (127,127,127,128) (about 1.0 in the GS 0x80 domain), with some meshes at (63,63,70,128) and (90,90,94,128). Overriding the colour to (127,127,127,128) via DW4_FL_COLOR gives MAE 6.37 and matching trace brightness, but 1.19% of pixels off, with a faint cyan centre structure the stream does not draw.

Run-4 colour counts in ring-f008 section 8: 645 x (127,127,127,128), 92 x (63,63,70,128), 12 x (90,90,94,128), 12 x (126,126,126,128). The centre structure is not yet attributed to a mesh. Also fixed: DW4_FL_NOMIRROR set u to the constant 1.0 instead of the straight UV, so earlier mirror probes using it were invalid; the mirror question (#666) remains untested with a correct probe. follow-up (#719): the vertex colours are per-vertex lit (ambient 0.5,0.5,0.55 plus one directional light), not data in the pack; the port now applies the fit. Folded into #721, the current answer for Port early-frame MAE against PCSX2 replay; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

### #718 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — PCSX2 replay of ring-f022 frames

PCSX2's own replay confirms the ordered diagnostic on all four ring-f022 frames. Scoring the software-renderer replay frames (PCSX2 v2.8.2 GS-dump runner, 824x618 emulated frame) against the diagnostic's four geometry sections (same scoring as #714): runner frame 1 matches section 5 (MAE 1.29), frame 2 section 8 (0.93), frame 3 section 11 (0.83), frame 4 section 2 (1.18); every other pairing scores 3.7-17.2. The F8 sidecar matches runner frame 4 (1.17), i.e. section 2, consistent with #714. So the runner's frame order is dump sections 5, 8, 11, 2; the capture agent's own OSD labelling of the same images is a one-frame offset it attributed to screenshot lag. Vulkan and software replays of ring-f022 agree (mean abs diff 0.09-0.19 of 255).

**Notes:** Corrects #714: ring-f008's four geometry sections have identical vertex positions, UVs, colours and draw state (only FRAME_1/2 and FINISH/LABEL differ), but PCSX2's replay of them differs by a few short radial trace segments (mean abs diff 0.12-0.25, 0.33-0.54% of pixels). That difference is outside the diagnostic (it uses one texture dump); likely texture or palette uploads between frames, not checked. Runner frame 1 of ring-f008 is pixel-identical to the software-renderer replay of dump frame 4 (#705). Keyboard hotkeys did not reach PCSX2 on the Wayland path, so frames were identified by content grouping and OSD readout. <a id="f719"></a>
### #719 · unconfirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — Per-vertex lighting of the opening layer

The opening layer's per-vertex colours are lit, not flat: for the layer meshes (which have no colour stream) the stream colour is min(1, ambient + diffuse * max(0, N.L)) in the GS 0x80 domain (x127.5, floored, alpha 128). Matching 4,494 of 4,526 ring-f008 section-8 stream vertices to the port's projected pack vertices (screen position within 2 px and the pack v) and fitting: ambient (0.5, 0.5, 0.55) (stream minimum (63,63,70)), one directional light toward L = (0.297, 0.414, 0.861) in world space, diffuse about 1.5 x (1, 1, 0.875); RMS error 17 levels, 94% of vertices within 8 levels (6% off by more than 8, partly mismatches). The in-between colours show a constant blue/red light-term ratio of about 0.875. Model- and view-space normals fit identically because the camera is nearly axis-aligned, so the light's space is not settled. Applied in the port as the layer's default shade: MAE against PCSX2's replay 7.54 -> 6.26 (flat (127,127,127) override: 6.37).

**Notes:** Hypothesis-grade fit, not recovered engine data: the diffuse scale is constrained only by about 100 vertices with intermediate colours, the light's z component by normals with nonzero z, and the pack contains no igLightAttr for this layer (entry 620 has none). The ambient (0.5,0.5,0.55) matches #563's rig ambient. Remaining differences against PCSX2: thin edge residue along the traces (line thickness and resampling), and faint centre rings that depend on the layer's clock (the stream's pCylinderShape7 scroll of -0.844 corresponds to t of about 1.984 s from node 902's keys, whereas the port frame is t=0). Matching used port frame 0 geometry, which equals the capture's for the static cylinder but not for animated meshes.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c (fl_lit_color, DW4_FL_DUMPV, DW4_FL_FLATCOLOR)

<a id="f720"></a>
### #720 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — Clock, mirror, wrap of the opening layer

The opening captures are at layer clock 1.78-2.03 s, and the layer is sampled unmirrored with REPEAT wrap; #666's u-mirror was compensating for rendering at t=0 with clamped texture lookups. (1) Per-mesh stream u minus pack u is exactly 0.000 for the static meshes (pCylinderShape3, pCubeShape3/4/7, polySurfaceShape101) and -0.446 (pCylinderShape2, polySurfaceShape97/98, pCubeShape2), -0.836 (pCylinderShape7/8), +0.013 (polySurfaceShape109) for the _target=9 meshes, so the GS does not mirror. (2) Those offsets are the transforms' keys at one time: nodes 878-887 go 0 -> -0.5 over 0-2.0 s (-0.446 at 1.784 s), nodes 899/902 go -0.8 -> -0.84 by 1.983 s (-0.836 at 1.785 s), node 890 goes 0 -> +0.015 by 2.0 s (+0.0134 at 1.785 s): ring-f008 is at t of about 1.785 s. (3) CLAMP is WMS = WMT = 0 (REPEAT), TEX1 is linear filtered. (4) The port's texel lookup clamped and truncated toward zero: pCylinderShape4's constant u = -1/128 flipped per pixel between texel 127 (black) and 0 (grey 24), drawing horizontal bars. With straight UV, REPEAT, floor lookup and the clock pinned: ring-f008 frame vs PCSX2 MAE 4.60 (0.08% of pixels off by more than 48). ring-f022's four PCSX2 frames are matched by port frames 120, 121, 122 and 119 (MAE 5.25, 5.69, 5.70, 5.55; other times 8-22), i.e. consecutive 60 Hz frames at 1.98-2.03 s; ring-f008 is best at 106-119 (4.5-5.9).

**Notes:** Measured by DW4_FL_T=\<frame> (pins the layer clock), DW4_FL_MIRROR / DW4_FL_CLAMP (old behaviour for A/B) and the port's per-frame render. Frame 0 (t=0) has no capture to compare, so the early (0-1.78 s) look is by construction, not verified. Remaining against PCSX2: edge residue along traces, a faint fade at the ring's top left, and faint centre rings (the pCylinderShape7/8 glow ring), not yet attributed. Linear filtering (TEX1) is not modelled by the port's nearest sampler. Supersedes #666's mirror and the mirror-based statements in #703/#715. Topic head for j_startback texture sampling (u-mirror vs unmirrored REPEAT): also read #666 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c, port/src/render.c (dw4_render_tex_wrap, floorf)

<a id="f721"></a>
### #721 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — Far clip, depth, alpha function, bilinear and registration of the opening layer

Five more facts take the port's early frame to MAE 2.2-2.9 against PCSX2's replay (ring-f008 2.22; ring-f022's four frames 2.57, 2.69, 2.75, 2.89; the ordered diagnostic's floor is 0.8-1.3; 0.0-0.01% of pixels off by more than 48 levels). (1) Far clip: the stream omits every primitive with a vertex beyond view depth 2048 (drawn vertices reach 2040; every omitted polySurfaceShape101 quad has a vertex at >= 2049; pCylinderShape3's shallowest absent vertex is at 2240) - 64 of polySurfaceShape101's 112 quads are never drawn. (2) The GS Z is an integer, z = 6.57e5 / depth - 329 (clamped at 0; 38 matched pairs, rms 7, larger = nearer), tested GEQUAL (ZMSK = 0) against a buffer cleared to 0; this hides the thin polySurfaceShape101 quads the stream does draw (the faint centre rings: 638 stray bright pixels -> 17). (3) The alpha test is the pack's: igAlphaFunctionAttr _func = 4 (GREATER), _refValue 0.6 on node 862 'New Object' (an ancestor of pCylinderShape3) and 0.1 elsewhere (default); AREF = floor(ref * 128) = 76 / 12 as in the stream's TEST (0x504cd / 0x500cd); applied as (At*Av)>>7 > AREF. (4) The GS draws bilinear (TEX1 MMAG/MMIN linear): 4.60 -> 4.41. (5) Registration: stream y is 0.873 px below the port's projection (median over 4,494 matched vertices, 80% within 0.81-0.96; dx -0.03), worth 4.25 -> 2.22. The abutting-quad double coverage hypothesis (top-left rule) was tested and changed nothing measurable (kept as a layer-only sampling offset).

**Notes:** Implemented in the port (DW4_FL_FAR, DW4_FL_NODEPTH, DW4_FL_NOATEST, DW4_FL_NEAREST, DW4_FL_NOCYOFF probes restore each). The Z fit uses few distinct depths, so the constants are approximate; the stream's Z is small (0-611) with float interpolation unmodelled. The 0.873 px offset is empirical (not derived from XYOFFSET/projection constants). Default alpha ref 0.1 is inferred from the stream, not read from a pack record in this scene. Other layers (later tunnel phases, UI) are unchecked. Registration also affected every earlier MAE in #712-#720: they carried a ~0.9 px vertical misregistration, so their absolute values were inflated but their orderings were not. Topic head for Port early-frame MAE against PCSX2 replay: also read #715, #717 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c, port/src/render.c, port/src/scene.c

<a id="f722"></a>
### #722 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startback / AREA.AFS 620 — GS fog of the opening/title tunnel

The tunnel's fog is the GS fog stage with a linear coefficient of view depth, F = floor(255 * (2000 - depth) / 1900) clamped to 0..255 (near 100, far 2000), FOGCOL white (0xffffff), colour C = Cs*F/255 + FOGCOL*(1 - F/255) applied after texture modulation and before blending, enabled by PRMODE.FGE. Exact fit: zero error over all 160 distinct (view depth, stream F) pairs of ring-f008's pCylinderShape3 vertices (the F spans 171 at depth 725 to 0 at about 2000). The pack gates it: igFogStateAttr _enabled = 1 on node 862 'New Object' (the cylinder's own set, also the one with the 0.6 alpha function) and 0 on every other layer set (BG02, PromotedAttr, WN01), so nearest-ancestor lookup reproduces the stream's FGE split (PRMODE 0x78 on runs 0-2 part 0, 0x58 elsewhere). The parameters are not in this pack (no igFogAttr in the layer scene). Implemented in the port (DW4_FL_NOFOG disables): the early ring frames are unchanged (the cylinder is covered, MAE 2.22-2.89), and the visible-tunnel title improves from MAE 22.6 to 11.3 against the real post-flash title screenshot (best port frame 340 of the loop), with the white central glow now present.

**Notes:** The fit uses a single scene (ring-f008, cylinder only); other fog-enabled sets in other scenes may use other near/far values (#604 found 60/300 for the world stage, also linear). Truncation (floor) matched exactly; rounding did not. The real title's clock was found by search (frame 340 here), not read from the dump, and the later tunnel phases (ring-f040..f084) are unchecked.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontlayer.c, render.c, scene.c (dw4_scene_node_fog)

<a id="f723"></a>
### #723 · confirmed

**Where:** j_startmenu / AREA.AFS 620 — Title UI draw state

The title's UI draws (logo, Press START button text, the translucent band of five quads, and the copyright quad whose texture holds all three lines) use one GS state, read from the post-flash title dump: ALPHA 0x44 (ordinary alpha), TEST AREF 12 GREATER, TEX1 linear (MMAG/MMIN), CLAMP WMS = WMT = 0 (REPEAT), vertex colour (127,127,127,128) = 1.0 with no tint (not the 0.9/0.95 igColorAttr of the font sets). The port's UI path was wrong in three ways: its texture decode copied the palette's 0..255 alpha where the GS holds 0..128 ((a+1)>>1, as for sback00), so the band's partial alpha and every soft glyph edge clamped to opaque (a solid black band, bold hard-edged text); it sampled nearest; and it applied no alpha test or wrap. Applying the GS alpha conversion, bilinear sampling, REPEAT wrap and AREF 12 to the UI draws makes the band translucent with the tunnel visible through it and the copyright and Press START text thin and soft, matching the real screenshot.

**Notes:** Compared by eye in magnified crops of the real screenshot against the port; no numeric UI-region score (the tunnel behind differs). Quad positions and sizes already matched. The same palette-alpha conversion is needed in any other port path that decodes pack textures without it.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontui.c

<a id="f724"></a>
### #724 · confirmed

**Where:** NDMW.AFS 806 / j_optionwin02 — Option screen graphs and behaviour

The title's Option screen is the pack's own graphs, not hand-built: NDMW.AFS entry 806 (the shared title / character-select pack) holds j_optionwin02 (the dark left band, the legend panel and four pill bars A-D at y = 60 / -10 / -80 / -170 each with an icon), j_optioncursor11 (the highlight outline pieces: group22 long outline, group32 the short outline) and j_optioncursor12 (the arrows: moveroot/left, moveroot/right). The real draw is one 2,051-vertex strip draw in a 32x32 texture (ALPHA 0x44, AREF 12, vertex colour 1.0, near-camera depth, no depth test) followed by the text. The window sequence has two passes: 0-1.9 s is a four-slot layout, 1.917-3.833 s the three-slot layout the US game shows (bar C parked off-screen at x = 360, a band without the third socket, the legend panel sliding in over 0.75 s). The cursor has no explicit row keys for three rows: row 1 = group22 at y 60 (t <= 1.167 s), row 2 = y -10 (t 1.333-2.667 s), the Return row = the short group32 outline resting at (-224, -170) (t 4.333-5.667 s) with group22 parked off to the right; the arrows are at y 60 / -10 and parked off-screen (x = 300, t 2.333-2.583 s) for the Return row, as in the capture (09). Driving each group's clock this way reproduces the real screen: the same bars, icons, outlines and arrows at the captured positions, sliding between rows over 10 frames. The settings: Left/Right flips the highlighted setting and applies at once; Up/Down moves between three rows (Dungeon camera, Light, Return to Menu); Circle or Cross on Return closes; Start is inert; closing returns the menu on Game Start (not Option); the tunnel keeps animating behind; power-on defaults are Dungeon camera Near, Light Hard (captured, contrary to the manual's 'Soft = normal').

**Notes:** Source: a separate agent's PCSX2 v2.8.2 captures of the option screen. The controller that selects the pass and row times was not recovered; the port picks the clocks from the keys above. Not reproduced: the white flash and logo fade around open/close (the burst shows the title rows fading out, a flash, then the option rows), the arrows' small bob, the cursor pulse. The settings are held in dw4_options but nothing consumes them yet.

**Evidence:** port/src/frontopt.c

<a id="f725"></a>
### #725 · confirmed

**Where:** NDMW.AFS 79 / bectext.igb — Front-end font text layout

The front-end font draws text as one quad per glyph from bectext.igb's 484 metric records (codes are Unicode-like: ASCII letters plus icon glyphs such as 8801 and 8711 (the D-pad and L-stick icons), 8811 (Circle) and 8810 (Cross) on the Option legend), with each glyph's pen advance rounded UP to a whole pixel in the 448-line frame: advance*96 = 15.36, 14.4, 8.64, 12.48 for 'Return' give 16, 15, 9, 13 in the dump's quad spacing (the port used the fractional advance and the text came out narrower). Space is a glyph with a quad of its own, so a line's first quad can be a space (the Option legend's lines start with an icon, then a space, then the text). The draw state is ALPHA 0x44, AREF 12 (TEST 0x300cd, ZTST ALWAYS), TEX1 linear, vertex colour (114,114,114,121) = 0.9/0.95, and the atlas needs the GS alpha conversion (a+1)>>1 like every other pack texture. Positions on the Option screen (first-glyph quad left/top at 640x448): legend icons (379.9, 24.0/53.0/83.0) and (402.9, 24.0); 'Move Cursor' 'M' at 431.9, 'Return to Menu' 'R' and 'Confirm' 'C' at 408.9 (tops 24/54/84); 'Dungeon camera' 'D' (126.5, 149.0), 'Light' 'L' (192.5, 219.0), 'Return to Menu' 'R' (155.5, 378.9); the values are centred on x of about 441 ('Near' starts at 412.9, 'Far' 417.9, 'Hard' 412.9, 'Soft' 414.9). All 82 glyph quads of dump 03 were identified by UV rectangle with zero distance.

**Notes:** The port's fefont now keeps all metric records (dw4_fefont_glyph / dw4_fefont_quad) and applies the alpha conversion; the older dw4_fefont_draw path (placeholder title text) still uses the fractional advance. Glyph-code to icon meaning inferred from the legend text (D-pad/L-stick for Move Cursor, Circle for Return, Cross for Confirm).

**Evidence:** port/src/fefont.c, port/src/frontopt.c

<a id="f735"></a>
### #735 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003f2490 — Gap::Bec::beNDMWLoadCtrl2::vfunc_34

beNDMWLoadCtrl2::vfunc_34 (0x003f2490, 1820 B) is the load-controller message handler (this=beNDMWLoadCtrl2, arg1=the load frame-info record whose +0x28 holds the current load work, arg2=Info whose +0x14 is the data list). It reads the message command via FUN_0038d4a0(messenger, MsgClass=FUN_003c7d40, dataList). cmd 0 (RUN) and 1 (GAMESTART) run the create path: if arg1+0x28==0, create the load work (FUN_0036ae80 'CTRL'); for GAMESTART read GAMERAM GETCOMMON, and if no difficulty (FUN_0038f240<1) set camera=0/light=0 else read DIFFICULTY 0/1 (FUN_0038f320); create the model-controller work (FUN_003c7a40) and ComMdlCtrl (FUN_003c4be0), wire GAMERAM per-slot loop GAMEDATAINIT (0x5182a0) or GETPLAYER (0x5182b0, guarded by ISUSE 0x517e58), then FUN_003f0ed0(work+0x34, messenger, 0) starts the load interface. cmd 3 additionally creates the COMMONINTF work (FUN_0038f660/FUN_0038f670). cmd 2 returns 1 without action. Handoff: vfunc_31 then runs the LOADINTF2 sub-controller (work+0x34) via FUN_003f1270/_12f0/_1380/_13d0, which is the beNDMWLoadIntf2 slot/character/name/difficulty family.

beNDMWLoadIntf2::vfunc_31 (0x3e4750) case-1 preamble: the recovered C if (P32(v7+0x24)==2) branch reset CTRL via SET_ST(0), but retail 0x3e4e90-0x3e4eac writes the per-player record (v0 from FUN_003e9240(COMMONINTF+0x28, frame+8)): sw zero at +0x24 (0x3e4e9c), +0x30 (0x3e4ea0), +0x2c (0x3e4ea4) and the delay slot +0x28 (0x3e4eac). CTRL keeps st0=2, so the case-0 dispatch (0x3e4f20, jump table 0x517580) re-enters state 2 and emits 0x3e3e00(JLINEWIN01), not the state-0 0x3e3e00(JCHRSELBASE). The reset target is the COMMONINTF+0x28 list element (FUN_003e9240 = lw v0,16(a0); sll v1,a1,2; lw v0,0(v0+a1*4)).

<a id="f743"></a>
### #743 · confirmed

**Where:** scripts/verify_loadintf2.py — scripts/verify_loadintf2.py / scripts/verify_loadctrl.py

The restricted R5900 interpreter implemented lb (opcode 0x20) as sx(v,32), so a loaded byte 0xff read as 255 instead of -1. Corrected to sign-extend the byte (8 bits) for lb; lbu/lhu stay zero-extended and lw stays a 32-bit sign extension. The same duplicated loader in scripts/verify_loadctrl.py is fixed the same way. A focused interpreter_selftest now pins lb(0xff)->-1, lb(0x80)->-128, lbu(0xff)->255, lw(0xffffffff)->-1, lhu(0x8000)->0x8000 and runs before every verification.

**Evidence:** scripts/verify_loadintf2.py:interpreter_selftest; scripts/verify_loadctrl.py:interpreter_selftest

### #749 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003ebe00 — beNDMWLoadIntf2 text-order publishing

The text-order publishing trio 0x003ebce0/0x003ebe00/0x003ec050 is recovered in recovered/frontend/textorder.{c,h}. Boundary/contract from retail: owner is opaque with +0x10 the messenger and +0x14 the lua node; a control id is (mode<<16)|token and is matched against the low 20 bits of the words in the id table at 0x004cda40 (stride 0xc: {u32 id; u32 text-ptr; u32 line-list}). 0x003ebe00 walks the list publishing each {name; font-set; font-index} triple via 0x003ebce0, latches 0x0058a220/0x0058a1e0 from the entrys 2nd word, fires 0x00389ff0 with token 0x517a28 when id&0xf000 is 0x1000/0x2000/0x3000 or 0x517a18 when 0, and when flag==1 and the entry carries bit 20 latches the NEXT entrys 2nd word into 0x58a220 and repeats the animation dispatch (it does NOT publish the next entrys line list; corrected by #755). 0x003ec050 is the close variant: list only, no globals or animation. 0x003ebce0 is the leaf (0x38d860/0x38f830/0x38f830/0x38f830/0x38e060).

**Evidence:** recovered/frontend/textorder.c
